Tropical intersection homology
Ryota Mikami

Abstract
. Numerical equivalence of algebraic cycles is defined abstractly by intersection numbers. Classically, for smooth complex proper toric varieties, the quotients by numerical equivalence with rational coefficients can be described geometrically as singular cohomology. They are also expressed in terms of tropical geometry, tropical cohomology, introduced by Itenberg-Katzarkov-Mikhalkin-Zharkov. This paper aims to generalize this to suitable pairs of smooth proper varieties and divisors by introducing a tropical analog of Goresky-MacPherson’s intersection homology.



1.
Introduction

Numerical equivalence

\sim_{\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits}
of algebraic cycles
Z^{p}(Y)

of smooth proper varieties
Y
over a field
K
is defined by intersection
numbers:
\alpha\sim_{\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits}\beta
(
\alpha,\beta\in Z^{p}(Y))
if
and only if
\deg(\alpha\cdot\gamma)=\deg(\beta\cdot\gamma)\in\mathbb{Z}

(
\gamma\in Z^{\dim Y-p}(Y)
). The quotient
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits}^{p}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}

(tensored with
\mathbb{Q}
) plays a central role in the theory of Grothendieck’s
pure motives (see e.g.,
[
MNP13
]
).
The abstract definition of numerical equivalence is one of the reasons
why some fundamental problems still remain.

Classically, for a smooth complex proper toric variety
T_{\Sigma}
,
we have a geometric description

(1.1)

\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits}^{p}(T_{\Sigma})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\cong H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{2p}(T_{\Sigma}(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q})

using singular cohomology. In the toric case, the quotient
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits}^{p}(T_{\Sigma})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}

is also isomorphic to a cohomology theory in
tropical geometry
,

tropical cohomology

H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,p}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma});\mathbb{Q})
,
introduced by Itenberg-Katzarkov-Mikhalkin-Zharkov (
[
IKMZ19
]
).
The aim of this paper is to give a generalization of this description
to suitable pairs of smooth proper varieties and divisors by introducing
a tropical analog of intersection homology,
tropical intersection
homology
.

We consider the following situation. Let
\varphi\colon Y\hookrightarrow T_{\Lambda}

be a closed immersion to the smooth toric variety
T_{\Lambda}
corresponding
to a unimodular fan
\Lambda
such that
\varphi(Y)
is tropically
compact (
[
Tev07
, Definition 1.1]
),
i.e., for any cone
\lambda\in\Lambda
, the intersection
\varphi(Y)\cap O(\lambda)

with the orbit
O(\lambda)
is non-empty and of codimension
=\dim\lambda

in
\varphi(Y)
. (The condition of codimension holds for example,
when irreducible components of intersections of
\varphi(Y)
and
toric divisors form simple normal crossing divisors of
\varphi(Y)
.)
Since we would like to give an expression of
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits}^{*}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}

using tropicalization of
\varphi(Y)
, we assume that the natural
map

(1.2)

\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits^{*}(T_{\Lambda})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\to\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits}^{*}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}

is surjective.

Remark 1.1
.

The existence of such a
\varphi\colon Y\hookrightarrow T_{\Lambda}

shows that
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits}^{*}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
is generated by divisor
classes and
[Y]
as a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra. When the base field is infinite
and
Y
is projective, by Bertini’s theorem, the converse is also
true (take an open toric subvariety
T_{\Lambda}\subset\prod_{i}\mathbb{P}^{n_{i}}
).
For a smooth irreducible projective variety
Y_{0}
, this condition
does not necessary hold (
[
HW17
]
),
but when the base field is infinite, there is always a blow-up
Y_{1}\to Y_{0}

satisfying this conditions (Proposition
7.11
,
see also Example
7.4
). In this sense, there are
many such
Y
and
\varphi\colon Y\hookrightarrow T_{\Lambda}
.

Tropical geometry
is, in a sense, a generalization of toric
geometry. The
tropicalization

\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y))
of
\varphi(Y)\subset T_{\Lambda}

with respect to the trivial valuation of the base field
K
is a
compactification of the dual intersection complex of pull-backs
\{\varphi^{-1}(D_{l})\}

of toric divisors
\{D_{l}\}
equipped with the information of intersection
numbers
\deg\varphi^{*}([\overline{O(\lambda)}])
for torus orbits

O(\lambda)\subset T_{\Lambda}
of codimension
\dim Y
(see e.g.,

[
GRW17
, Subsection 3.3 and 3.5]

for details).

A tropical analog of singular cohomology,
tropical cohomology

H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,q}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y));\mathbb{Q})
, was introduced by Itenberg-Katzarkov-Mikhalkin-Zharkov
(
[
IKMZ19
]
) to
study limit mixed Hodge structures of some maximally degenerate varieties
(cf.
[
GS10
]
).
As we have seen, when
Y=T_{\Lambda}
over
\mathbb{C}
, we have

H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,p}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y));\mathbb{Q})\cong H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{2p}(Y(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q})

(
[
IKMZ19
]
) (cf.

[
AAPS23
]
).
In general, Amini-Piquerez (
[
AP24b
, Theorem 1.1]
)
proved

(1.3)

\displaystyle H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,q}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y));\mathbb{Q})\cong

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits^{p}(T_{\Lambda})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}&(p=q)\\
0&(p\leq q-1)\end{cases},

and that a pairing of
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits^{*}(T_{\Lambda})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
given
by
(\alpha,\beta)\mapsto\deg\varphi^{*}(\alpha\cdot\beta)
coincides
with cup products of
H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{*,*}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y));\mathbb{Q})
. (There
is also a similar result (
[
Mik20
, Theorem 1.1]
)
for tropical cohomology
H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,q}(Y;\mathbb{Q})
of
Y
itself.)
By assumption (
1.2
), we have a natural
surjective morphism

H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,p}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y));\mathbb{Q})\twoheadrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits}^{p}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}.

However, this is not necessary isomorphism, in other words,
Poincaré
duality
for
H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{*,*}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y));\mathbb{Q})
does not necessary
hold. (In
1
-dimensional case, recently, Gubler-Jell-Rabinoff (
[
GJR21b
]
,

[
GJR21a
]
)
introduced new tropical cohomology, and proved Poincaré duality
for it.)

Classically, the failure of Poincaré duality for singular (co)homology
of singular complex proper algebraic varieties is known, and Goresky-MacPherson
(
[
GM80
]
,
[
GM83
]
)
introduced new cohomology theory,
intersection homology
, which
coincides with singular homology for smooth varieties, and proved
Poincaré-Verdier duality for them (of the middle perversity)
in full generality.

In this paper, we will introduce a tropical analog of Goresky-MacPherson’s
intersection homology,
tropical intersection (co)homology

IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,q}(X;\mathbb{Q})

for a tropical variety
X
regular at infinity (
[
MZ14
, Definition 1.2]
).
It coincides with
H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,q}(X;\mathbb{Q})
when Poincaré-Verdier
duality holds (e.g.,
X=\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y))
for
Y=T_{\Lambda}
(see

[
JSS19
]
,

[
GS23
]
,

[
AP24a
]
)).
Our main result is the following.

Theorem 1.2
.

(Corollary
5.27
,
Corollary
7.3
) For
p,q\geq 0
, there is
a non-degenerate pairing

IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,q}(X;\mathbb{Q})\times IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,c}^{\dim X-p,\dim X-q}(X;\mathbb{Q})\to\mathbb{Q}.

Moreover, when
X=\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y))
, under assumption (
1.2
),
we have a natural isomorphism

(1.4)

IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,q}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y));\mathbb{Q})\cong\begin{cases}\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits}^{p}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}&(p=q)\\
0&(p\neq q)\end{cases},

under which the pairing of
IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{*,*}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y));\mathbb{Q})

coincides with intersection numbers in
Y
.

Idea of proof and definitions

Let us discuss idea of proof of isomorphism (
1.4
)
and definitions of tropical intersection homology.

First, we recall a proof of isomorphism (
1.1
)

\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits}^{p}(T_{\Sigma})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\cong H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{2p}(T_{\Sigma}(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q})

(
[
CLS11
, Section 12.3]
,
[
Tot14
, Theorem 4]
).
A filtration

\mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}\nolimits(C_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{r}(T_{\Sigma}(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q})\to C_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{r}(T_{\Sigma}(\mathbb{C})\setminus\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\Sigma_{p}\end{subarray}}\overline{O(\sigma)}(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q}))

of the vector space
C_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{r}(T_{\Sigma}(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q})
of
singular cochains induces a cohomological spectral sequence

\displaystyle E_{1}^{p,q}

\displaystyle=H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits,\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\Sigma_{p}\end{subarray}}\overline{O(\sigma)}(\mathbb{C})\setminus\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\Sigma_{p+1}\end{subarray}}\overline{O(\sigma)}(\mathbb{C})}^{p+q}(T_{\Sigma}(\mathbb{C})\setminus\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\Sigma_{p+1}\end{subarray}}\overline{O(\sigma)}(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q})\Rightarrow H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{p+q}(T_{\Sigma}(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q})

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\Sigma_{p}\end{subarray}}H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{q-p}(O(\sigma)(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q})(-p),

where
O(\sigma)
is the torus orbit corresponding to
\sigma\in\Sigma
,
the subset
\Sigma_{i}\subset\Sigma
consists of cones of dimension

i
, and
(-p)
is the Tate twist. Since
E_{1}^{p,q}
is of pure
weight
2q
, and
H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{p+q}(T_{\Sigma}(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q})
is
of pure weight
p+q
, this degenerates at
E_{2}
-pages, and we
have a surjective morphism

\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\Sigma_{p}\end{subarray}}H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{0}(O(\sigma)(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q})(-p)\cong E_{1}^{p,p}\twoheadrightarrow E_{2}^{p,p}\cong H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{2p}(T_{\Sigma}(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q}).

By an identification
H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{0}(O(\sigma)(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q})(-p)\cong\mathbb{Q}[\overline{O(\sigma)}]
,
this surjective morphism coincides with the restriction of the cycle
class map, which is compatible with intersection numbers and cup products.
Since cup products are non-degenerate, we get isomorphism (
1.1
).

Next, we consider
\varphi\text{$\colon Y\hookrightarrow$}T_{\Lambda}

as in the beginning of this section. We still have a stratification

\varphi(Y)=\bigsqcup_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\varphi(Y)\cap O(\lambda)
,
and hence when
K=\mathbb{C}
, we have a similar spectral sequence. However,
since
H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{q-p}(\varphi(Y)\cap O(\lambda)(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q})
is not
necessary of the pure highest weight
2(q-p)
, the above discussion
does not work. Instead, we use cohomology theories in tropical geometry.

Tropical cohomology
H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,*}
and tropical intersection cohomology

IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,*}
use sheaves of tropical holomorphic
p
-forms:
original one
\mathscr{F}^{p}
(Definition
6.4
) introduced
by Itenberg-Katzarkov-Mikhalkin-Zharkov (
[
IKMZ19
]
)
and a version of Gubler-Jell-Rabinoff’s new one
\mathscr{F}^{p,w}
(
[
GJR21a
]
,

[
GJR21b
]
), respectively
(see the beginning of Subsection
3.1
and Definition

5.1
). For
\lambda\in\Lambda_{p}
, their stalks at the
center
0_{\lambda}
of the fan
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y)\cap O(\lambda))

approximate the highest weight garded quotient. In the following,
for the purpose of explanation, we assume that
K=\mathbb{C}
and irreducible
components of intersections of
\varphi(Y)
and toric divisors form
simple normal crossing divisors of
\varphi(Y)
. Then the highest
weight graded quotient is

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}\nolimits_{2(q-p)}^{W}H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{q-p}(\varphi(Y)\cap O(\lambda)(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\bigg\{(a_{R})_{R}\in\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\subset R\in\Lambda_{q}\end{subarray}}H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{0}(\varphi(Y)\cap\overline{O(R)}(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q})

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subset R\in\Lambda_{q}\end{subarray}}\epsilon_{S,R}a_{R}\cap[\varphi(Y)\cap\overline{O(R)}]=0\in H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{2}(\varphi(Y)\cap\overline{O(S)}(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q})\ (\lambda\subset S\in\Lambda_{q-1})\bigg\}

(
[
PS08
, Proposition 4.10]
),
where
\cap
is the cap product, and the sign
\epsilon_{S,R}\in\{\pm 1\}

is detemined by the fixed orientations of cones
S
and
R
. Since
homological equivalence and numerical equivalence are same for
\mathbb{Q}
-coefficients
divisors (
[
Ful98
, 19.3.1 (ii)]
), the stalk

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{0_{\lambda}}^{q-p,w}:=\mathscr{F}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y)),0_{\lambda}}^{q-p,w}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\bigg\{(a_{R})_{R}\in\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\subset R\in\Lambda_{q}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{Q}

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subset R\in\Lambda_{q}\end{subarray}}\epsilon_{S,R}a_{R}\deg(\varphi^{*}[\overline{O(R)}]\cdot[\overline{O(Q)}])=0\ (\lambda\subset S\in\Lambda_{q-1},\ Q\in\Lambda_{\dim Y-q}\ (Q\cap S=\{(0,\dots,0)\}))\bigg\}

approximates it, and the stalk

\mathscr{F}_{0_{\lambda}}^{q-p}:=\mathscr{F}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y)),0_{\lambda}}^{q-p}:=\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits(\mathscr{F}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda}),0_{\lambda}}^{q-p,w}\to\mathscr{F}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y)),0_{\lambda}}^{q-p,w})

approximates the image of
H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{q-p}(O(\lambda)(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q})
to
it. (In fact, we have

\mathscr{F}_{0_{\lambda}}^{q-p,w}\cong\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}\nolimits_{2(q-p)}^{W}H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{q-p}(\varphi(Y)\cap O(\lambda)(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q})

when each
\varphi(Y)\cap\overline{O(R)}
is connected and
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits}^{\dim Y-q}(\varphi(Y)\cap\overline{O(S)})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}

is generated by
[\varphi(Y)\cap\overline{O(Q)}\cap\overline{O(S)}]

(
Q\in\Lambda_{\dim Y-q},\ Q\cap S=\{(0,\dots,0)\}
), and
\mathscr{F}_{0_{\lambda}}^{q-p}

is isomorphic to them when moreover, the natural morphism

H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{q-p}(O(\lambda)(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q})\to\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}\nolimits_{2(q-p)}^{W}H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{q-p}(\varphi(Y)\cap O(\lambda)(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q})

is surjective. For example, when
Y=T_{\Lambda}
, these three coincide.)

Similarly to the toric case, for
H=H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits},IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}
and
s\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
we have spectral sequences

\displaystyle E_{1}^{p,q}

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\Lambda_{p}\end{subarray}}H^{s-p,q-s}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y)\cap O(\lambda));\mathbb{Q})\Rightarrow H^{s,p+q-s}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y));\mathbb{Q}).

By using retractions of supports of fans to points, we have

\displaystyle H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{s-p,q-s}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y)\cap O(\lambda));\mathbb{Q})

\displaystyle=0\ (q-s\geq 1)

\displaystyle IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{s-p,q-s}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y)\cap O(\lambda));\mathbb{Q})

\displaystyle=0\ (q-s\geq 1\text{ and }p+q\geq 2s)

(Lemma
7.6
). Hence for both
H=H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits},IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}
,
we have

\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\Lambda_{s}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{Q}\cong\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\Lambda_{s}\end{subarray}}H^{0,0}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y)\cap O(\lambda));\mathbb{Q})\cong E_{1}^{s,s}\twoheadrightarrow H^{s,s}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y));\mathbb{Q})

and
H^{p,q}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y));\mathbb{Q})=0
for
p\leq q-1
. Hence by
a natural non-degenerate pairing

IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,q}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y));\mathbb{Q})\times IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{\dim Y-p,\dim Y-q}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y));\mathbb{Q})\to\mathbb{Q},

we have isomorphisms (
1.3
) and (
1.4
).
(Note that
H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{r,0}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y)\cap O(\lambda));\mathbb{Q})\cong\mathscr{F}_{0_{\lambda}}^{r}
.)
(When
Y=T_{\Lambda}
, for
H=H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits},IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}
, the direct
sum of these spectral sequence (with respect to
s\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
)
coincides with the spectral sequence for singular cohomology.)

The point is the non-degeneracy of the natural pairing of
IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}
.
Let us give an observation related to it. Remind that algebraic equivalence
and homological equivalence are same for
\mathbb{Q}
-coefficients divisors
(
[
Ful98
, 19.3.1 (ii)]
). Hence the image of

IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{1,0}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y)\cap O(\lambda));\mathbb{Q})\cong\mathscr{F}_{0_{\lambda}}^{1,w}\to\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\subset R\in\Lambda_{s}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{Q}\cdot\varphi^{*}[\overline{O(R)}]\subset\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits^{s}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}

(
\lambda\in\Lambda_{s-1}
) are close to algebraically equivalent
to zero. Hence the
E_{2}^{s,s}
-terms

\displaystyle E_{2}^{s,s}\cong

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Coker}}\nolimits(\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\Lambda_{s-1}\end{subarray}}IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{1,0}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y)\cap O(\lambda)))\to\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\Lambda_{s}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{Q})

should not equal to
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits}^{p}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
in general.
Therefore, we need
E_{\infty}^{s,s}\not\cong E_{2}^{s,s}
for the
non-degeneracy of the pairing of
IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}
while
E_{\infty}^{s,s}=E_{2}^{s,s}

for
H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}
in the toric case and
H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}
. More precisely,
we need
E_{\infty}^{s,s}
to be “as small as possible”, in otherwords,
we need
E_{1}^{p,q}
(
p+q=2s-1
,
q-s\geq 1
) to be “as large
as possible” . We define
IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}
so that these vanishing and
non-vanishing of
E_{1}^{p,q}
holds. (Other terms
E_{1}^{p,q}

(
p+q\leq 2s-2
,
q-s\geq 1
) are also “as large as possible”.)

Since
E_{1}
-terms are isomorphic to the direct sums of stalks of
cohomology sheaves at the center
0_{\lambda}
of the fan
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y)\cap O(\lambda))
,
these vanishing and non-vanishing are achieved by using
allowability

in geometric definition and
truncation
functors in sheaf-theoretic
definition, which are analogs of ones used in definitions of Goresky-MacPherson’s
intersection homology (
[
GM80
]
,

[
GM83
]
). Actual proof of
the non-degeneracy of the pairing of
IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}
is formal computation
in sheaf theory based on the truncation functors, similarly to
[
GM83
]
.

Note that due to Künneth formula, our allowaility and truncation
functors involve some filtrations, or we should say grades (see Remark

3.15
). For technical reason, we will introduce
notion of locally graded sheaves (Section
4
),
and the first assertion of Theorem
1.2
holds
at the level of Poincaré-Verdier duality in the derived category
of locally graded sheaves (Theorem
5.26
).

Contents of the paper

Contents of the paper are as follows. In Section
2
,
we recall tropical varieties. In Section
3
,
we study geometrically defined tropical intersection (co)chains
IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{p,*}
.
In Section
4
, we discuss locally graded sheaves
and truncation functors which involve grades. In Section
5
,
we introduce
IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{p,*}
using truncation functors,
and prove Poincaré-Verdier duality (Theorem
5.26
).
In Section
6
, we give several comparisons. In
Section
7
, the second assertion of Theorem
1.2

is proved and Example
7.4
is given. In Section

8
(Appendix), for convenience, we discuss six-functors
formalism for derived categories of sheaves of graded modules in the
classical way.

2.
Tropical varieties

Throughout this paper, we fix a free
\mathbb{Z}
-module
M
of finite rank

n
. We put
N:=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(M,\mathbb{Z})
. We also fix a fan
\Sigma
in
N\otimes\mathbb{R}

and a corresponding (normal) toric variety
T_{\Sigma}
over
\mathbb{C}
.
Remind that there is a natural bijection between cones
\sigma\in\Sigma

and torus orbits
O(\sigma)
in
T_{\Sigma}
. The torus orbit
O(\sigma)

is isomorphic to the torus
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[M\cap\sigma^{\perp}].
We put

N_{\sigma}:=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(M\cap\sigma^{\perp},\mathbb{Z})
. For
R=\mathbb{Q}
or
\mathbb{R}
,
we put
N_{\sigma,R}:=N_{\sigma}\otimes R
. We put
T_{\sigma}:=\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tau\in\Sigma\\
\tau\subset\sigma\end{subarray}}O(\tau)
the affine toric variety corresponding to a cone
\sigma\in\Sigma
.
See
[
CLS11
]
for toric geometry.

We shall recall the Kajiwara-Payne partial compactification
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma})
,
a
tropical toric variety
, of
N_{\mathbb{R}}\cong\mathbb{R}^{n}
. See
[
Pay09
]
,

[
Rab12
]
.
We put
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma}):=\bigsqcup_{\sigma\in\Sigma}N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}

as a set. We define a topology on
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma})
as follows.
We extend the canonical topology on
\mathbb{R}
to that on
\mathbb{R}\cup\{\infty\}

so that
(a,\infty]
for
a\in\mathbb{R}
are a basis of neighborhoods of

\infty
. We also extend the addition on
\mathbb{R}
to that on
\mathbb{R}\cup\{\infty\}

by
a+\infty=\infty
for
a\in\mathbb{R}\cup\{\infty\}
. We consider the
set of semigroup homomorphisms
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(M\cap\sigma^{\vee},\mathbb{R}\cup\{\infty\})

as a topological subspace of
(\mathbb{R}\cup\{\infty\})^{M\cap\sigma^{\vee}}
.
We define a topology on
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\sigma}):=\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tau\in\Sigma\\
\tau\subset\sigma\end{subarray}}N_{\tau,\mathbb{R}}
by the canonical bijection

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(M\cap\sigma^{\vee},\mathbb{R}\cup\{\infty\})\cong\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tau\in\Sigma\\
\tau\subset\sigma\end{subarray}}N_{\tau,\mathbb{R}}.

Then we endow
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma})=\bigsqcup_{\sigma\in\Sigma}N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}

with the topology generated by open subsets of
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\sigma})

(
\sigma\in\Sigma
). For each
\sigma\in\Sigma
, we also put
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(O(\sigma)):=N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}
.
Note that when
T_{\Sigma}
is smooth, we have
T_{\sigma}\cong\mathbb{A}^{\dim\sigma}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n-\dim\sigma}
,
hence

\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\sigma})\cong(\mathbb{R}\cup\{\infty\})^{\dim\sigma}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-\dim\sigma}.

Definition 2.1
.

A subset of
\mathbb{R}^{r}
is called a polyhedron if it is an intersection
of finitely many sets of the form

\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{r}\mid\langle x,a\rangle\leq b\}\ (a\in\mathbb{R}^{r},b\in\mathbb{R}),

here
\langle x,a\rangle
is the usual inner product of
\mathbb{R}^{r}
.
Unless otherwise stated, we assume that a polyhedron is rational,
i.e.,
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{r}
.

For a polyhedron
P\subset N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}\cong\mathbb{R}^{n-\dim\sigma}
and

R=\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{Q}
, or
\mathbb{R}
, we put
\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{R}P
its tangent space with
R
-coefficient.
It is well-defined because of rationality of
P
. We consider
\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{R}P\subset N_{\sigma,R}

in the natural way.

Definition 2.2
.

A polyhedron in
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma})
is the closure
\overline{C}

in
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma})
of a polyhedron
C\subset N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}

for some cone
\sigma\in\Sigma
. We put
\dim(\overline{C}):=\dim(C)
,

\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits\overline{C}:=\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits C
, and
\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{R}\overline{C}:=\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{R}C
.

Let
P\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma})
be a polyhedron. We put
\sigma_{P}\in\Sigma

the unique cone such that
P\cap N_{\sigma_{P},\mathbb{R}}\subset P
is dense.
A subset
Q
of
P
is called a face of
P
if it is the closure
of the intersection
P^{a}\cap N_{\tau,\mathbb{R}}
in
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma})

for some
a\in M\cap\sigma_{P}^{\perp}
and some cone
\tau\in\Sigma
,
where
P^{a}
is the closure of

\{x\in P\cap N_{\sigma_{P},\mathbb{R}}\mid x(a)\leq y(a)\text{ for any }y\in P\cap N_{\sigma_{P},\mathbb{R}}\}

in
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma})
. A locally finite collection
\Lambda
of
polyhedra in
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma})
is called a polyhedral complex if
it satisfies the following two conditions.

•

For all
P\in\Lambda
, each face of
P
is also in
\Lambda
.

•

For all
P,Q\in\Lambda
, the intersection
P\cap Q
is a face of

P
and
Q
.

For a locally finite collection
\Lambda
of polyhedra, we put
\Lambda_{i}\subset\Lambda

the subset of polyhedra of dimension
i
. We also put
\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\nolimits\Lambda:=\bigcup_{P\in\Lambda}P\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma})
.
When
\Lambda
is a polyhedral complex, we call
\Lambda
a polyhedral
complex structure of
\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\nolimits\Lambda
.

Definition 2.3
.

A locally closed subset

A\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma})
is said to be
polyhedral
if there
is a polyhedral complex structure
\Lambda
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma})

such that
A
is a union of relative interiors of some polyhedra
in
\Lambda
. The set
\left\{\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits P\cap A\right\}_{P\in\Lambda}

gives an unrestricted topological stratification (Definition
8.19
)
of
A
. We call it a
polyhedral stratification
of
A
.

Definition 2.4
.

Let
A
and
B
be locally
closed polyhedral subsets of tropical toric varieties. A continuous
map
f\colon A\to B
is called a
polyhedrally stratified map

if for any polyhedral stratifications
\Lambda_{A}
of
A
and
\Lambda_{B}

of
B
, there exist polyhedral stratifications
\Lambda_{A,2}
of

A
and
\Lambda_{B,2}
of
B
finer than
\Lambda_{A}
and
\Lambda_{B}

respectively such that
f
is stratified (Definition
8.20
)
with respect to
\Lambda_{A,2}
and
\Lambda_{B,2}
.

Remark 2.5
.

Projections from products to components and embeddings of polyhedral
subsets are polyhedral stratified maps. Only these two types of morphisms
are used in our sheaf-theoretic study.

Definition 2.6
.

In this paper, a
tropcial
variety

X=(\Lambda,w=(w_{P})_{P\in\Lambda_{d}})
of dimension
d

in
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma})
consists of a finite polyhedral complex
\Lambda

of pure dimension
d
and positive integers
w_{P}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}

for
d
-dimensional polyhedra
P\in\Lambda
such that for any cone

Q\in\Lambda
of dimension
(d-1)
, we have

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\Lambda_{d}\\
Q\subset P,\sigma_{Q}=\sigma_{P}\end{subarray}}w_{P}v_{P,Q}=0

in
N_{\sigma_{Q}}/\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Z}}Q
, where
v_{P,Q}\in N_{\sigma_{Q},\mathbb{Q}}/\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}Q

is the primitive vector such that
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\cdot v_{P,Q}
equals
the image of
P\cap N_{\sigma_{Q},\mathbb{R}}
in
N_{\sigma_{Q},\mathbb{R}}/\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{R}}Q
.
(Here
\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{R}Q
(
R=\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{R}
) is the tangent space considered as
a
R
-submodule of
N_{\sigma_{Q},R}
in the natural way.) By abuse
of notation, let
X
also denote
\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\nolimits\Lambda\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma})
.

Remark 2.7
.

In most literature, we only assume that
\Lambda
is locally finite.
Finiteness of
\Lambda
is used to ensure that
X
is compactifiable
(Definition
8.30
), which is needed to see that
tropical intersection homology is finite dimensional.

Example 2.8
.

The pair

(\left\{\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}}\mid\sigma\in\Sigma\right\},(w_{\overline{N_{\mathbb{R}}}}:=1)_{\overline{N_{\mathbb{R}}}=\overline{N_{\left\{(0,\dots,0)\right\},\mathbb{R}}}\in\left\{\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}}\mid\sigma\in\Sigma\right\}_{n}}),

where the closures
\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}}
are taken in
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma})
,
is a tropical variety of dimension
n
whose support is
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma})
.
By abuse of notation, we also call this a
tropical toric variety,

and denote it by
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma})
. For example, we have
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\mathbb{A}^{s})=(\mathbb{R}\cup\left\{\infty\right\})^{s}

for a fixed toric strcture of
\mathbb{A}^{s}
.

For technical reason, in this paper, we consider only tropcial varieties

regular at infinity
. This notion is used in the literature,
see e.g.,
[
MZ14
, Definition 1.2]
.

Definition 2.9
.

We assume that
T_{\Sigma}
is smooth.
In this paper, we say that a polyhedron
P\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma})

is
regular at infinity
if

•

P\cap N_{\mathbb{R}}\neq\emptyset
, and

•

for any
\tau\in\Sigma
and
x\in P\cap N_{\tau,\mathbb{R}}
, there exists
an open neighborhood
U_{x}\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma})
of
x
such
that

P\cap N_{\mathbb{R}}\cap U_{x}=((P\cap N_{\tau,\mathbb{R}})\times\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{R}}\tau)\cap U_{x}

for some (any) splitting
N\cong N_{\tau}\times\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Z}}\tau
of
natural exact sequence

0\to\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Z}}\tau\to N\to N_{\tau}\to 0.

We say that a tropical variety
(\Lambda,w)
is
regular at
infinity
if every maximal dimensional polyhedron
P\in\Lambda_{d}

is regular at infinity.

Definition 2.10
.

For
i=1,2
, let
M_{i}

be a free
\mathbb{Z}
-module of finite rank,
N_{i}:=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(M_{i},\mathbb{Z})
, and

\Sigma_{i}
a fan in
N_{i,\mathbb{R}}
. Let
X_{i}=(\Lambda_{i},w_{i}=(w_{i,P_{i}})_{P_{i}\in\Lambda_{i,d_{i}}})

(
i=1,2
) be a tropical variety of dimension
d_{i}
in
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma_{i}})
.
We put

\Lambda_{X_{1}\times X_{2}}:=\Lambda_{1}\times\Lambda_{2}:=\left\{R_{1}\times R_{2}\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma_{1}})\times\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma_{2}})\mid R_{1}\in\Lambda_{1},R_{2}\in\Lambda_{2}\right\}

a pure-
(d_{1}+d_{2})
-dimensional polyhedral complex in
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma_{1}})\times\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma_{2}})
,
and for
P_{X_{1}\times X_{2}}\in\Lambda_{X_{1}\times X_{2},d_{1}+d_{2}}
,
we put

w_{X_{1}\times X_{2},P_{X_{1}\times X_{2}}}:=w_{1,P_{1}}\times w_{2,P_{2}},

where
P_{i}\in\Lambda_{i,d_{i}}
(
i=1,2
) is the polyhedron such
that
P_{X_{1}\times X_{2}}=P_{1}\times P_{2}
. We put

X_{1}\times X_{2}:=(\Lambda_{X_{1}\times X_{2}},w_{X_{1}\times X_{2}}:=(w_{X_{1}\times X_{2},P_{X_{1}\times X_{2}}})_{P_{X_{1}\times X_{2}}\in\Lambda_{X_{1}\times X_{2},d_{1}+d_{2}}})

a tropical variety of dimension
(d_{1}+d_{2})
in
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma_{1}}\times T_{\Sigma_{2}})
.

3.
Geometric tropical intersection homology

In the section, let
X=(\Lambda,w=(w_{P})_{P\in\Lambda_{d}})
be
a tropical variety of dimension
d
in a tropical toric variety
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma})
.
We assume that
T_{\Sigma}
is smooth, and
X
is regular at infinity
(Definition
2.9
).

3.1.
Geometric definition

Tropical homology, introduced by Itenberg-Katzarkov-Mikhalkin-Zharkov
(
[
IKMZ19
]
), is
defined as homology of singular chains on
X
with some coefficients

F_{p}
. In this subsection, we shall give a geometric definition
(Definition
3.9
) of tropical analog of Goresky-MacPherson’s
intersection homology (
[
GM80
]
,

[
GM83
]
),
tropical
intersection homology
, of
X
. We put
\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\subset\Lambda

the subset of polyhedra
R
of dimension
\geq(d-1)
such that
R\cap N_{\mathbb{R}}\subset R

is dense (i.e.,
R\cap N_{\mathbb{R}}\neq\emptyset
). (We have
\Lambda_{d}\subset\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}

since
X
is regular at infinity.) We also put
\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}:=\Lambda\setminus\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}
.
The subset
\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}
plays the role of singular strata in
the theory of Goresky-MacPherson’s intersection homology.

In Goresky-MacPherson’s theory, they only consider spaces smooth away
from subsets of codimension
\geq 2
. This is because when there are
singular subsets of codimension
1
, we can hope at best duality
between absolute cohomology and relative cohomology. In tropical geometry,
the subsets
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits Q
(
Q\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d-1}
) play the role
of
1
-codimensional strata. However, the complex of sheaves of tropical
cochains
\mathscr{C}^{p,*}
does not necessary satisfy Poincaré-Verdier
duality near
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits Q
(
Q\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d-1}
) (i.e.,
\mathscr{C}^{p,*}\not\cong D(\mathscr{C}^{d-p,*})
).
For this reason, we will use a version of Gubler-Jell-Rabinoff’s coefficients

[
GJR21a
]

[
GJR21b
]
on
X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}:=\bigcup_{R\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits R
,
which give a cohomology satisfying Poincaré-Verdier duality near
such “
1
-codimensional strata”. (Note that the author is not sure
whether our coefficients can be considered as an analog of their coefficients
on the whole
X
, but the coefficients outside of
X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}
are
not important in this paper.)

Definition 3.1
.

Let
p\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

•

For
P\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d}
of dimension
d
, we put

F_{p,w}(P):=\wedge^{p}\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}P.

•

For
Q\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d-1}
of dimension
(d-1)
, we put
F_{p,w}(Q)

the quotient of

\bigoplus_{P\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d},Q\subsetneq P}\wedge^{p}\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}P

by identifying

\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits(\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}Q\to\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}P_{1})=\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits(\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}Q\to\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}P_{2})

for
P_{i}\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d}
with
Q\subsetneq P_{i}
(
i=1,2
)
and

\sum_{P\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d},Q\subsetneq P}w_{P}v_{P,Q}=0,

where
\tilde{v_{P,Q}}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}P\subset N_{\mathbb{Q}}
is a lifting
of
v_{P,Q}
as in Definition
2.6

such that we have
\sum_{P\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d},Q\subsetneq P}w_{P}v_{P,Q}=0

in
N_{\mathbb{Q}}
.

It is easy to see that
F_{p,w}(Q)
is independent of the choices
of
v_{P,Q}
.

•

For a polyhedron
R\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits,d-1}
of dimension
(d-1)
,
since
X
is regular at infinity, there exist a unique
d
-dimensional
polyhedron
\tilde{{R}}\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d}
containing
R
and a
unique
1
-dimensional cone
l\in\Xi
such that
R=\tilde{{R}}\cap\overline{N_{l,\mathbb{R}}}
.
Then
\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}l\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}\tilde{{R}}
, and we put

F_{p,w}(R):=\wedge^{p}(\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}\tilde{{R}}/\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}l).

For
R\in\Lambda_{d-1}
and
P\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d}
with
R\subset P
,
there is a natural map
\iota_{R\subset P}\colon F_{p,w}(P)\to F_{p,w}(R)
.
We extend
F_{p,w}
to any
S\in\Lambda
in a formal way.

Definition 3.2
.

For a polyhedron
S\in\Lambda
, we put

F_{p,w}(S):=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d}\\
S\subset P\end{subarray}}{\bigoplus}F_{p,w}(P)/\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}R\in\Lambda_{d-1}\\
S\subset R\end{subarray}}{\sum}\mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}\nolimits(\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d}\\
R\subset P\end{subarray}}{\bigoplus}F_{p,w}(P)\to F_{p,w}(R)).

For
S_{1},S_{2}\in\Lambda
with
S_{2}\subset S_{1}
, there is
a natural map
\iota_{S_{2}\subset S_{1}}\colon F_{p,w}(S_{1})\to F_{p,w}(S_{2}).

Remark 3.3
.

Let
P\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d}
be a polyhedron of dimension
d
, and
a polyhedron
S\in\Lambda
contained in
P
. Since
X
is regular
at infinity, we have

\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}\sigma_{S}\subset\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\sigma_{S}}^{-1}(\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}S)\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}P,

where
\sigma_{S}\in\Sigma
is the cone such that
N_{\sigma_{S},\mathbb{R}}

contains
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S
, and
\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\sigma_{S}}\colon N_{\mathbb{Q}}\to N_{\sigma_{S},\mathbb{Q}}

is the projection (given by the restriction to
M\cap\sigma_{S}^{\perp}
),
and we have

\dim\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\sigma_{S}}^{-1}(\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}S)=\dim S+\dim\sigma_{S}.

The following numbers
v(\alpha\cap\sigma_{S})
and
u(\alpha\cap\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\sigma_{S}}^{-1}(S))

are cores in geometric definition (Definition
3.9
)
of tropical intersection homology.

Definition 3.4
.

For a polyhedron
P\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d}
of dimension

d
, a polyhedron
S\in\Lambda
contained in
P
, and
\alpha\in F_{p,w}(P)
,
we put

v(\alpha\cap\sigma_{S}):=\max\left\{j\leq p\mid\alpha\in\bigwedge^{j}\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}\sigma_{S}\wedge\bigwedge^{p-j}\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}P\right\}

and

\displaystyle u(\alpha\cap\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\sigma_{S}}^{-1}(S)):=\max\left\{l\leq p\mid\alpha\in\bigwedge^{l}\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\sigma_{S}}^{-1}(\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}S)\wedge\bigwedge^{p-l}\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}P\right\}

(see
[
MZ14
, Subsection 1.2, 1.3, and 3.1]

for a related notion).

Of course, we have
v(\alpha\cap\sigma_{S})\leq u(\alpha\cap\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\sigma_{S}}^{-1}(S))
.

Let
T
be a (locally finite) possibly non-rational polyhedral complex
structure of
X
finer than
\Lambda
. For each polyhedron
\Delta\in T
,
we put
R_{\Delta}\in\Lambda
the polyhedron such that
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits R_{\Delta}\supset\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits\Delta
.
Then we get the natural boundary map

\partial\colon\prod_{\Delta\in T_{q}}F_{p,w}(R_{\Delta})[\Delta]\to\prod_{\tau\in T_{q-1}}F_{p,w}(R_{\tau})[\tau]

given by

\alpha_{\Delta}[\Delta]\mapsto\sum_{i}\iota_{R_{\tau_{i}}\subset R_{\Delta}}(\alpha_{\Delta})\epsilon_{\tau_{i},\Delta}[\tau_{i}]

(
\alpha_{\Delta}\in F_{p,w}(R_{\Delta})
,
\partial[\Delta]=\sum_{i}\epsilon_{\tau_{i},\Delta}[\tau_{i}]
),
where
T_{i}\subset T
is the subset of
i
-dimensional polyhedra,

[\mu]\in H_{\dim\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}(\mu,\partial\mu;\mathbb{Z})
(
\mu\in T
)
is a generator,
\epsilon_{\tau_{i},\Delta}\in\left\{\pm 1\right\}
.
We put

C_{p,q,w}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,T}(X):=\prod_{\Delta\in T_{q}}F_{p,w}(R_{\Delta})[\Delta].

We put

C_{p,q,w}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(X):=\underrightarrow{\lim}C_{p,q,w}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,T}(X),

where the right hand side is the inductive limit of all polyhedral
complex structures
T
of
X
with respect to subdivisions.

Definition 3.5
.

A
(p,q)
-chain
\gamma=\sum\alpha_{\Delta}[\Delta]\in C_{p,q,w}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(X)

is
allowable
at
S\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}
if for any polyhedron

\Delta
intersecting with
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S
, the coefficient
\alpha_{\Delta}\in F_{p,w}(R_{\Delta})

is
0
or a sum of images of
\alpha_{\Delta,i}\neq 0\in F_{p,w}(P_{\Delta,i})

(
R_{\Delta}\subset P_{\Delta,i}\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d}
) under the natural
map
\iota_{R_{\Delta}\subset P_{\Delta,i}}\colon F_{p,w}(P_{\Delta,i})\to F_{p,w}(R_{\Delta})

such that

q-\dim(\Delta\cap\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S)+v(\alpha_{\Delta,i}\cap\sigma_{S})\geq\max\left\{2,p+\dim\sigma_{S}-u(\alpha_{\Delta,i}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\sigma_{S}}^{-1}(S))+1\right\}

or

\dim\sigma_{S}=0\ \text{and}\ q-\dim(\Delta\cap\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S)=d-\dim S.

Some reasons of our allowability are explained in Remark
3.6

and Remark
3.15
.

Remark 3.6
.

By definition, we have

u(\alpha_{\Delta,i}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\sigma_{S}}^{-1}(S))\geq p-d+\dim S+\dim\sigma_{S}.

We assume
\dim\sigma_{S}=0
and
q-\dim(\Delta\cap\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S)=d-\dim S.

Then when

\displaystyle u(\alpha_{\Delta,i}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\sigma_{S}}^{-1}(S))\geq p-d+\dim S+1,

the first condition (i.e., the inequality) of Definition
3.5

holds. Therefore the second condition is needed only when

u(\alpha_{\Delta,i}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\sigma_{S}}^{-1}(S))=p-d+\dim S.

This holds e.g., when
\gamma
is the fundamental class
[X]
(Subsection

5.1
).

Definition 3.7
.

A
(p,q)
-chain
\gamma\in C_{p,q,w}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(X)
is
allowable

if it is allowable at all
S\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}
.

Remark 3.8
.

For an allowable chain
\gamma=\sum\alpha_{\Delta}[\Delta]\in C_{p,q,w}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(X)
,
since

p+\dim\sigma_{S}-v(\alpha_{\Delta,i}\cap\sigma_{S})-u(\alpha_{\Delta,i}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\sigma_{S}}^{-1}(S))\geq 0,

we have
R_{\Delta}\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}
for
\Delta
with
\alpha_{\Delta}\neq 0
.

Definition 3.9
.

We put

IC_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{BM}}\nolimits}(X):=\left\{\gamma\in C_{p,q,w}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(X)\mid\gamma\ \text{and }\partial\gamma\ \text{{are}}\ \text{{allowable}}\right\},

the
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space of tropical intersection locally finite geometric

(p,q)
-chains. We put
IC_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{cpt}}\nolimits}(X)
its subsets of
chains of compact supports. We put
IH_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{BM}}\nolimits}(X):=IH_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{BM}}\nolimits}(X;\mathbb{Q})

(resp.
IH_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{cpt}}\nolimits}(X):=IH_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{cpt}}\nolimits}(X;\mathbb{Q})
) the

q
-th homology group of
IC_{p,*}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{BM}}\nolimits}(X)
(resp.
IC_{p,*}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{cpt}}\nolimits}(X)
),
called the
tropical intersection Borel-Moore
(resp.
compactly
supported
)

(p,q)
-homology group
. When
X
is compact,
they coincide, and hence we omit the superscripts
\mathop{\mathrm{cpt}}\nolimits
and
\mathop{\mathrm{BM}}\nolimits
,
and call it the
tropical intersection

(p,q)
-homology
group.

For an open subset
U\subset X
, we put

\displaystyle IC_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{BM}}\nolimits}(X,X\setminus U)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle IC_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{BM}}\nolimits}(X)/\left\{\gamma\in IC_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{BM}}\nolimits}(X)\mid\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\nolimits\gamma\subset X\setminus U\right\}.

Definition 3.10
.

We put
IC_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}
the
sheafification of the presheaf

U\mapsto IC_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{BM}}\nolimits}(X,X\setminus U)

on
X
(cf.
[
GS23
, Definition 4.6(a)]

and
[
Bea84
, Chapter II. Section 1]
).
We put

IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d-p,*}:=IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}:=IC_{p,-*-p}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}

and

IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,\epsilon_{1}}^{d-p,d-q}(X):=IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,\epsilon_{1}}^{d-p,d-q}(X;\mathbb{Q}):=H_{\epsilon_{1}}^{-p-q}(R\Gamma(IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d-p,*}))=IH_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\epsilon_{2}}(X)

(
(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})=(\emptyset,\mathop{\mathrm{BM}}\nolimits),(c,\mathop{\mathrm{cpt}}\nolimits)
).

In Subsection
5.2
, we will also
define
IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,\epsilon}^{d-p,d-q}(X)

(\epsilon=\emptyset,c
),
and show that it is canonically isomorphic to
IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,\epsilon}^{d-p,d-q}(X)

(Proposition
6.3
). Hence we will
simply denote them by
IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\epsilon}^{d-p,d-q}(X)
.

Note that similarly to usual intersection chains
[
Bea84
, Chapter II, Proposition 5.1]
,
the complex
IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}
is a complex of soft sheaves.

We shall define filtrations on
IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}
locally,
which play the central role in our local computation in Subsection

3.2
. For
S\in\Lambda
, we fix open
polyhedral (Definition
2.3
)
neighborhoods
W_{S}
of
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S
in
X
such that
W_{S}\cap W_{S^{\prime}}=\emptyset

for
S,S^{\prime}\in\Lambda
with
S\not\subset S^{\prime}
and
S^{\prime}\not\subset S
.
In particular, we have

W_{S}\subset\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}R\in\Lambda\\
S\subset R\end{subarray}}\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits R.

Definition 3.11
.

For each
S\in\Lambda
and
v,u,p,q\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
satisfying
v\leq u\leq p
,
we put

T_{\sigma_{S}}^{v}T_{S}^{u}IC_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}|_{W_{S}}\subset IC_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}|_{W_{S}}

the subsheaf given by sections
\gamma=\sum\alpha_{\Delta}[\Delta]

of
IC_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}|_{W_{S}}
such that for any
\Delta
,
the coefficient
\alpha_{\Delta}\in F_{p,w}(R_{\Delta})
is
0

or a sum of images of
\alpha_{\Delta,i}\in F_{p,w}(P_{\Delta,i})

(
R_{\Delta}\subset P_{\Delta,i}\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d}
) under the natural
map
\iota_{R_{\Delta}\subset P}\colon F_{p,w}(P_{\Delta,i})\to F_{p,w}(R_{\Delta})

such that

v(\alpha_{\Delta,i}\cap\sigma_{S})\geq v\ \text{and }u(\alpha_{\Delta,i}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\sigma_{S}}^{-1}(S))\geq u.

We put
i_{W_{S}}\colon W_{S}\hookrightarrow X
and

T_{\sigma_{S}}^{v}T_{S}^{u}IC_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}:=i_{W_{S}!}(T_{\sigma_{S}}^{v}T_{S}^{u}IC_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}|_{W_{S}})\subset IC_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}.

Remark 3.12
.

We have
T_{\sigma_{S}}^{v}T_{S}^{u}IC_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}=0
unless

v\leq\dim\sigma_{S}
and
u\leq\dim S+\dim\sigma_{S}
.

Definition 3.13
.

Let
S\in\Lambda
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}
. We put

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{k}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,-p-q}(a):=\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{k}IC_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}:=\begin{cases}IC_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}&(k\leq 0)\\
\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\leq u\leq p\\
v+u=k\end{subarray}}T_{\sigma_{S}}^{v}T_{S}^{u}IC_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}&(\text{1$\leq$k$\leq$}\dim S+2\dim\sigma_{S})\\
0&(k\geq\dim S+2\dim\sigma_{S}+1)\end{cases}

a subsheaf of
IC_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}
.

For
a=(a_{S})_{S\in\Lambda}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}
, we put

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}(a):=\bigcap_{S\in\Lambda}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{-a_{S}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}(a)\subset IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}.

For
a,b\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}
with
a_{S}\leq b_{S}
(
S\in\Lambda
),
we have

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}(a)\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}(b).

These define an object

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}\in D_{c}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda}),

see Section
4
for the derived category
D_{c}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})
.

Remark 3.14
.

For each stratum
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S
(
S\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}
), we considered
filtrations on an open neighborhood
W_{S}
of
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S
instead
of that on
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S
. This is because we need to recover
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}

from its restriction to an open subset
X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}:=\bigcup_{R\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits R
,
which does not intersect with
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S
(
S\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}
).
(See Section
5
for details.)

Remark 3.15
.

We give remark on allowable and non-allowable
chains. Let
S\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}
and
\gamma=\sum\alpha_{\Delta}[\Delta]\in C_{p,q,w}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(X)
.

•

When
q=d
and
\partial\gamma=0
, the chain
\gamma
is always
allowable at any
S
. The case of
\dim\sigma_{S}=0
follows from
the second condition of allowability (cf. Remark
3.6
).
Otherwise, we can take
v(\alpha_{\Delta,i}\cap\sigma_{S})\geq 1

in Definition
3.5
because
X
is regular at infinity
and
\partial\gamma=0
, hence by

u(\alpha_{\Delta,i}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\sigma_{S}}^{-1}(S))\geq p-d+\dim S+\dim\sigma_{S},

\gamma
is allowable at
S
.

•

When
S
is
0
-dimensional,
S\subset N_{\mathbb{R}}
, and
\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\nolimits\gamma\cap S\neq\emptyset
,
the chain
\gamma
is allowable at
S
if and only if
q=d
or

\max\left\{2,p+1\right\}\leq q
. This is used in Lemma
7.6
.

•

Small open neighborhoods of
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S
in
X
can be identified
with small open neighborhood of

\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(O(\tau_{S}))\times\{\mathop{\mathrm{pt}}\nolimits\}\times\{(0,\dots,0)\}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\tau_{S}})\times\mathbb{R}^{\dim S}\times\Lambda_{S},

where
\tau_{S}
is the cone
\sigma_{S}
considered as a cone
in
\mathop{\mathrm{Span}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{R}}\sigma_{\mathbb{R}}
, the affine toric variety
T_{\tau_{S}}

corresponds to the cone
\tau_{S}
(in particular,
O(\tau_{S})\subset T_{\tau_{S}}

is the
0
-dimensional orbit),
\mathbb{R}^{\dim S}=\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{s})
is
a tropical toric variety,
\mathop{\mathrm{pt}}\nolimits\in\mathbb{R}^{\dim S}
is any point, and
\Lambda_{S}

is the star fan of
S
, which is a fan in
\mathbb{R}^{n-\dim S-\dim\sigma_{S}}
.
Since Künneth formula (Subsection
6.1
)
holds for
IH_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}
, our allowability is affected by this
decomposition, in particular, is affected by a decomposition of coefficients

\displaystyle F_{p,w}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\tau_{S}})\times\{\mathop{\mathrm{pt}}\nolimits\}\times\{(0,\dots,0)\})

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{i,j}F_{j,w}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\tau_{S}}))\otimes F_{i,w}(\mathbb{R}^{\dim S})\otimes F_{p-i-j,w}(\{(0,\dots,0)\}).

This leads our two functions
u
and
v
.

•

(toric case) We assume that
X=\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma})
(Example
2.8
).
Let
\delta\in C_{p+q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{BM}}\nolimits}(T_{\Sigma}(\mathbb{C}))
be a singular
chain such that
\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\nolimits\delta\cap\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}(\mathbb{C})\subset\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\nolimits\delta

is dense, and the restriction
\delta|_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}(\mathbb{C})}
to
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}(\mathbb{C})\subset T_{\Sigma}(\mathbb{C})

is of the form

\delta|_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}(\mathbb{C})}=\sum_{l}\alpha_{l}\times\beta_{l}

(
\alpha_{l}\in C_{p}^{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}((S^{1})^{n})
,
\beta_{l}\in C_{q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{BM}}\nolimits}(\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{n})
),
where we identify

\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}(\mathbb{C})\cong(\mathbb{C}^{\times})^{n}\cong\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{n}\times(S^{1})^{n}.

We consider
\alpha_{l}\in C_{p}^{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}((S^{1})^{n})
as an element
of

\bigwedge^{p}\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{R}}N_{\mathbb{R}}\cong\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(\bigwedge^{p}M\otimes\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R})

by

\alpha_{l}\colon\bigwedge^{p}M\otimes\mathbb{R}\ni\sum_{i}a_{i}f_{i,1}\wedge\dots\wedge f_{i,p}\mapsto\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{p}}\sum_{i}a_{i}\int_{\alpha}d\log f_{i,1}\wedge d\log f_{i,p}\in\mathbb{R}

(
a_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
,
f_{i,j}\in M
), where
f_{i,j}\in M
is also considered
as a holomorphic function on
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}(\mathbb{C})=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[M](\mathbb{C})
. We
assume that the restriction
\gamma|_{N_{\mathbb{R}}}
of
\gamma
is of
the form

\gamma|_{N_{\mathbb{R}}}=\sum_{l}\alpha_{l}(-\log_{*}(\beta_{l})),

where
-\log\colon\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
is the coordinate-wise map.
The toric variety
T_{\Sigma}(\mathbb{C})
is stratified by torus orbits,
and we can consider allowability of
\delta
with respect to the
middle perversity
\overline{m}
(
[
GM80
, Subsection 1.3 and Subsection 5.1]
)
(here for simplicity, we ignore piecewise linearity). Then by definition,
for each
\sigma\in\Sigma
, the chain
\delta
is allowable at strata

O(\sigma)(\mathbb{C})
if and only if
\gamma
is allowable at
\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}}
.

3.2.
Local computations

For
S\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}
and
x\in\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S
, we shall prove attaching
property when
\dim\sigma_{S}=0
(Proposition
3.20
)
and a vanishing type result of the stalk
\mathscr{H}^{m}(IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*})_{x}

(Corollary
3.26
). When
\dim\sigma_{S}=0
,
proofs are same as
[
GM83
, Subsection 2.4 and 2.5]
.
When
\dim\sigma_{S}\geq 1
, attaching property will follow from comparison
to sheaf-theoretic definition (Proposition
6.3
).

Remark 3.16
.

When
\dim\sigma_{S}=0
(i.e.,
x\in\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S\subset N_{\mathbb{R}}
), there
is a neighborhood of
x
in
X\cap N_{\mathbb{R}}
which is stratified PL-homeomorphic
to
x*S^{\dim S-1}*L_{S}
, where
X\cap N_{\mathbb{R}}
is equipped with
the natural PL-structure,
*
means PL-joins,
S^{\dim S-1}
is
the
(\dim S-1)
-PL-sphere,
L_{S}
is the stratified PL-link of

\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S
in
X\cap N_{\mathbb{R}}
. We take
L_{S}\subset W_{S}
.

For a compact subset
L\subset X
, we put

C_{p,q,w}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(L;F_{X}):=\left\{\gamma\in C_{p,q,w}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{cpt}}\nolimits}(X)\mid\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\nolimits\gamma\subset L\right\}.

We also put

IC_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(L;F_{X}):=C_{p,q,w}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(L;F_{X})\cap IC_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{cpt}}\nolimits}(X)

and put
IH_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(L;F_{X})
its homology group. When
L\subset W_{S}
,
we also put

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{k}IC_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(L;F_{X}):=IC_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(L;F_{X})\cap\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{k}IC_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}|_{W_{S}}(W_{S})

and
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{k}IH_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(L;F_{X})
its homology group.

Proposition 3.17
.

When
\dim\sigma_{S}=0
, for

2\leq q\leq d-1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{-p-q}(IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*})_{x}

\displaystyle\cong\begin{cases}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{p+\dim S-q+1}IH_{p,q-\dim S-1}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(L_{S};F_{X})&(q\geq\dim S+2)\\
0&(q\leq\dim S+1)\end{cases}.

Though proof is same as
[
GM83
, Subsection 2.4]
,
since our coefficients are complicated a little, we give proof for
the reader’s convenience.

Remark 3.18
.

Let
A=\sum_{\boxempty}[\boxempty]\in C_{j}(S^{\dim S-1})

be a PL-chain, and

B=\sum_{\Delta,i}\alpha_{\Delta,i}[\Delta]\in C_{p,q-j-2,w}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(L_{S};F_{X}).

Then for a polyhedron
S^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}
with
S^{\prime}\supsetneq S

(in particular,
\dim\sigma_{S^{\prime}}=0
), we have

\displaystyle q-1-\dim(([\boxempty]*[\Delta])\cap\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S^{\prime})\geq\max\left\{2,p-u(\alpha_{\Delta,i}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\sigma_{S^{\prime}}}^{-1}(S^{\prime}))+1\right\}

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle q-j-2-\dim([\Delta]\cap\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S^{\prime})\geq\max\left\{2,p-u(\alpha_{\Delta,i}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\sigma_{S^{\prime}}}^{-1}(S^{\prime}))+1\right\},

and

\displaystyle q-1-\dim(([\boxempty]*[\Delta])\cap\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S^{\prime})=d-\dim S^{\prime}

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle q-j-2-\dim([\Delta]\cap\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S^{\prime})=d-\dim S^{\prime}.

Namely, the join
A*B
is allowable at
S^{\prime}
if and only if
B

is allowable at
S^{\prime}
. Similarly, the join
A*B
is allowable at

S
if and only if

\displaystyle q-j-1\geq\max\left\{2,p-u(\alpha_{\Delta,i}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\sigma_{S}}^{-1}(S))+1\right\}

or

q-j-1=d-\dim S.

Lemma 3.19
.

Let
A_{j,l}\in C_{j}(S^{\dim S-1})

and
B_{j,l}\in C_{p,q-j-2,w}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(L_{S};F_{X})
with
\dim(A_{j,l}\cap A_{j,m})\leq j-1

for
m\neq l
. Then

\sum_{j\geq 0,l}A_{j,l}*B_{j,l}+B_{-1,0}\in IC_{p,q-1}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(S^{\dim S-1}*L_{S};F_{X})

if and only if each
A_{j,l}*B_{j,l}
and
B_{-1,0}
is in
IC_{p,q-1}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(S^{\dim S-1}*L_{S};F_{X})
.

Proof.

If part is trivial. To show the converse, it suffices to show that
each
\partial(A_{j,l}*B_{j,l})
and
\partial B_{-1,0}
is allowable.
Since
A_{j,l}*B_{j,l}
are allowable, by Remark
3.18
,
the chain
(\partial A_{j,l})*B_{j,l}
is allowable. Since

\partial(\sum_{j\geq 0,l}A_{j,l}*B_{j,l}+B_{-1,0})=\sum_{j\geq 0,l}(\partial A_{j,l})*B_{j,l}+(-1)^{j+1}A_{j,l}*(\partial B_{j,l})+\partial B_{-1,0}

is allowable, by the assumption on
A_{j,l}
, each
\partial(A_{j,l}*B_{j,l})

and
\partial B_{-1,0}
is allowable.
∎

Proof.

(Proposition
3.17
) We shall compute

IH_{p,q-1}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(S^{\dim S-1}*L_{S};F_{X}).
We put

Z_{q-j-2}:=\begin{cases}0&(q-j-2\geq d-\dim S)\\
IZ_{p,q-j-2}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(L_{S};F_{X})\\
+\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{p+j+3-q}IC_{p,q-j-2}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(L_{S};F_{X})&(2\leq q-j-2=d-\dim S-1)\\
IZ_{p,q-j-2}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(L_{S};F_{X})&(1=q-j-2=d-\dim S-1)\\
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{p+j+2-q}IZ_{p,q-j-2}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(L_{S};F_{X})\\
+\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{p+j+3-q}IC_{p,q-j-2}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(L_{S};F_{X})&(2\leq q-j-2\leq d-\dim S-2)\\
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{p+j+2-q}IZ_{p,q-j-2}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(L_{S};F_{X})&(1=q-j-2\leq d-\dim S-2)\\
0&(q-j-2\leq 0)\end{cases},

where
IZ_{*,*}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}\subset IC_{*,*}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}
is the subset of
closed chains. Let

f\colon\bigoplus_{j=-1}^{\dim S-1}\hat{C}_{j}(S^{\dim S-1})\otimes Z_{q-j-2}\to IC_{p,q-1}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(S^{\dim S-1}*L_{S};F_{X})

be the map given by

\sum_{j\geq-1,l}A_{j,l}\otimes B_{j,l}\mapsto\sum_{j\geq 0,l}(-1)^{\dim B_{j,l}}A_{j,l}*B_{j,l}+\sum(-1)_{l}^{\dim B_{-1,l}}A_{-1,l}B_{-1,l},

where we put

\hat{C}_{j}(S^{\dim S-1}):=\begin{cases}C_{j}(S^{\dim S-1})&(j\geq 0)\\
\mathbb{Q}&(j=-1)\end{cases}

a complex with the boundary map
\hat{C}_{0}(S^{\dim S-1})\to\hat{C}_{-1}(S^{\dim S-1})

given by
\sum_{y\in S^{\dim S-1}}a_{y}[y]\mapsto\sum a_{y}
. By
Remark
3.18
, the map

f
is well-defined and gives a morphism

f\colon(\hat{C}_{*}(S^{\dim S-1})\otimes Z_{*})_{*}\to IC_{p,*}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(S^{\dim S-1}*L_{S};F_{X})

of complexes from the single complex
(\hat{C}_{*}(S^{\dim S-1})\otimes Z_{*})_{*}

associated to the double complex
\hat{C}_{*}(S^{\dim S-1})\otimes Z_{*}
.

We shall give a homotopy inverse
\psi
of
f
. For a polyhedron

\Delta\subset S^{\dim S-1}*L_{S}
, we put
\varphi(\Delta)\subset S^{\dim S-1}*L_{S}

the polyhedron whose vertices are the vertices of
\Delta\cap S^{\dim S-1}

and the image of the other vertices of
\Delta
under the peusdo-radical
retraction

(S^{\dim S-1}*L_{S})\setminus S^{\dim S-1}\twoheadrightarrow L_{S}

along join lines. (We have
\varphi(\Delta)=(\Delta\cap S^{\dim S-1})*(\varphi(\Delta)\cap L_{S})
.)
This give a morphism

\psi\colon IC_{p,q-1}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(S^{\dim S-1}*L_{S};F_{X})\to\bigoplus_{j=-1}^{\dim S-1}\hat{C}_{j}(S^{\dim S-1})\otimes C_{p,q-j-2,w}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(L_{S};F_{X})

by
\alpha_{\Delta}[\Delta]\mapsto\epsilon_{\Delta}[\Delta\cap S^{\dim S-1}]\otimes\alpha_{\Delta}[\varphi(\Delta)\cap L_{S}]

(
\epsilon_{\Delta}\in\{\pm 1\}
) (where we canonically identify

\hat{C}_{-1}(S^{\dim S-1})\otimes C_{p,q-1,w}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(L_{S};F_{X})

with
C_{p,q-1,w}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(L_{S};F_{X})
). We shall show that the
image of
\psi
is contained in the source of
f
. For an element

\eta
in the source of
\psi
, we put

\psi(\eta)=\sum_{j\geq 0,l}A_{j,l}\otimes B_{j,l}+B_{-1,0}\quad(A_{j,l}\in C_{j}(S^{\dim S-1}),B_{j,l}\in C_{p,q-j-2,w}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(L_{S};F_{X}))

with
\dim(A_{j,l}\cap A_{j,m})\leq j-1
for
m\neq l
. Then since
for each polyhedron
\Delta
and strata
S^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}
,
we have

\dim(\Delta\cap\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S^{\prime})=\dim(\varphi(\Delta)\cap\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S^{\prime}),

we have
f(\psi(\eta))\in IC_{p,q-1}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(S^{\dim S-1}*L_{S};F_{X})

(where
f
is extended canonically). By Lemma
3.19
,
the chains
A_{j,l}*B_{j,l}
and
B_{-1,0}
are in
IC_{p,q-1,w}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(S^{\dim S-1}*L_{S};F_{X})
.
Hence by Remark
3.18
,
we have
\psi(\eta)\in(\hat{C}_{*}(S^{\dim S-1})\otimes Z_{*})_{q-1}
,
i.e., the morphism
\psi
induces

\psi\colon IC_{p,*}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(S^{\dim S-1}*L_{S};F_{X})\to(\hat{C}_{*}(S^{\dim S-1})\otimes Z_{*})_{*}.

It is easy to see that
\psi
is a homotopy inverse of
f
(with
a chain homotopy
h
given by mapping cylinder of natural PL-map

\varphi\colon\Delta\cong\varphi(\Delta)
). Since

H_{j}(\hat{C}_{*}(S^{\dim S-1}))=\begin{cases}\mathbb{Q}[S^{\dim S-1}]&(j=\dim S-1)\\
0&(\text{otherwise}),\end{cases}

we have

IH_{p,q-1}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(S^{\dim S-1}*L_{S};F_{X})\cong\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{p+\dim S-q+1}IH_{p,q-\dim S-1}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(L_{S};F_{X})

for
2\leq q\leq d-1
. Every element of
\mathscr{H}^{-p-q}(IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*})_{x}

has a representative of the form
x*\gamma
for some
\gamma\in IZ_{p,q-1}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(S^{\dim S-1}*L_{S};F_{X})
.
Conversely, for such a
\gamma
, the join
x*\gamma
gives
an elment of
\mathscr{H}^{-p-q}(IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*})_{x}
. This give
an isomorphism

\mathscr{H}^{-p-q}(IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*})_{x}\cong IH_{p,q-1}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(S^{\dim S-1}*L_{S};F_{X}).

Therefore we have completed proof.
∎

Let
i_{S}\colon W_{S}\setminus\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S\hookrightarrow W_{S}
be
an open embedding, and
j_{S}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S\hookrightarrow W_{S}

a closed embedding.

Proposition 3.20
.

When
\dim\sigma_{S}=0
, the natural map

IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}|_{W_{S}}\to Ri_{S*}i_{S}^{*}(IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}|_{W_{S}})

induces isomorphisms

\mathscr{H}^{m}(j_{S}^{*}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{k}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}|_{W_{S}})\cong\mathscr{H}^{m}(j_{S}^{*}Ri_{S*}i_{S}^{*}(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{k}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}|_{W_{S}}))

for

\displaystyle m\leq\max\left\{-p-d,\min\left\{-2p-\dim S+k-1,-p-\dim S-2\right\}\right\}.

Proof.

Proof is completely same as
[
GM83
, Subsection 2.5]
.
We omit it.
∎

Remark 3.21
.

Let
\sigma\in\Sigma
.
We put

\Lambda\cap\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}}:=\left\{R\cap\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}}\mid R\in\Lambda\right\}

a (
d-\dim\sigma
)-dimensional polyhedral complex. For a
(d-\dim\sigma)
-dimensional
polyhedron
L\in\Lambda\cap\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}}
, we put
w_{L}:=w_{P_{L}}
,
where
P_{L}\in\Lambda_{d}
is the unique
d
-dimensional polyhedron
such that
L=P\cap\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}}
. Then

X\cap\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}}:=(\Lambda\cap\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}},(w_{L})_{L})

is a tropical variety of dimension (
d-\dim\sigma
).

Remind that
\sigma_{S}\in\Sigma
is the cone such that
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S\subset N_{\sigma_{S},\mathbb{R}}
.

Proposition 3.22
.

We assume that

\dim\sigma_{S}\geq 1
. Let
\sigma\in\Sigma
be a cone of dimension

\geq 1
contained in
\sigma_{S}
. (In particular,
x\in\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S\subset X\cap\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}}
.)
Then for
1\leq q\leq d
, we have an isomorphism

\mathscr{H}^{-p-q+2\dim\sigma}(IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X\cap\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}}}^{d-p,*})_{x}\cong\mathscr{H}^{-p-q}(IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d-p,*})_{x}.

Moreover, for
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, it gives an isomorphism

\mathscr{H}^{-p-q+2\dim\sigma}(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{k}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X\cap\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}}}^{d-p,*})_{x}\cong\mathscr{H}^{-p-q}(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{k+2\dim\sigma}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d-p,*})_{x}.

For tropical homology, this proposition is known
[
AP24b
, Lemma 3.2]
.

Proof.

When
q=d
, the assertion is easy. We assume that
q\leq d-1
. By
induction on
\dim\sigma
, we may assume that
\dim\sigma=1
. To
simplify notation, we fix an isomorphism
N\cong\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. We may assume
that
\sigma=\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\cdot e_{1}
, where
e_{1}
is the first
coordinate. We identify
N\cong\mathbb{Z}\cdot e_{1}\times N_{\sigma}.
Since
the problem is local, we may assume that

X=(\mathbb{R}\cup\left\{\infty\right\})\times(X\cap\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}}),

where
\mathbb{R}\cup\left\{\infty\right\}=\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\mathbb{A}^{1})
is a tropical
toric variety (Example
2.8
). The local
homology group
\mathscr{H}^{-p-q}(IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d-p,*})_{x}
is the
direct sum of groups
\mathscr{H}_{I}^{-p-q}(IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d-p,*})_{x}

(
I=\emptyset
or
\left\{1\right\}
) of elements having representatives

\gamma
of the form

\gamma=\sum_{\Delta}\bigwedge_{i\in I}e_{i}\wedge\alpha_{\Delta}^{\prime}[\Delta]\in IC_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(X)

with
\alpha_{\Delta}^{\prime}=\sum_{j}\alpha_{\Delta,j}^{\prime}
and
\alpha_{\Delta,j}^{\prime}\in\bigwedge^{p-\#I}(\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}P_{\Delta,j}\cap N_{\sigma,\mathbb{Q}})

(
\Delta\subset P_{\Delta,j}\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d}
). We shall show that

\mathscr{H}_{\emptyset}^{-p-q}(IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d-p,*})_{x}=0
, and

\sum_{\boxempty}\beta_{\boxempty}[\boxempty]\mapsto\sum_{\boxempty}e_{1}\wedge\beta_{\boxempty}[(\mathbb{R}\cup\left\{\infty\right\})\times\boxempty]

gives an isomorphism

\mathscr{H}^{-p-q+2}(IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X\cap\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}}}^{d-p,*})_{x}\cong\mathscr{H}_{\left\{1\right\}}^{-p-q}(IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d-p,*})_{x},

which gives

\mathscr{H}^{-p-q+2}(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{k}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X\cap\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}}}^{d-p,*})_{x}\cong\mathscr{H}^{-p-q}(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{k+2}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d-p,*})_{x}.

Claim 3.23
.

When
I=\emptyset,

we have
\mathscr{H}_{\emptyset}^{-p-q}(IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d-p,*})_{x}=0
.

Proof.

(Claim
3.23
) We
put

\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{0}\times\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}\nolimits_{\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}}}\colon(\mathbb{R}\cup\left\{\infty\right\})\times\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}}\to\left\{0\right\}\times\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}},

where
\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}\nolimits_{\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}}}
is the identity map, and
\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{0}\colon\mathbb{R}\cup\left\{\infty\right\}\to\left\{0\right\}

is the map to
0
. Let
c([\Delta])
be the mapping cyclinder of
this map. We put

c(\gamma):=\sum_{\Delta}\alpha_{\Delta}^{\prime}c([\Delta])\in C_{p,q+1,w}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(X).

Then since
\alpha_{\Delta}^{\prime}=\sum_{j}\alpha_{\Delta,j}^{\prime}
and
\alpha_{\Delta,j}^{\prime}\in\bigwedge^{p}(\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}P_{\Delta,j}\cap N_{\sigma,\mathbb{Q}})
,
the mapping cyclinder
c(\gamma)
is allowable. Namely, for
S_{1}\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}

and
\Delta
with
\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\nolimits c([\Delta])\cap\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{1}\neq\emptyset
,
there is a strata
S_{2}=S_{1}
or

S_{2}=S_{1}\cap(\left\{\infty\right\}\times\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}})

such that
\Delta\cap\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{2}\neq\emptyset
and

\dim(\Delta\cap\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{2})\geq\dim(\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\nolimits c([\Delta])\cap\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{1})-1.

When
S_{2}\neq S_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle q+1-\dim(\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\nolimits c([\Delta])\cap\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{1})+v(\alpha_{\Delta,j}^{\prime}\cap\sigma_{S_{1}})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle q-\dim(\Delta\cap\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{2})+v(\alpha_{\Delta,j}^{\prime}\cap\sigma_{S_{2}})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\max\left\{2,p+\dim\sigma_{S_{2}}-u(\alpha_{\Delta,j}^{\prime}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\sigma_{S_{2}}}^{-1}(S_{2}))+1\right\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\max\left\{2,p+\dim\sigma_{S_{1}}-u(\alpha_{\Delta,j}^{\prime}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\sigma_{S_{1}}}^{-1}(S_{1}))+1\right\},

where the first inequality follows from
v(\alpha_{\Delta,j}^{\prime}\cap\sigma_{S_{1}})=v(\alpha_{\Delta,j}^{\prime}\cap\sigma_{S_{2}})

for
\alpha_{\Delta,j}^{\prime}\in\bigwedge^{p}(\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}P_{\Delta,j}\cap N_{\sigma,\mathbb{Q}})
.
(The case of
S_{2}=S_{1}
is similar.) Similarly,
\alpha_{\Delta}^{\prime}(\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{0}\times\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}\nolimits_{\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}}})[\Delta]

is allowable. Note that

\partial c([\Delta])=[\Delta]-(\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{0}\times\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}\nolimits_{\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}}})[\Delta]+c(\partial[\Delta])

Since
\partial\gamma
is allowable,
c(\partial\gamma)
is allowable.
Consequently, the boundary
\partial c(\gamma)
is also allowable.
Therefore
c(\gamma)\in IC_{p,q+1}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(X)
. Then
\gamma-\partial c(\gamma)

has the same homology class as
\gamma
in
\mathscr{H}_{\emptyset}^{-p-q}(IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d-p,*})_{x}
.
Since
\alpha_{\Delta,j}^{\prime}\in\bigwedge^{p}(\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}P_{\Delta,j}\cap N_{\sigma,\mathbb{Q}})
,
the support of
\gamma-\partial c(\gamma)
is in
(\left\{0\right\}\times(X\cap\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}}))\cup\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\nolimits c(\partial\gamma)
.
(Note that this is not necessary the case when
I=\left\{1\right\}
,
since the natural map
F_{p,w}(P_{\Delta,j})\to F_{p,w}(P_{\Delta,j}\cap N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}})

is not injective.). Since
\partial\gamma=0
near
x
, we have
\mathscr{H}_{\emptyset}^{-p-q}(IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d-p,*})_{x}=0
.
∎

We continue proof of Proposition
3.22
.
We assume
I=\left\{1\right\}
. We put

\displaystyle\psi:=

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\infty}\times\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}\nolimits_{\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}}}\colon(\mathbb{R}\cup\left\{\infty\right\})\times\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}}\to\left\{\infty\right\}\times\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}},

where
\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\infty}\colon\mathbb{R}\cup\left\{\infty\right\}\to\left\{\infty\right\}

is the map to
\infty
. Similarly to the above, we put
c_{1}(\gamma)\in C_{p,q+1,w}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(X)

the mapping cyclinder of
\psi
. By taking a different representative
in the homology class of
\gamma
, we may assume that for
\Delta

with
\alpha_{\Delta}\neq 0
, we have
x\in\Delta
and
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits\Delta\subset\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits P

for some polyhedron
P\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d}
of dimension
d
.

Claim 3.24
.

We have

c_{1}(\gamma)\in IC_{p,q+1}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(X)
by taking a different representative
in the homology class of
\gamma
if necessary.

Proof.

(Claim
3.24
) By
allowability of
\gamma,
we can easily see that the chain
c_{1}(\gamma)

is allowable. (Note that if there existed
\Delta
with
\alpha_{\Delta}^{\prime}\neq 0

and
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits\Delta\subset\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits Q
for some polyhedron
Q\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d-1}

of dimension
(d-1)
, the mapping cyclinder
c_{1}(\gamma)
may
not be allowable at

Q\cap(\left\{\infty\right\}\times\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}})\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}.)

Since
\partial\gamma
is allowable,
c_{1}(\partial\gamma)
is
also allowable. (Strictly speaking, in this case, we need to show
that for a polyhedron
\Delta
with
\alpha_{\Delta}^{\prime}\neq 0
and
a face
\tau\subset\Delta
of codimension
1
such that
\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\nolimits c_{1}([\tau])\not=\emptyset

and
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits\tau\subset\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits Q
for some polyhedron
Q\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d-1}

of dimension
(d-1)
, we have
\tau\not\subset\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\nolimits\partial\gamma
.
Since
Q
is of the form
(\mathbb{R}\cup\left\{\infty\right\})\times(Q\cap\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}})

and
\Delta\not\subset Q
, we have

\tau=\Delta\cap((\mathbb{R}\cup\left\{\infty\right\})\times(Q\cap\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}})).

Then since
x\in\Delta
, by taking a different representative in
the homology class of
\gamma
if necessary, we have
x\in\tau
.
Consequently, since
\partial\gamma=0
near
x
, we have
\tau\not\subset\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\nolimits\partial\gamma
.)
Since
\gamma=\sum_{\Delta}e_{1}\wedge\alpha_{\Delta}^{\prime}[\Delta]
,
the support of
\gamma-\partial c_{1}(\gamma)
is in
\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\nolimits c_{1}(\partial\gamma)
.
Consequently, the boundary
\partial c_{1}(\gamma)
is also allowable.
∎

We continue proof of Proposition
3.22
.
By Claim
3.24
,

\gamma^{\prime}:=\gamma-\partial c_{1}(\gamma)=\sum_{\Delta}e_{1}\wedge\beta_{\Delta}[\Delta]\in IC_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(X)

has the same homology class as
\gamma
in
\mathscr{H}_{\left\{1\right\}}^{-p-q}(IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d-p,*})_{x}
,
and a polyhedron
\Delta
with
\beta_{\Delta}\neq 0
satisfies
\dim\psi(\Delta)=q-1
.

Claim 3.25
.

The chain
\gamma^{\prime}
is of the form

\gamma^{\prime}=\sum_{\boxempty\subset X\cap\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}}}e_{1}\wedge\beta^{\prime}_{\boxempty}[(\mathbb{R}\cup\left\{\infty\right\})\times\boxempty]

near
x
.

Proof.

(Claim
3.25
) Let
\Delta
be a polyhedron
with
\beta_{\Delta}\neq 0
, and
\tau\subset\Delta
a face of codimension

1
with
\tau\not\subset\left\{\infty\right\}\times\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}}
,

x\in\tau
, and
\dim\psi(\tau)=q-1
. Since
\partial\gamma^{\prime}=0

near
x
, we have another polyhedron
\Delta_{1}\neq\Delta
with

\tau\subset\Delta_{1}
and
\beta_{\Delta_{1}}\neq 0
. This
\Delta_{1}

is unique and
\beta_{\Delta_{1}}=\beta_{\Delta}
since
\psi(\Delta_{1})=q-1

(i.e., “
\Delta
and
\Delta_{1}
live in
(\pm e_{1})
-directions
of
\tau
respectively”). This show that
\gamma^{\prime}
is of that form.
∎

It is easy to see that we have

\sum_{\boxempty\subset X\cap\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}}}\beta^{\prime}_{\boxempty}[\boxempty]\in IC_{p-1,q-1}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(X\cap\overline{N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}}),

and this gives the isomorphisms in the assertion of Proposition
3.22
.
∎

Corollary 3.26
.

When
\dim\sigma_{S}\geq 1
, we
have

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{-p-q}(IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*})_{x}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathscr{H}^{-p-q}(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{p+\dim S+2\dim\sigma_{S}-d}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*})_{x}&(q=d)\\
\mathscr{H}^{-p-q}(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{p+\dim S+2\dim\sigma_{S}-q+1}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*})_{x}&(\dim S+\dim\sigma_{S}+2\leq q\leq d-1)\\
0&(q\leq\min\left\{\dim S+\dim\sigma_{S}+1,d-1\right\})\end{cases}.

Proof.

This is a direct corollary of Proposition
3.17

and Proposition
3.22
.
See Remark
3.6
for
q=d
.
∎

Remark 3.27
.

When
\dim\sigma_{S}\geq 1
, by
Corollary
3.26
, in particular, we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{-p-q}(IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*})_{x}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathscr{H}^{-p-q}(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{p+\dim S+\dim\sigma_{S}-q+1}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*})_{x}&(\dim S+1\leq q)\\
0&(q\leq\dim S)\end{cases}.

This weaker result is enough for sheaf theoretic study in Section

5
.
