For our sheaf-theoretic approach in Section
5
,
we need slight generalizations (Proposition
3.28
)
of Proposition
3.17
, Remark
3.27
,
and Proposition
3.20
. Let
S_{0}\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}
,

L=(S_{L,1}\subsetneq S_{L,2}\subsetneq\dots\subsetneq S_{L,r(L)}=S_{0})

(
S_{L,i}\in\Lambda
,
r(L)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
) a sequence, and
a=(a_{S^{\prime}})_{S^{\prime}\in\Lambda}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}

with
a_{S^{\prime}}\geq 0
(
S^{\prime}\in\Lambda\setminus\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}
).
We put

m_{p,S_{0}}:=-2p-\dim S_{0}-\dim\sigma_{S_{0}}-1

and
W_{L}:=\bigcap_{i=1}^{r(L)}W_{S_{L,i}}
. Remind that
W_{S_{L,i}}

are small open polyhedral neighborhoods of
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{L,i}
. We
put
a(S_{0}=m_{p,S_{0}}+p+q)\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}
by

\displaystyle a(S_{0}

\displaystyle=m_{p,S_{0}}+p+q)_{S_{0}}=m_{P,S_{0}}+p+q

\displaystyle a(S_{0}

\displaystyle=m_{p,S_{0}}+p+q)_{S^{\prime}}=a_{S^{\prime}}

(
S^{\prime}\neq S_{0}\in\Lambda
). Remind that we put

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}(a):=\bigcap_{S^{\prime}\in\Lambda}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S^{\prime}}^{-a_{S^{\prime}}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}\subset IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}.

Proposition 3.28
.

(1)

(local vanishing) When
\dim\sigma_{S_{0}}=0
, for
q\leq d-1
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{-p-q}(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}|_{\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{0}\cap W_{L}}(a))

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathscr{H}^{-p-q}(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}|_{\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{0}\cap W_{L}}(a(S_{0}=m_{p,S_{0}}+p+q)))&(\dim S_{0}+2\leq q\leq a_{S_{0}}-m_{p,S_{0}}-p)\\
0&(q\leq\dim S_{0}+1)\end{cases}.

(2)

(local vanishing) When
\dim\sigma_{S_{0}}\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{-p-q}(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}|_{\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{0}\cap W_{L}}(a))

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathscr{H}^{-p-q}(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}|_{\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{0}\cap W_{L}}(a(S_{0}=m_{p,S_{0}}+p+q)))&(\dim S_{0}+1\leq q\leq a_{S_{0}}-m_{p,S_{0}}-p)\\
0&(q\leq\dim S_{0})\end{cases}.

(3)

(attaching property) When
\dim\sigma_{S_{0}}=0
, the natural map

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}(a)|_{W_{L}}\to Ri_{S*}i_{S}^{*}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}(a)|_{W_{L}}

induces an isomorphism

\mathscr{H}^{m}(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}(a)|_{\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{0}\cap W_{L}})\cong\mathscr{H}^{m}((Ri_{S*}i_{S}^{*}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}(a))|_{\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{0}\cap W_{L}})

for

\displaystyle m\leq\max\left\{-p-d,\min\left\{m_{p,S}-a_{S},-p-\dim S-2\right\}\right\}.

Proof.

For
c=(c_{S_{L,i}})_{S_{L,i}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}
,
we put
(c,(0))\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}
the element whose
S_{L,i}
-component
(
1\leq i\leq r(L)
) is
c_{S_{L,i}}
and the other components are

0
. For
b\in\mathbb{Z}^{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}
, we put

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits_{b}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}|_{W_{L}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}((b,(0)))|_{W_{L}}/\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}\\
b^{\prime}\lneq b\end{subarray}}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}((b^{\prime},(0)))|_{W_{L}},

where
b^{\prime}\lneq b
means
b_{S_{L,i}}^{\prime}\leq b_{S_{L,i}}
for any
1\leq i\leq r(L)

and the equality does not hold for some
1\leq i\leq r(L)
. For each

P\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d}
containing
S_{0}
, we have a sequence

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}\sigma_{S_{L,r(L)}}\subseteq\dots.\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}\sigma_{S_{L,1}}

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\sigma_{S_{L,1}}}^{-1}(\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}S_{L,1})\subseteq\dots\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\sigma_{S_{L,r(L)}}}^{-1}(\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}S_{L,r(L)})

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}P.

We fix an inner product of
N\otimes\mathbb{Q}
. Then the above sequence
gives an orthogonal decomposition of
\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}P
. It gives a decomposition
of
F_{p,w}(P)
, and these decompositions for all such
P
gives
a decomposition

IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}|_{W_{L}}\cong\bigoplus_{b\in\mathbb{Z}^{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}}\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits_{b}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}|_{W_{L}},

which induces

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S_{0}}^{k}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}|_{W_{L}}\cong\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}^{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}\\
b_{S_{L,r(L)}}\geq-k\end{subarray}}\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits_{b}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}|_{W_{L}}.

Our proofs of Proposition
3.17
, Proposition

3.20
, and Proposition
3.22

just use mapping cyclinders,
F_{p,w}
-linearly, hence they also
work for each
\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits_{b}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}|_{W_{L}}\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S_{0}}^{k}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}|_{W_{L}}
.
Hence the assertions hold.
∎

Remark 3.29
.

By Proposition
6.3
,
attaching property (
5.16
) also holds for
S_{0}
with

\dim\sigma_{S_{0}}\geq 1
.

4.
Derived categories of locally graded sheaves

As in Goresky-MacPherson’s intersection complex
[
GM83
]
,
the tropical intersection complex has a sheaf-theoretic characterization
(Section
5
and Subsection
6.1
)
using a kind of truncation functors. As we have seen in Subsection

3.2
, our truncation functors should involve
filtrations (or we should say grades). Hence we use derived categories
of sheaves of graded modules.

However, there is a techinical problem. Later (Theorem
5.26
,
Proposition
6.3
), we will prove
that
D(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{p,*})
also satisfies
the sheaf-theoretic characterization, and hence prove Poincaré-Verdier
duality. For each stratum
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S
(
S\in\Lambda
), the filtration

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{k}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{p,*}
(
k\geq 1
) was extended by

0
from an open neighborhood
W_{S}
of
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S
. Of course,
for
x\notin\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S
, the stalk of
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{k}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{p,*}
(
k\geq 1
)
at
x
is
0
, but the costalk can be non-zero, i.e.,

j_{x}^{*}(D(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{k}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{p,*}))\cong D(j_{x}^{!}(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{k}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{p,*}))

can be non-zero, where
j_{x}\colon\{x\}\to X
. Hence
D(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{k}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{p,*})

may not be quasi-isomorphic to
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{k}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}
.
For this reasons, in this section, we introduce localizations of categories,

(derived categories of) locally graded sheaves,
and
D(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{p,*})

is isomorphic to
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}
in
the localized category. (Some problems which may happen due to the
non-existence of canonical choices of
W_{S}
are also solved by
these localizations.)

In the first three subsections, we will give a quick introduction
to (multi)graded modules, their sheaves, and derived categories. Six
functors are discussed in Section
8
. In Subsection

4.4
, we discuss LG-(quasi-)isomorphisms
of (complexes of) graded sheaves (where LG stands for “locally graded”).
In Subsection
4.5
,
we impose a finiteness condition on grades. In Subsection
4.6
,
we study the truncation functors which involve local grades.

Note that while we use categories of graded modules as a framework,
probably, categories of filtered modules also work. We use the former
simply because it seems much simpler, and is enough for our purpose.
There are many references of sheaves of filtered and graded modules,
e.g.,
[
Ill71
]
,
[
Lau83
]
.
Many parts of this section and Section
8
about
derived categories of sheaves of (non-locally) graded modules must
be contained in some existing literature.

In this section, let
k
be a noetherian commutative ring of finite
global dimension with unit. We put
\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k
the abelian category
of
k
-modules.

4.1.
The abelian category of graded modules

Let
I
be a finite set. For
a=(a_{i})_{i\in I}
and
b=(b_{i})_{i\in I}\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
,
we put
a\leq b
if and only if
a_{i}\leq b_{i}
for any
i
.
We consider
\mathbb{Z}^{I}
as a preordered abelian group. By abuse of notation,
we put
0:=(0,\dots,0)\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
.

Definition 4.1
.

Let
k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
be the ring of polynomials in
T_{i}
(
i\in I
)
over
k
.

(1)

A (
\mathbb{Z}^{I}
-)graded
k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
-module
A
is a
k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
-module
equipped with a decomposition
A=\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}}A(a)
into

k
-modules
A(a)
such that multiplications
T_{i}
are the direct
sums of
T_{i}\colon A(a)\to A(a+e_{i})
(
i\in I,a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
),
where
e_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
is the element whose
i
-component is
1

and other components are
0
.

(2)

We put
\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
the category of graded
k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
-modules
with grade-preserving morphisms, i.e.,

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}}(A,B):=\left\{\varphi\in\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}}(A,B)\mid\varphi(A(a))\subset B(a)(a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I})\right\}.

We also put
\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}^{I},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)
the functor category from the
preordered set
\mathbb{Z}^{I}
to the abelian category
\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k
. Then
we have a natural isomorphism

\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}^{I},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)\cong\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}

given by

F\mapsto\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}}F(a)

and

T_{i}:=F(a\to a+e_{i})\colon F(a)\to F(a+e_{i})

(
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
,
i\in I
). We identify both hand sides.

Remark 4.2
.

The functor category
\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}^{I},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)
is an abelian category

[
Sch72
, Proposition 12.1.6]
admitting small direct
sums and products
[
Pop73
, Section 3.4, Theorem 4.1 and Section 2.8]
.
Kernels, images, limits, and colimits are computed in a grade-wise
manner. Explicitly,

•

for a morphism
f\in\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}}(A,B)
, we have

(\mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}\nolimits f)(a)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}\nolimits f\cap A(a)
and
(\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits f)(a)=f(A(a))
, and

•

for
A_{j}=\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}}A_{j}(a)\in\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
,
we have
(\bigoplus_{j}A_{j})(a)=\bigoplus_{j}(A_{j}(a))
and
(\prod_{j}A_{j})(a)=\prod_{j}(A_{j}(a))
.

Definition 4.3
.

Let
A=\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}}A(a)
and
B=\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}}B(a)\in\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
.
Let
A\otimes_{k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}}B
denote their non-graded tensor
product. We equip it with the structure of graded
k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
-module
by

(A\otimes_{k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}}B)(a)=\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits(\bigoplus_{a_{1}+a_{2}=a}A(a_{1})\otimes_{k}B(a_{2})\to A\otimes_{k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}}B).

By abuse of notation, let
A\otimes_{k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}}B
also denote
this graded tensor product.

For a
k
-module
A
and a subset
\Omega\subset\mathbb{Z}^{I}
, we define

\bigoplus_{a\in\Omega}A\in\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
by

(\bigoplus_{a\in\Omega}A)(b):=\begin{cases}A&(b\in\Omega)\\
0&(b\not\in\Omega)\end{cases}

(
b\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
), the identity map
A\to A
, and the zero maps
0\to A

and
A\to 0
. For
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
, we put
\mathbb{Z}_{*a}^{I}:=\left\{b\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}\mid b*a\right\}

(
*\in\{\geq,\leq\}
). By abuse of notation, we put
k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}:=\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I}}k
.
We have
B\otimes_{k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}}k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}\cong B
(
B\in\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
)
as graded
k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
-modules.

Definition 4.4
.

A graded
k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
-module
M
is flat if for any exact
sequence

0\to N_{1}\to N_{2}\to N_{3}\to 0

in
\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
, a sequence

0\to N_{1}\otimes M\to N_{2}\otimes M\to N_{3}\otimes M\to 0

is also exact.

Definition 4.5
.

Let
A=\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}}A(a)\in\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}

and
b\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
.

(1)

We define
A[b]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}\in\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
by
A[b]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}(a):=A(a+b)

and natural morphisms
T_{i}\colon A(a+b)\to A(a+b+e_{i})
.

(2)

We also put

\mathop{\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(A,B)(b):=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}}(A,B[b]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}).

In particular, we put

\mathop{\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(A,B)(0):=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}}(A,B).

(3)

We put

T_{i}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(A,B)(b)\to\mathop{\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(A,B)(b+e_{i})

the map given by multiplying
T_{i}
, and define

\mathop{\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(A,B):=\bigoplus_{b\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}}\mathop{\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(A,B)(b)\in\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}.

Remark 4.6
.

The functor
-[b]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}
(
b\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
) is an exact functor. We
have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(k[T_{i}]_{i\in I},B)(b)

\displaystyle=B(b)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k,

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(k[T_{i}]_{i\in I},B)

\displaystyle=B\in\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}.

Example 4.7
.

We assume
I=\left\{\mathop{\mathrm{pt}}\nolimits\right\}
.
Let
A=\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}^{\left\{\mathop{\mathrm{pt}}\nolimits\right\}}}A(a)\in\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{\mathop{\mathrm{pt}}\nolimits}]
.
Then for
b\in\mathbb{Z}^{\left\{\mathop{\mathrm{pt}}\nolimits\right\}}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(A,k[T_{\mathop{\mathrm{pt}}\nolimits}])(b)\cong

\displaystyle\left\{f\in\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(\lim_{a\to\infty}A(a),k)\mid f(A(-b-1))=0\right\}.

In particular, for
a_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\left\{\mathop{\mathrm{pt}}\nolimits\right\}}
,

•

when
A(a)=A(a_{0})
for
a\geq a_{0}
, we have
\mathop{\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(A,k[T_{\mathop{\mathrm{pt}}\nolimits}])(b)=0

for
b\leq-a_{0}-1
, and

•

conversely, when
A(a)=0
for
a\leq a_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(A,k[T_{\mathop{\mathrm{pt}}\nolimits}])(b)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(\lim_{a\to\infty}A(a),k)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(A,k[T_{\mathop{\mathrm{pt}}\nolimits}])(-a_{0}-1)

for
b\geq-a_{0}-1
.

This kind of duality is used in proof of Poincaré-Verdier duality
in Subsection
5.2
.

We shall also define external tensor product (Definition
4.13
)
which is used to prove Künneth formula (Proposition
6.2
).
Let
J
be a finite set. We put
\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}\colon I\times J\to I
the
projection.

Definition 4.8
.

We define
\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{I\times J}\to\mathbb{Z}^{I}
by
\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}(b){}_{i}:=\min_{j\in J}\left\{b_{(i,j)}\right\}

(
i\in I
) for
b=(b_{(i,j)})_{(i,j)\in I\times J}
, and define

\iota_{I}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{I}\to\mathbb{Z}^{I\times J}
by
\iota_{I}(a)_{(i,j)}:=a_{i}

(
(i,j)\in I\times J
) for
a=(a_{i})_{i\in I}
.

Remark 4.9
.

For
b\in\mathbb{Z}^{I\times J}
,
we have
b\geq\iota_{I}(\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}(b))
and

\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}(b)=\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}(\iota_{I}(\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}(b))).

Definition 4.10
.

Let
A\in\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
,
B\in\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{(i,j)}]_{(i,j)\in I\times J}
.

•

We define
\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}^{*}A\in\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{(i,j)}]_{(i,j)\in I\times J}

by

\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}^{*}A(b):=A(\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}(b))

(
b\in\mathbb{Z}^{I\times J}
) with natural morphisms.

•

We define
\iota_{I}^{*}B\in\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
by

\iota_{I}^{*}B(a):=B(\iota_{I}(a))

(
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
) with natural morphisms.

Obviously, the functors
\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}^{*}
and
\iota_{I}^{*}
are
exact functors.

Lemma 4.11
.

Let
A\in\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}

and
B\in\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{(i,j)}]_{(i,j)\in I\times J}
. Then we have a
natural isomorphism

(\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}^{*}A\otimes B)(b^{\circ}+\iota_{I}(a^{\circ}))\cong(A\otimes\iota_{I}^{*}(B[b^{\circ}]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}))(a^{\circ})

(
a^{\circ}\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
,
b^{\circ}\in\mathbb{Z}^{I\times J}
).

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle(\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}^{*}A\otimes B)(b^{\circ}+\iota_{I}(a^{\circ}))

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}^{I\times J}}\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}^{*}A(b)\otimes B(b^{\circ}+\iota_{I}(a^{\circ})-b)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}^{I\times J}}\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}^{*}A(\iota_{I}(\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}(b)))\otimes B(b^{\circ}+\iota_{I}(a^{\circ})-\iota_{I}(\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}(b)))

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}}\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}^{*}A(\iota_{I}(a))\otimes B(b^{\circ}+\iota_{I}(a^{\circ})-\iota_{I}(a))

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}}A(a)\otimes\iota_{I}^{*}(B[b^{\circ}]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits})(a^{\circ}-a)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle(A\otimes\iota_{I}^{*}(B[b^{\circ}]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}))(a^{\circ}),

where the second isomorphism follows from Remark
4.9
.
∎

Corollary 4.12
.

The functors
\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}^{*}

and
\iota_{I}^{*}
send flat modules to flat modules.

Proof.

This immediately follows from Lemma
4.11
.
∎

Corollary
4.12
is used to define external
tensor products for derived categories of sheaves of graded modules
(Subsection
4.3
).

Definition 4.13
.

For
A_{I}\in\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}

and
A_{J}\in\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{j}]_{j\in J}
, we put

A_{I}\XBox A_{J}:=\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}^{*}A_{I}\otimes\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{J}^{*}A_{J}\in\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{(i,j)}]_{(i,j)\in I\times J},

the
external tensor product
.

4.2.
Sheaves of graded modules

Let
X
be a topological space.

Remark 4.14
.

The abelian category
\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}

sastisfies nice properties. It has a generator
\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}}k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}[a]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}\in\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
.
By
[
Pop73
, Section 3.4, Theorem 4.1 and Section 2.8]
,
filtered inductive limits in
\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
preserve exact
sequences. Consequently, by
[
Sch72
, Theorem 15.3.7]
,
every object in
\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
has an injective envelope
. By
[
Pop73
, Section 3.4, Theorem 4.1 and Section 2.8]
,
the locally small category
\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
also satisfies
the following: for any object
A
and any set
J
such that for
each
j\in J
, a dierct set
\{A_{j(i)}\}_{i\in I_{j}}
of subobjects
of
A
is given, we have

\bigcap_{j\in J}(\sum_{i\in I_{j}}A_{j(i)})=\sum_{(j(i))\in\prod_{j\in J}I_{j}}A_{j(i)}.

We put
\mathop{\mathrm{Psh}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
the abelian category of
presheaves of graded
k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
-modules. By Remark
4.14
,
we have the abelian category of sheaves
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

with enough injectives (
[
Pop73
, Section 4.7]
).
(See also
[
KS06
, Chapter 17]

for sheaf theory.) It is the full subcategory of
\mathop{\mathrm{Psh}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

consisting of presheaves
\mathscr{F}
satisfying the usual axiom:

0\to\mathscr{F}(U)\to\prod_{i\in I}\mathscr{F}(U_{i})\to\prod_{i,j\in I}\mathscr{F}(U_{i}\cap U_{j})

is exact for any open subset
U\subset X
and open covering
\left\{U_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}

of
U
. We also have the natural sheafification functor

\mathop{\mathrm{Psh}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\to\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}).

We obviously have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Psh}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}^{I},\mathop{\mathrm{Psh}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)),

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}^{I},\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)).

In particular, we can compute stalks, kernels, images, colimits and
sheafifications in a grade-wise manner, and for
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
, we
have an exact functor

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

\displaystyle\to\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle\mapsto F(a).

When
X
is a locally closed polyhedral subset (Definition
2.3
)
of a tropical toric variety, we put

\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{c}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}).

the full abelian subcategory of sheaves constructible (Definition

8.21
) with respect to some polyhedral
stratifications.

4.3.
Derived categories of sheaves of graded modules

Let
X
and
Y
be locally closed polyhedral subsets (Definition

2.3
) of tropical toric varieties,
and
f\colon X\to Y
a polyhedrally stratified map (Definition
2.4
).
Let

D(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}):=D(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}))

be the derived category of complexes of sheaves of
\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
-modules,
and
D^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
(resp.
D_{c}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
)
its full triangulated subcategory of complexes quasi-isomorphic to
bounded complexes (resp. bounded complexes constructible (Definition

8.22
) with respect to some
polyhedral stratifications).

We assume that
Rf_{*},Rf_{!}\colon D^{+}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)\to D^{+}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)

have finite cohomological dimensions, and that for any polyhedral
stratification of
X
, there is a refinement of it such that
f^{-1}(y)

(
y\in Y
) is compactifiable (Definition
8.30
)
with respect to the restriction of the stratification. Then we have
six functors

\displaystyle f_{*}

\displaystyle\colon D_{c}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\to D_{c}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

\displaystyle f^{*}

\displaystyle\colon D_{c}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\to D_{c}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

\displaystyle f_{!}

\displaystyle\colon D_{c}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\to D_{c}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

\displaystyle f^{!}

\displaystyle\colon D_{c}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\to D_{c}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{L}}{\otimes}

\displaystyle\colon D_{c}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\times D_{c}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\to D_{c}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{R}gr\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits

\displaystyle\colon D_{c}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\times D_{c}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\to D_{c}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

(see Section
8
for details). They satisfies the
usual compatibility.

Example 4.15
.

We have
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}\in D_{c}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

(Subsection
3.1
) for a tropical variety
X=(\Lambda,w)

regular at infinity.

Remark 4.16
.

For an abelian category
\mathcal{A}
, we put
C(\mathcal{A})
the
category of (cochain) complexes. We have

\displaystyle C(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}))

\displaystyle=C(\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}^{I},\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)))

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}^{I},C(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k))).

i.e., a complex
A\in C(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}))
of sheaves

A^{j}=\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}}A^{j}(a)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

(
j\in\mathbb{Z}
) can be also considered as

A=\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}}A(a)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}^{I},C(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k))),

where we put
A(a):=(A^{j}(a))_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\in C(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k))
. We
use this notation freely. For
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
, we have a functor

\displaystyle D_{c}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

\displaystyle\to D_{c}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle\mapsto F(a).

Notation 4.17
.

Let
A\in D(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
.
We put

•

\mathscr{H}^{j}(A)\in C(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}))
is the sheaf of

j
-th cohomology group,

•

A[n]_{\deg}\in D(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
the usual shift given
by
(A[n]_{\deg})^{j}=A^{j+n}
and

d_{A[n]_{\deg}}:=(-1)^{n}d_{A}\colon(A[n]_{\deg})^{j}\to(A[n]_{\deg})^{j+1},

•

A[b]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}\in D(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
the shift of grading
given by
A[b]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}(a):=A(a+b)
, and

•

\tau_{\leq p}A
and
\tau^{\geq p}A
the usual truncations given
by

\displaystyle\tau_{\leq p}A^{j}

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}0&(j\geq p+1)\\
\mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}\nolimits(d\colon A^{p}\to A^{p+1})&(j=p)\\
A^{j}&(j\leq p-1)\end{cases}

\displaystyle\tau^{\geq p}A^{j}

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}A^{j}&(j\geq p+1)\\
A^{p}/\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits(d\colon A^{p-1}\to A^{p})&(j=p)\\
0&(j\leq p-1)\end{cases}.

Let
J
be also a finite set. For
A\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
,
we put

\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}^{*}A\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{(i,j)}]_{(i,j)\in I\times J})

the sheaf given by

U\mapsto\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}^{*}(A(U))

(Definition
4.10
). Since
\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}^{*}

is exact, it defines a functor

\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}^{*}\colon D^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\to D^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{(i,j)}]_{(i,j)\in I\times J}).

Remind that
\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}^{*}
sends flat modules to flat modules
(Corollary
4.12
). For
A_{I}\in D^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

and
A_{J}\in D^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{j}]_{j\in J})
, using flat resolutions
(Remark
8.7
), we define the

external tensor product
by

A_{I}\overset{L}{\XBox}A_{J}:=\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}^{*}\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{X}^{*}A_{I}\overset{\mathrm{L}}{\otimes}\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{J}^{*}\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{Y}^{*}A_{J}\in D^{b}(X\times Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{(i,j)}]_{(i,j)\in I\times J}),

where
\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{Z}\colon X\times Y\to Z
(
Z=X,Y
) is the projection.

Lemma 4.18
.

We have

\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}^{*}\circ D_{X}=D_{X}\circ\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}^{*}\colon D^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\to D^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{(i,j)}]_{(i,j)\in I\times J}).

Proof.

Let
B\in D^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
be a complex as in Lemma

8.18
. Then
\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}^{*}B\in D^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{(i,j)}]_{(i,j)\in I\times J})

also satisfies the condition in Lemma
8.18
.
By Lemma
8.18
, for
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I\times J}
,
we have

\displaystyle\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}^{*}(D_{X}(B))(a)=

\displaystyle D_{X}(B)(\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}(a))

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathop{gr\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(B,\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I}\end{subarray}}\omega_{X})(\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}(a))

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathop{gr\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}^{*}B,\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}c\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I\times J}\end{subarray}}\omega_{X})(a)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle D(\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{I}^{*}B)(a),

where
\omega_{X}\in D^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)
is the injective dualizing
complex. Since every complex is quasi-isomorphic to such a
B
, the
assertion holds.
∎

4.4.
Derived categories of locally graded sheaves

In this subsection, we discuss localizations
D_{\epsilon,LG}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

(
\epsilon=\emptyset,c
) of derived categories of sheaves of graded
modules by
LG-quasi-isomorphisms
(Definition
4.20
),
which are used in our sheaf-theoretic study of tropical intersection
homology groups. To prove a basic property (Corollary
4.59
)
of our truncation functor (Definition
4.48
) in the
next subsection, we also show that
D_{\epsilon,LG}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

is equivalent to the derived category of its heart, which is equivalent
to localizations
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,LG}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

of abelian categories of sheaves of graded modules by
LG-isomorphisms

(Definition
4.33
).

In this subsection, let
X=(\Lambda,w)
be a tropical variety, and

Y\subset X
a locally closed polyhedral subset (Definition
2.3
).

Definition 4.19
.

A pair
(L,a)
of a sequenece
L=(S_{L,1}\subsetneq\dots\subsetneq S_{L,r(L)})

(
S_{L,i}\in\Lambda
,
r(L)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
) and
a=(a_{S})_{S\in\Lambda}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}

is called an
adapted
pair if
a_{S}\geq 0
for
S\in\Lambda\setminus\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}
.

Definition 4.20
.

Let
A,B\in D_{\epsilon}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

(
\epsilon=\emptyset,c
). A morphism
\varphi\colon A\to B
in
D_{\epsilon}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

is an
LG-quasi-isomorphism
(where LG stands for “locally graded”)
if there are open polyhedral neighborhoods
W_{S}^{\prime}\subset X
of
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S

(
S\in\Lambda
) such that for an adapted pair
(L,a)
, the morphism

\varphi
induces a quasi-isomorphism

A|_{Y\cap W^{\prime}_{L}}(a)\cong B|_{Y\cap W^{\prime}_{L}}(a),

where we put
W^{\prime}_{L}:=\bigcap_{i=1}^{r(L)}W_{S_{L,i}}^{\prime}
We denote
LG-quasi-isomorphisms by
\cong_{LG}
.

Remark 4.21
.

In Definition
4.20
, for an adapted pair
(L,a)

with
r(L)=0
(i.e.,
\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}=\emptyset
),
we define the condition as
\varphi(a)\colon A(a)\cong B(a)
on
Y
.

Remark 4.22
.

For
S_{1},S_{2}\in\Lambda

with
S_{1}\not\subset S_{2}
and
S_{1}\not\supset S_{2}
, there
exist open polyhedral neighborhoods
W_{S_{i}}^{\prime}\subset X
of
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{i}

(
i=1,2
) such that
W_{S_{1}}^{\prime}\cap W_{S_{2}}^{\prime}=\emptyset
. Hence
the condition in Definition
4.20
can be written
as follows: there are open polyhedral neighborhoods
W_{S}^{\prime}\subset X

of
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S
(
S\in\Lambda
) such that for any
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}
,
the morphism
\varphi
induces a quasi-isomorphism

A|_{Y\cap\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}S\in\Lambda\\
a_{S}\leq-1\end{subarray}}{\bigcap}W_{S}^{\prime}}(a)\cong B|_{Y\cap\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}S\in\Lambda\\
a_{S}\leq-1\end{subarray}}{\bigcap}W_{S}^{\prime}}(a).

We introduce a usuful functor.

Definition 4.23
.

Let
i\colon V\hookrightarrow Y

be an open polyhedral subset. Let
A\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

(
\epsilon=\emptyset,c
) and
S_{0}\in\Lambda
. We define
(i_{!}i^{*})_{S_{0}}A\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

by

(i_{!}i^{*})_{S_{0}}A(a):=\begin{cases}A(a)&(a_{S_{0}}\geq 0)\\
i_{!}i^{*}A(a)&(a_{S_{0}}\leq-1)\end{cases}

(
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}
) and natural morphisms.

Remark 4.24
.

The functor
(i_{!}i^{*})_{S_{0}}
is exact, hence it induces a functor

(i_{!}i^{*})_{S_{0}}
on the derived category. Note that we have
a natural morphism
(i_{!}i^{*})_{S_{0}}A\to A
.

Definition 4.25
.

Let
A\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

(
\epsilon=\emptyset,c
), and
W_{S}^{\prime}\subset X
open polyhedral
neighborhoods of
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S
(
S\in\Lambda
). We put

\displaystyle(i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}A

\displaystyle:=(i_{W_{S_{\#\Lambda}}^{\prime}\cap Y!}i_{W_{S_{\#\Lambda}}^{\prime}\cap Y}^{*})_{S^{\#\Lambda}}\dots(i_{W_{S_{1}}^{\prime}\cap Y!}i_{W_{S_{1}}^{\prime}\cap Y}^{*})_{S_{1}}A

\displaystyle\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda}),

where
i_{W_{S}^{\prime}\cap Y}\colon W_{S}^{\prime}\cap Y\hookrightarrow Y
(
S\in\Lambda
)
is an open immersion, and we fix an ordering
\Lambda=\left\{S^{1},\dots,S^{\#\Lambda}\right\}
.

Remark 4.26
.

The functor
(i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}

is exact, hence it induces a functor
(i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}

on the derived category. Note that we have a natural morphism
(i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}A\to A
,
and for a morphism
A\to B
in
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

or
D_{\epsilon}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})
(
\epsilon=\emptyset,c
),
we have a commutative diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 35.11195pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.75pt\raise 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{B}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-35.11195pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 94.5173pt\raise-5.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Remark 4.27
.

Let
\varphi\colon A\to B
be a
morphism in
D_{\epsilon}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})
(
\epsilon=\emptyset,c
),
and
W_{S}^{\prime}\subset X
open polyhedral neighborhoods of
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S

(
S\in\Lambda
).

•

The natural morphism
(i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}A\to A

is an LG-quasi-isomorphism.

•

When
\varphi\colon A\to B
is an LG-quasi-isomorphism, the morphism

(i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}\varphi\colon(i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}A\to(i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}B

is also an LG-quasi-isomorphism. Moreover, in this case, for sufficiently
small
W_{S}^{\prime}
(
S\in\Lambda
), the morphism
(i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}\varphi

is a quasi-isomorphism.

The system
S_{\epsilon,LGq}
(
\epsilon=\emptyset,c
) of all LG-quasi-isomorphisms
in
D_{\epsilon}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})
is the union
of the systems
S_{\epsilon,LGq,W^{\prime}}
(with respect to
W^{\prime}=(W_{S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}
)
arising from cohomological functors

\displaystyle\prod_{L=(S_{L,1}\subsetneq\dots\subsetneq S_{L,r(L)})}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}\\
a_{S}\geq 0\ (S\in\Lambda\setminus\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)})\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathscr{H}^{j}(-|_{Y\cap W^{\prime}_{L}}(a))

\displaystyle\colon

\displaystyle D_{\epsilon}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})\to\prod_{L=(S_{L,1}\subsetneq\dots\subsetneq S_{L,r(L)})}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}\\
a_{S}\geq 0\ (S\in\Lambda\setminus\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)})\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon}(Y\cap W^{\prime}_{L},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k).

The systems
S_{\epsilon,LGq,W^{\prime}}
are multiplicative, and their localizations
are triangulated (
[
Wei94
, Proposition 10.4.1]
).
The proof ([loc.cit.]) of this fact shows that the system
S_{\epsilon,LGq}

is also multiplicative, its localization
S_{\epsilon,LGq}^{-1}D_{\epsilon}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

is a triangulated category, and the canonical functor

D_{\epsilon}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})\to D_{\epsilon,LG}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda}):=S_{\epsilon,LGq}^{-1}D_{\epsilon}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

is a morphism of triangulated categories.

Remark 4.28
.

A priori, the localization
D_{\epsilon,LG}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

exists in a larger universe, but in fact it exists in our universe.
This can be proven in the almost same way as the case of the system
of quasi-isomorphisms (
[
Wei94
, Proposition 10.4.4]
),
using Remark
4.26
. We omit details.

Remark 4.29
.

By Remark
4.27
, for a complex
A\in D_{c}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})
,
a complex
B\in D^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})
and an LG-quasi-isomorphism

\varphi\colon B\to A
, the complex
(i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}B

is in
D_{c}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})
for some
(W_{S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}
.
Since the composition
(i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}B\to B\to^{\varphi}A

is also an LG-quasi-isomorphism, by
[
Wei94
, Lemma 10.3.13.2]
,
the full triangulated subcategory
D_{c}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

of
D^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})
is localizing for
S_{LGq}
,
and

S_{c,LGq}=S_{LGq}\cap D_{c}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda}).

Remark 4.30
.

For a locally closed polyhedral
subset
h\colon Z\hookrightarrow Y
, since functors
Rh_{*},h^{*},Rh_{!},h^{!}

are given in a grade-wise manner (Remark
8.2

and Definition
8.10
), they preserve LG-quasi-isomorphisms,
and hence induce functors on the localizations.

The usual truncation functors
\tau_{\leq r}
and
\tau^{\geq r}

(
r\in\mathbb{Z}
) on
D_{\epsilon}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

(
\epsilon=\emptyset,c
) induce truncation functors
\tau_{\leq r}

and
\tau^{\geq r}
on
D_{\epsilon,LG}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})
.

Definition 4.31
.

We put

D_{\epsilon,LG}^{b,?r}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})\subset D_{\epsilon,LG}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

(
\epsilon=\emptyset,c
and
?=\leq,\geq
) the essentail image of

D_{\epsilon}^{b,?r}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})
under the natural
functor. This is a t-structure on
D_{\epsilon,LG}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})
.
This easily follows from the following Lemma
4.32
.

Lemma 4.32
.

For
A\in D_{\epsilon,LG}^{b,\leq-1}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

and
B\in D_{\epsilon,LG}^{b,\geq 0}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})
,
we have

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{D_{\epsilon,LG}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})}(A,B)=0.

Proof.

Let
\varphi\colon C\to A
be an LG-quasi-isomorphism in
D_{\epsilon}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})
.
Then the composition
\tau_{\leq-1}C\to C\to^{\varphi}A
is also
an LG-quasi-isomorphism. Since

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{D_{\epsilon}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})}(\tau_{\leq-1}C,B)=0,

the assertion holds.
∎

We put

\mathscr{H}(D_{\epsilon,LG}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})):=D_{\epsilon,LG}^{b,\leq 0}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})\cap D_{\epsilon,LG}^{b,\geq 0}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

the heart, which is an abelian category.

We shall give a description of
\mathscr{H}(D_{\epsilon,LG}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda}))

(up to equivalences of categories), and shall show that
D_{\epsilon,LG}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

is equivalent to its bounded derived category.

Definition 4.33
.

Let
A,B\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

(
\epsilon=\emptyset,c
). A morphism
\varphi\colon A\to B
in
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

is an
LG-isomorphism
(where LG stands for “locally graded”)
if there are open polyhedral neighborhoods
W_{S}^{\prime}\subset X
of
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S

(
S\in\Lambda
) such that for an adapted pair
(L,a)
, the morphism

\varphi
induces an isomorphism

A|_{Y\cap W^{\prime}_{L}}(a)\cong B|_{Y\cap W^{\prime}_{L}}(a).

We denote LG-isomorphisms by
\cong_{LG}
.

Remark 4.34
.

Similarly to Remark
4.22
,
the condition in Definition
4.33
can be written
as follows: there are open polyhedral neighborhoods
W_{S}^{\prime}\subset X

of
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S
(
S\in\Lambda
) such that for any
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}
,
the morphism
\varphi
induces an isomorphism

A|_{Y\cap\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}S\in\Lambda\\
a_{S}\leq-1\end{subarray}}{\bigcap}W_{S}^{\prime}}(a)\cong B|_{Y\cap\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}S\in\Lambda\\
a_{S}\leq-1\end{subarray}}{\bigcap}W_{S}^{\prime}}(a).

Remark 4.35
.

Let
\varphi\colon A\to B

be a morphism in
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

(
\epsilon=\emptyset,c
), and
W_{S}^{\prime}\subset X
open polyhedral
neighborhoods of
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S
(
S\in\Lambda
).

•

The natural morphism
(i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}A\to A

is an LG-isomorphism.

•

When
\varphi\colon A\to B
is an LG-isomorphism, the morphism

(i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}\varphi\colon(i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}A\to(i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}B

is also an LG-isomorphism. Moreover, in this case, for sufficiently
small
W_{S}^{\prime}
(
S\in\Lambda
), the morphism
(i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}\varphi

is an isomorphism.

We put
\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon,LG}\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

(
\epsilon=\emptyset,c
) the abelian full subcategory of objects

A
LG-isomorphic to
0
. This is a Serre subcategory (i.e., closed
under subobjects, quotients, and extensions). The system
S_{\epsilon,LG}

of all LG-isomorphisms in
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

(
\epsilon=\emptyset,c
) is the set of morphisms whose kernels and
cokernels are in
\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon,LG}
. Hence
S_{\epsilon,LG}
is
a multiplicative system (
[
Wei94
, Exercise 10.3.2.1]
).
It is locally small by Remark
4.35
,
hence by
[
Wei94
, Lemma 10.3.13.2]
,
the localization

\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,LG}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda}):=S_{\epsilon,LG}^{-1}\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

exists in our universe. It is abelian and the natural functor

\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})\to\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,LG}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

is exact (
[
Wei94
, Exercise 10.3.2.4]
).

Remark 4.36
.

By Remark
4.35
,
every morphism
\varphi\colon A\to B
in
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,LG}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

is given by a morphism

f\colon(i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}A\to(i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}B

in
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})
(and natural
LG-isomorphisms
(i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}C\to C
(
C=A,B
))
for some open polyhedral neighborhoods
W_{S}^{\prime}\subset X
of
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S

(
S\in\Lambda
). Moreover, the morphism
f
is unique up to the
choice of
(W_{S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}
.

For
r\in\mathbb{Z}
, we put

\mathscr{H}^{r}:=\tau^{\geq r}\tau_{\leq r}\colon D_{\epsilon,LG}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})\to\mathscr{H}(D_{\epsilon,LG}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})).

Remark 4.37
.

Obviously, the functor
\mathscr{H}^{r}
induces
a functor

\mathscr{H}^{r}\colon D_{\epsilon,LG}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})\to\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,LG}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda}).

It is easy to see that

\mathscr{H}^{0}\colon\mathscr{H}(D_{\epsilon,LG}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda}))\to\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,LG}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

is an equivalence of category. A natural inclusion

\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,LG}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})\to\mathscr{H}(D_{\epsilon,LG}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda}))

is a quasi-inverse of it.

Lemma 4.38
.

For
\epsilon=\emptyset,c
, there
is an equivalence

D^{b}(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,LG}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda}))\to D_{\epsilon,LG}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

of categories which is t-exact and triangulated.

Proof.

We construct a functor

\Phi\colon C^{b}(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,LG}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda}))\to D_{\epsilon,LG}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

as follows, where
C^{b}(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,LG}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda}))

is the category of bounded complexes. Let
A=(A^{j})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\in C^{b}(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,LG}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda}))
.
Since
A
is bounded, by Remark
4.36
,
there exist open polyhedral neighborhoods
W_{A,S}^{\prime}\subset X
of

\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S
(
S\in\Lambda
) such that the morphisms
d\colon A^{j}\to A^{j+1}

in
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,LG}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})
is given
by morphisms

(i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{A,S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}A^{j}\to(i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{A,S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}A^{j+1}

in
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})
. Hence

(i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{A,S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}A:=((i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{A,S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}A^{j})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}

is a complex in
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})
.
We fix such a
(W_{A,S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}
for each
A
, and put
\Phi(A):=(i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{A.S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}A
.
Let
A\to B
be a morphism in
C^{b}(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,LG}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda}))
.
Then by Remark
4.36
, we have a morphism

(i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{S}^{\prime\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}A\to(i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{S}^{\prime\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}B

in
C^{b}(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda}))
for
some open polyhedral neighborhoods
W_{S}^{\prime\prime}\subset X
of
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S

(
S\in\Lambda
). Since natural morphisms

(i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{S}^{\prime\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}C\to(i_{!}i^{*})_{(W_{C,S}^{\prime})_{S\in\Lambda}}C

(
C=A,B
) are LG-quasi-isomorphisms, the functor
\Phi
is well-defined.
By Remark
4.35
and similar reasoning,
we can see that
\Phi
induces the required equivalence of triangulated
categories.
∎

4.5.
A finiteness condition

In this subsection, we discuss certain finiteness condition on grade.
We also introduce
L
-local isomorphisms. Let
X=(\Lambda,w)
be
a tropical variety regular at infinity, and
Y\subset X
a locally
closed polyhedral subset. We put

\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X):=\sum_{S\in\Lambda}(\dim S+2\dim\sigma_{S})e_{S}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda},

where
e_{S}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}
is the element whose
S
-component
is
1
and other components are
0
. Let
I\subset\Lambda
be a
subset. We put
-|_{I}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}\to\mathbb{Z}^{I}
the projection.
Let
\epsilon=\emptyset,c
.

Definition 4.39
.

We put

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I},0]}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in I})

\displaystyle\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in I})

\displaystyle(\text{resp. }D_{\epsilon,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I},0]}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in I})

\displaystyle\subset D_{\epsilon}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in I}))

the full abelian (resp. triangulated) subcategory consisting of objects

E
such that

•

for each
S\in I
and
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
with
a_{S}\geq 0
, the morphism

E(a)\to E(a+e_{S})
is an isomorphism (resp. a quasi-isomorphism),
and

•

E(a)\cong 0
for
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
with
-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I}\not\leq a
.

Definition 4.40
.

Let
E

be a sheaf (resp. a complex of sheaves) in
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in I})
.
We put
E|_{[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I},0]}
the sheaf (resp. the complex of sheaves)
in
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in I})
such that

E|_{[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I},0]}(a):=\begin{cases}E((\min\left\{0,a_{S}\right\})_{S\in I})&(-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I}\leq a)\\
0&(-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I}\not\leq a)\end{cases}

(
a=(a_{S})_{S\in I}\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
) and the morphisms between
E|_{[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I},0]}(a)

(
a=(a_{S})_{S\in I}\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
) are the natural ones, where
(\min\left\{0,a_{S}\right\})_{S\in I}\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}

is the element whose
S
-component is
\min\left\{0,a_{S}\right\}
.

Remark 4.41
.

•

By definition, for
-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I}\leq a\leq 0
, we have
E|_{[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I},0]}(a)=E(a)
.

•

We have a natural morphism
E|_{[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I},0]}\to E
.

•

The functor

\displaystyle-|_{[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I},0]}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in I})

\displaystyle\to\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I},0]}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in I})

\displaystyle(\text{resp. }-|_{[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I},0]}\colon D_{\epsilon}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in I})

\displaystyle\to D_{\epsilon,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I},0]}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in I}))

is an exact functor (resp. a functor of triangulated categories),
whose restriction to
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I},0]}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in I})

(resp.
D_{\epsilon,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I},0]}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in I})
)
is an equivalence.

Let

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle\in K^{b}(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I},0]}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in I})),

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle\in K^{b}(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in I})),

and
B\to A
a quasi-isomorphism. Then the composition
B|_{[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I},0]}\to B\to A

is also a quasi-isomorphism. Hence by
[
Wei94
, Lemma 10.3.13.2]
,
the full triangulated subcategory

K^{b}(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I},0]}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in I}))\subset K^{b}(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in I}))

is a localizing for quasi-isomorphisms. The essential image of the
localization in
D_{\epsilon}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in I})
is

D_{\epsilon,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I},0]}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in I})
.

For

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle\in D_{\epsilon,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0]}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda}),

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle\in D_{\epsilon}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda}),

and an LG-quasi-isomorphism
B\to A
, the composition
B|_{[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0]}\to B\to A

is also an LG-quasi-isomorphism. Hence by
[
Wei94
, Lemma 10.3.13.2]
,
the full subcategory
D_{\epsilon,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0]}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

of
D_{\epsilon}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})
is localizing
for
S_{\epsilon,LGq}
. We put

D_{\epsilon,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

the localization. Similarly, the full subcategory
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0]}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

of the category
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

is localizing for
S_{\epsilon,LG}
. We put

\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

the localization.

Definition 4.42
.

Let
L=(S_{L,1}\subsetneq\dots\subsetneq S_{L,r(L)})

(
S_{L,i}\in\Lambda
,
r(L)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
) be a sequenece,
A,B\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)
.
A morphism
\varphi\colon A\to B
in
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)
is an
L
-local
isomorphism
if there are open polyhedral neighborhoods
W_{S_{L,i}}^{\prime}\subset X

of
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{L,i}
(
1\leq i\leq r(L)
) such that the morphism

\varphi
induces an isomorphism
A|_{Y\cap W^{\prime}_{L}}\cong B|_{Y\cap W^{\prime}_{L}}
,
where
W_{L}^{\prime}:=\bigcap_{i=1}^{r(L)}W_{S_{L,i}}^{\prime}
. We denote
L
-local
isomorphism by
\cong_{L}
.

Let
L=(S_{L,1}\subsetneq\dots\subsetneq S_{L,r(L)})
(
S_{L,i}\in\Lambda
,

r(L)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
) be a sequenece. Similarly to the system
S_{\epsilon,LG}

of all LG-isomorphisms (Subsection
4.4
),
the system
S_{\epsilon,L}
of all
L
-local isomorphism in
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)

is locally small, the localization

\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,L}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k):=S_{\epsilon,L}^{-1}\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)

exists in our universe, and is abelian. For
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}
adapted
to
L
, we have an exact functor

-(a)\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,LG}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})\to\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,L}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)

given by
F\mapsto F(a)
.

Remark 4.43
.

The functor

\bigoplus^{\prime}_{a\in[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0]}-(a)\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in I}))\to\bigoplus^{\prime}_{a\in[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0]}\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,L(a)}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)

is faithful, where
a
runs through elements in
[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0]
such
that the set of
S\in\Lambda
with
a_{S}\leq-1
forms a sequence

L(a)=(S_{L(a),1}\subsetneq\dots\subsetneq S_{L(a),r(L(a))})
(
r(L(a))\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
).

We put
\omega_{Y}\in D_{c}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)
the injective dualizing
complex.

Lemma 4.44
.

The functor

D(-)[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I}]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}\colon D_{c}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in I})\to D_{c}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in I})

induces a functor

D(-)[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I}]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}\colon D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I},0]}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in I})\to D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I},0]}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in I}).

Proof.

Let
E\in D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I},0]}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in I})
.
Then there is
B\in D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I},0]}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in I})

quasi-isomorphic to
E
such that
B^{j}
(
j\in\mathbb{Z}
) is a direct
sum of sheaves of the form
i_{V!}\underline{k[T_{S}]_{S\in I}[b]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}}_{V}

(an open immersion
i_{V}\colon V\hookrightarrow Y
and
-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I}\leq b\leq 0
).
Then by Lemma
8.18
, we have

D(E)[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I}]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}\cong\mathop{gr\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(B,\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I}}^{I}\end{subarray}}\omega_{Y}).

Obviously, by the property of
B^{j}
(
j\in\mathbb{Z}
), we have

\mathop{gr\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(B,\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I}}^{I}\end{subarray}}\omega_{Y})\in D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{I},0]}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in I}).

∎

Definition 4.45
.

For
J\subset\Lambda
, we put

\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{J}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0]}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})\to\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{\Lambda\setminus J},0]}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda\setminus J})

the “forgetful” functor given by

F\mapsto(a\mapsto F((a,(0))),

where for
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda\setminus J}
, we put
(a,(0))\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}

the element whose
S
-component (
S\in\Lambda\setminus J
) is
a_{S}

and
S_{j}
-component (
S_{j}\in J
) is
0
.

The functor
\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{J}
is an exact functor. It induces a functor

\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{J}\colon D_{\epsilon,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0]}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})\to D_{\epsilon,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{\Lambda\setminus J},0]}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda\setminus J}).

Lemma 4.46
.

For a subset
J\subset\Lambda
, we
have a natural transformation

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{J}\circ D(-)[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}\cong D(-)[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{\Lambda\setminus J}]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{J}

\displaystyle:D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0]}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})\to D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{\Lambda\setminus J},0]}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda\setminus J}).

Proof.

Let
B\in D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0]}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

as in proof of Lemma
4.44
. Then
\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{J}B

also satisfies the similar condition. By Lemma
8.18
,
the assertion follows from a natural isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{J}\mathop{\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(B,\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)}^{\Lambda}\end{subarray}}\omega_{Y})

\displaystyle\cong\mathop{\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{J}B,\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{\Lambda\setminus J}}^{\Lambda\setminus J}\end{subarray}}\omega_{Y}).

∎

Lemma 4.47
.

The functor
D(-)[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}
induces
a functor

D(-)[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}\colon D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})\to D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda}).

Proof.

Let
A,B\in D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0]}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})
,
and
\varphi\colon A\to B
an LG-quasi-isomorphism. By Lemma
4.44
,
it suffices to show that

D(\varphi)[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}\colon D(B)[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}\to D(A)[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}

is also an LG-quasi-isomorphism. Let
(L,a)
be an adapted pair.
Since
\varphi
is an LG-quasi-isomorphism, for sufficiently small
open polyhedral neighborhoods
W_{S_{L,i}}^{\prime}\subset X
of
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{L,i}

(
1\leq i\leq r(L)
), the morphism
\varphi
induces a quasi-isomorphism

\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda\setminus\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}A|_{Y\cap W_{L}^{\prime}}\cong\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda\setminus\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}B|_{Y\cap W_{L}^{\prime}}\in D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}},0]}^{b}(Y\cap W_{L}^{\prime},\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S_{L,i}}]_{i=1}^{r(L)}),

where we put
W_{L}^{\prime}:=\bigcap_{i=1}^{r(L)}W_{S_{L,i}}^{\prime}
. Henbe by
Lemma
4.44
and Lemma
4.46
,
we have

\displaystyle D(B)[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}|_{Y\cap W_{L}^{\prime}}(a)

\displaystyle\cong(\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda\setminus\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}(D(B)[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}))|_{Y\cap W_{L}^{\prime}}(a|_{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}})

\displaystyle\cong D(\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda\setminus\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}B)[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}|_{Y\cap W_{L}^{\prime}}(a|_{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}})

\displaystyle\cong D(\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda\setminus\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}A)[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}|_{Y\cap W_{L}^{\prime}}(a|_{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}})

\displaystyle\cong D(A)[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}|_{Y\cap W_{L}^{\prime}}(a).

Thus the assertion holds.
∎

4.6.
Truncation functors

We shall define a kind of truncation functors which involves grades.
There is one (
[
Bei87
, Appendix]
)
for filtered objects. The difference of it and ours is for the exceptional
part (
q=d
in Subsection
3.2
). (There
should be other kinds of truncation functors which can play the same
role as ours.) Let
X=(\Lambda,w)
be a tropical variety,
Y\subset X

a locally closed polyhedral subset (Definition
2.3
).
We fix
S_{0}\in\Lambda
. Let
Z\subset Y\cap\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{0}
be a
polyhedral subset closed in
Y
, and
r,s,t\in\mathbb{Z}
integers with

t+1\leq s
.

Definition 4.48
.

Let
E
be a complex of flat sheaves
E^{j}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})
.
Let
a=(a_{S})_{S\in\Lambda}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}
. For
u\in\mathbb{Z},
we put

a(S_{0}=u)=(a(S_{0}=u)_{S})_{S\in\Lambda}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}
by
a(S_{0}=u)_{S_{0}}:=u

and
a(S_{0}=u)_{S}:=a_{S}
(
S\neq S_{0}
). We put
\tau_{\leq(t,r-S_{0},s)}^{Z}E^{j}(a)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)

the sheafification of the presheaf whose sections on
U\subset Y

are

\begin{cases}E^{j}(a)(U)&(U\cap Z=\emptyset\\
&or\ j\leq\min\left\{\max\left\{t,r-a_{S_{0}}\right\},s\right\}-1)\\
\mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}\nolimits(d\colon E^{t}(a)\to E^{t+1}(a))(U)&(U\cap Z\neq\emptyset\ \text{and}\ j=t\geq r-a_{S_{0}})\\
+\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits(T_{S_{0}}^{a_{S_{0}}-r+t+1}\colon E^{t}(a(S_{0}=r-t-1))\to E^{t}(a))(U)\\
\mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}\nolimits(d\colon E^{j}(a(S_{0}=r-j))\to E^{j+1}(a(S_{0}=r-j)))(U)&(U\cap Z\neq\emptyset\\
+\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits(T_{S_{0}}\colon E^{j}(a(S_{0}=r-j-1))\to E^{j}(a(S_{0}=r-j)))(U)&\text{and}\ \max\left\{t+1,r-a_{S_{0}}\right\}\leq j\leq s-1)\\
\mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}\nolimits(d\colon E^{s}(a)\to E^{s+1}(a))(U)&(U\cap Z\neq\emptyset\ \text{and}\ j=s\leq r-a_{S_{0}})\\
\mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}\nolimits(d\colon E^{s}(a(S_{0}=r-s))\to E^{s+1}(a(S_{0}=r-s)))(U)&(U\cap Z\neq\emptyset\ \text{and}\ j=s\geq r-a_{S_{0}}+1)\\
0&(U\cap Z\neq\emptyset\ \text{and}\ j\geq s+1)\end{cases}.

Remark 4.49
.

The truncations

\tau_{\leq(t,r-S_{0},s)}^{Z}E^{j}:=\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}}\tau_{\leq(t,r-S_{0},s)}^{Z}E^{j}(a)

(
j\in\mathbb{Z}
) give a complex
\tau_{\leq(t,r-S_{0},s)}^{Z}E
of sheaves

\tau_{\leq(t,r-S_{0},s)}^{Z}E^{j}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})
.
Note that since for a flat
\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}
-graded module
M
, the
multiplication map
T_{S}\colon M(a)\to M(a+e_{S})
is injective
(
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}
,
S\in\Lambda
), the map
d\colon\tau_{\leq(t,r-S_{0},s)}^{Z}E^{j}(a)\to\tau_{\leq(t,r-S_{0},s)}^{Z}E^{j+1}(a)

is well-defined.

There is a natural morphism
\tau_{\leq(t,r-S_{0},s)}^{Z}E\to E
.

Remark 4.50
.

For
x\in Z
and
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}
, we have

\mathscr{H}^{j}(\tau_{\leq(t,r-S_{0},s)}^{Z}E(a))_{x}\cong\begin{cases}\mathscr{H}^{j}(E(a))_{x}&(j\leq\min\left\{\max\left\{t,r-a_{S_{0}}\right\},s\right\})\\
\mathscr{H}^{j}(E(a(S_{0}=r-j)))_{x}&(\max\left\{t,r-a_{S_{0}}\right\}+1\leq j\leq s)\\
0&(j\geq s+1)\end{cases}.

By flat resolutions, we get a
truncation functor

\tau_{\leq(t,r-S_{0},s)}^{Z}\colon D^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})\to D^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda}).

Obviously, it preserves LG-quasi-isomorphisms, hence we get a truncation
functor

\tau_{\leq(t,r-S_{0},s)}^{Z}\colon D_{LG}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})\to D_{LG}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda}).

Our goal in this subsection is Corollary
4.59
,
which is used to show that truncation functors and push-forward by
open immersions give equivalences of categories (Proposition
5.17
).
These are analogs of results for the usual truncation functors (
[
GM83
, Subsection 1.15, Theorem 3.5]
).

Remark 4.51
.

For
u\in\mathbb{Z}
and
?\in\{\leq,\geq,=\}
, we put

\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}?u}^{\Lambda}:=\left\{a\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}\mid a_{S_{0}}?u\right\}

a partially ordered subset of
\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}
. Since
\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}?u}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)

(
?\in\{\leq,\geq,=\}
) is a good abelian category like
\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)

(see Subsection
4.1
), we
have the abelian category of sheaves
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}?u}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k))

(see Subsection
4.2
) with enough injectives,
and also have
D^{b}(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}?u}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)))
.

Since
Z\subset\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{0}
, the following LG-(quasi-)isomorphisms
only involve
S\in\Lambda
with
S\subset S_{0}
.

Definition 4.52
.

A morphism

\varphi\colon A\to B
in a derived category
D^{b}(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}?u}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)))

(resp. an abelian category
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}?u}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k))
)
(
u\in\mathbb{Z}
,
?\in\{\leq,\geq,=\}
) is an LG-quasi-isomorphism (resp.
an LG-isomorphism) if there are open polyhedral neighborhoods
W_{S}^{\prime}\subset X

of
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S
(
S\in\Lambda
with
S\subset S_{0}
) such that
for an adapted pair
(L,a)
with
a\in\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}?u}^{\Lambda}
,
r(L)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
,
and
S_{L,r(L)}=S_{0}
, the morphism
\varphi
induces a quasi-isomorphism
(resp. an isomorphism)

A|_{Z\cap W^{\prime}_{L}}(a)\cong B|_{Z\cap W^{\prime}_{L}}(a).

Remark 4.53
.

In the same way as
D^{b}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

(Subsection
4.4
), we have
a localization

D_{LG}^{b}(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}?u}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k))):=S_{LGq,?}^{-1}D^{b}(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}?u}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)))

(
u\in\mathbb{Z}
,
?\in\{\leq,\geq,=\}
) by all LG-quasi-isomorphisms

S_{LGq,?}
, its heart is equivalent to a localization

\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{LG}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}?u}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)):=S_{LG,?}^{-1}\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}?u}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k))

by all LG-isomorphisms
S_{LG,?}
, and we have an equivalence

D^{b}(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{LG}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}?u}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)))\cong D_{LG}^{b}(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}?u}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k))).

Let
v\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. We put

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),(v,\dots,v)],LG}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}?u}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k))

\displaystyle\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{LG}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}?u}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k))

the full abelian subcategory of objects LG-isomorphic to objects

E
such that

•

for each
S\in I
and
a\in\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}?u}^{\Lambda}
with
a_{S}\geq v

and
a+e_{S}\in\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}?u}^{\Lambda}
, the morphism
E(a)\to E(a+e_{S})

is an isomorphism, and

•

E(a)=0
for
a\in\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}?u}^{\Lambda}
with
-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)\not\leq a
.

Remark 4.54
.

The functor

\displaystyle\bigoplus^{\prime}_{a\in[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),(v,\dots,v)]\cap\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}?u}^{\Lambda}}-(a):

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),(v,\dots,v)],LG}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}?u}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k))

\displaystyle\to\bigoplus^{\prime}_{a\in[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),(v,\dots,v)]\cap\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}?u}^{\Lambda}}\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{L(a)}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)

is faithful, where
a
runs through elements in
[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),(v,\dots,v)]\cap\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}?u}^{\Lambda}

such that the set of
S_{0}
and
S\in\Lambda
with
a_{S}\leq-1

forms a sequence
L(a)=(S_{L(a),1}\subsetneq\dots\subsetneq S_{L(a),r(L(a))-1}\subsetneq S_{L(a),r(L(a))}=S_{0})

(
r(L(a))\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
). (See below Definition
4.42

for
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{L(a)}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)
.)

Remark 4.55
.

Let
u\in\mathbb{Z}
. We have
the natural restriction functor

\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq u}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}\leq u}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)

and “restriction” functors

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits^{S_{0}\geq u}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}\geq u}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits^{S_{0}\geq u}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}\leq u}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}=u}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)

given by

F\mapsto(a\mapsto F(a)/\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits(T_{S_{0}}^{a_{S_{0}}-u+1}\colon F(a(S_{0}=u-1))\to F(a))).

We put
\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}=u}:=\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits^{S_{0}\geq u}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq u}
.
We also have an extension functor

\mathop{\mathrm{ext}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq u}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}\leq u}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)

given by

F\mapsto\left(a\mapsto\begin{cases}F(a)&(a_{S_{0}}\leq u)\\
F(a(S_{0}=u))&(a_{S_{0}}\geq u+1)\end{cases}\right)

and extension functors

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{ext}}\nolimits^{S_{0}\geq u}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}\geq u}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{ext}}\nolimits^{S_{0}\geq u}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}=u}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}\leq u}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)

given by

F\mapsto\left(a\mapsto\begin{cases}F(a)&(a_{S_{0}}\geq u)\\
0&(a_{S_{0}}\leq u-1)\end{cases}\right).

A restriction functor
\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq u}
is the right adjoint of
an extension functor
\mathop{\mathrm{ext}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq u}
. “Restriction” functors

\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits^{S_{0}\geq u}
are the left adjoints of extension functors

\mathop{\mathrm{ext}}\nolimits^{S_{0}\geq u}
.

Remark 4.56
.

Functors in Remark
4.55
give
functors of presheaves on
Z
, hence by sheafifications, give functors
of sheaves in
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits
on
Z
. Obviously, they preserves LG-isomorphisms.
Consequently, we have functors

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{ext}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq u}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{LG}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}\leq u}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k))

\displaystyle\rightleftarrows\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{LG}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda}):\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq u}

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits^{S_{0}\geq u}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{LG}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

\displaystyle\rightleftarrows\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{LG}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}\geq u}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k))\colon\mathop{\mathrm{ext}}\nolimits^{S_{0}\geq u}

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits^{S_{0}\geq u}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{LG}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}\leq u}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k))

\displaystyle\rightleftarrows\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{LG}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}=u}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k))\colon\mathop{\mathrm{ext}}\nolimits^{S_{0}\geq u}.

Functors
\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq u}
and
\mathop{\mathrm{ext}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq u}
are exact,
hence we also have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{ext}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq u}\colon D_{LG}^{b}(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}\leq u}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)))

\displaystyle\rightleftarrows D_{LG}^{b}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})\colon\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq u}.
