Lemma 4.57
.

Let
r,s,t\in\mathbb{Z}
be
with
t+1\leq s
, and
A,B\in D_{[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

such that for an adapted pair
(L,a)
with
r(L)\geq 1
and
S_{L,r(L)}=S_{0}
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{j}(A)(a)\cong_{L}\mathscr{H}^{j}(A)(a(S_{0}=r-j))

\displaystyle\quad(\max\left\{t,r-a_{S_{0}}\right\}+1\leq j\leq s)

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{j}(A)=0

\displaystyle\quad(j\geq s+1)

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{j}(B)(a)=0

\displaystyle\quad(j\leq\min\left\{\max\left\{t,r-a_{S_{0}}\right\},s\right\}-1).

(See Definition
4.42
for
L
-local isomorphisms

\cong_{L}
.) Then the canonical map

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{D_{LG}^{b}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})}(A,B)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{LG}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}\geq r-t}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k))}(\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits^{S_{0}\geq r-t}\mathscr{H}^{t}(A),\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits^{S_{0}\geq r-t}\mathscr{H}^{t}(B))

\displaystyle\oplus\bigoplus_{t+1\leq j\leq s-1}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{LG}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}=r-j}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k))}(\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}=r-j}\mathscr{H}^{j}(A),\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}=r-j}\mathscr{H}^{j}(B))

\displaystyle\oplus\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{LG}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}\leq r-s}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k))}(\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq r-s}\mathscr{H}^{s}(A),\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq r-s}\mathscr{H}^{s}(B))

is an injection.

Proof.

By an exact sequence

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(\tau_{\leq u}A[1],B)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(\tau^{\geq u+1}A,B)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(A,B)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(\tau_{\leq u}A,B)

(where let
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits
denote
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{D_{LG}^{b}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})}

for short), five lemma, and induction on the number of
j\in\mathbb{Z}
such
that
\mathscr{H}^{j}(A)\neq 0\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{LG}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})
,
we may assume that
A^{u}=0
(
u\neq v
) for some
v\in\mathbb{Z}
. We
assume that
t+1\leq v\leq s-1
. The other cases can be proved similarly.
Since

\mathop{\mathrm{ext}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq r-v}\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq r-v}(A)\cong A,

we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{D_{LG}^{b}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})}(A,B)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{D_{LG}^{b}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})}(\mathop{\mathrm{ext}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq r-v}\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq r-v}(A),B)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{D_{LG}^{b}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}\leq r-v}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k))}(\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq r-v}(A),\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq r-v}(B))

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{LG}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}\leq r-v}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k))}(\mathscr{H}^{v}(\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq r-v}(A)),\mathscr{H}^{v}(\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq r-v}(B)))

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{LG}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}\leq r-v}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k))}(\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq r-v}(\mathscr{H}^{v}(A)),\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq r-v}(\mathscr{H}^{v}(B)))

where the third isomophism follows from an equivalence

D_{LG}^{b}(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}\leq r-v}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)))\cong D^{b}(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{LG}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}\leq r-v}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)))

(Remark
4.53
),
\mathscr{H}^{j}(\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq r-v}(A))=0

(
j\geq v+1
), and

\mathscr{H}^{j}(\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq r-v}(B))=0\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{LG}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}\leq r-t}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k))\quad(j\leq v-1).

Since by assumption,

\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq r-v}(\mathscr{H}^{v}(B))=\mathop{\mathrm{ext}}\nolimits^{S_{0}\geq r-v}\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits^{S_{0}\geq r-v}\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq r-v}(\mathscr{H}^{v}(B)),

we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{LG}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}\leq r-v}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k))}(\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq r-v}(\mathscr{H}^{v}(A)),\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq r-v}(\mathscr{H}^{v}(B)))

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{LG}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}\leq r-v}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k))}(\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq r-v}(\mathscr{H}^{v}(A)),\mathop{\mathrm{ext}}\nolimits^{S_{0}\geq r-v}\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits^{S_{0}\geq r-v}\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}\leq r-v}(\mathscr{H}^{v}(B)))

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{LG}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}=r-v}^{\Lambda},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k))}(\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}=r-v}(\mathscr{H}^{v}(A)),\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}=r-v}(\mathscr{H}^{v}(B))).

∎

Corollary 4.58
.

Let
r,s,t\in\mathbb{Z}
be with

t+1\leq s
,
A\in D_{[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

satisfy the condition in Lemma
4.57
,
and
\psi\colon B\to C
a morphism in
D_{[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

such that for an adapted pair
(L,a)
with
r(L)\geq 1
and
S_{L,r(L)}=S_{0}
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{j}(\psi)(a)\colon\mathscr{H}^{j}(B)(a)\cong_{L}\mathscr{H}^{j}(C)(a)

\displaystyle\quad(j\leq\min\left\{\max\left\{t,r-a_{S_{0}}\right\},s\right\}).

Then we have

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{D_{[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})}(A,B)\cong\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{D_{[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})}(A,C).

Proof.

This can be proved in the same way as
[
GM83
, Subsection 1.15, Proposition]

as follows. Let
M
be the mapping cyclinder of
\psi
. Then we
have

\mathscr{H}^{j}(M)(a)=0\quad(j\leq\min\left\{\max\left\{t,r-a_{S_{0}}\right\},s\right\}-1)

and
\mathscr{H}^{j}(C)(a)\to\mathscr{H}^{j}(M)(a)
is
0
map for
j=\min\left\{\max\left\{t,r-a_{S_{0}}\right\},s\right\}
.
By Remark
4.54
and Lemma
4.57
,
we have an injection

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{D_{LG}^{b}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})}(A,M)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\bigoplus^{\prime}_{a\in[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),(v,\dots,v)]\cap\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}\geq r-t}^{\Lambda}}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,L(a)}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)}((\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits^{S_{0}\geq r-t}\mathscr{H}^{t}(A))(a),\mathscr{H}^{t}(M)(a))

\displaystyle\oplus\bigoplus_{t+1\leq j\leq s-1}\bigoplus^{\prime}_{a\in[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),(v,\dots,v)]\cap\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}=r-j}^{\Lambda}}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,L(a)}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)}((\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}=r-j}\mathscr{H}^{j}(A))(a),\mathscr{H}^{j}(M)(a))

\displaystyle\oplus\bigoplus^{\prime}_{a\in[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),(v,\dots,v)]\cap\mathbb{Z}_{S_{0}\leq r-s}^{\Lambda}}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,L(a)}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)}(\mathscr{H}^{s}(A)(a),\mathscr{H}^{s}(M)(a)),

where
v:=\max\{0,r-t\}
,
a
runs through elements such that the
set of
S_{0}
and
S\in\Lambda
with
a_{S}\leq-1
forms a sequence

L(a)=(S_{L(a),1}\subsetneq\dots\subsetneq S_{L(a),r(L(a))-1}\subsetneq S_{L(a),r(L(a))}=S_{0})

(
r(L(a))\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
). Note that for
T:=\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits^{S_{0}\geq r-t},\mathop{\mathrm{res}}\nolimits_{S_{0}=r-j}
,
the natural map

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,L(a)}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)}((T\mathscr{H}^{j}(A))(a),\mathscr{H}^{j}(M)(a))\to\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{\epsilon,L(a)}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)}(\mathscr{H}^{j}(A)(a),\mathscr{H}^{j}(M)(a))

is injective. Hence the map

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{D_{[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})}(A,C)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{D_{[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})}(A,M)

is
0
map. We also have

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{D_{[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(Z,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})}(A,M)[-1]=0.

Hence the assertion holds.
∎

Let
j\colon\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{0}\cap Y\hookrightarrow Y
is an embedding.
We put
i\colon Y\setminus\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{0}\hookrightarrow Y
the complement.

Corollary 4.59
.

We assume that
j\colon\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{0}\cap Y\hookrightarrow Y

is a closed subset. Let
r,s,t\in\mathbb{Z}
be with
t+1\leq s
,
A,B,C\in D_{[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})
,
and
\psi\colon B\to C
a morphism in
D_{[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

such that for an adapted pair
(L,a)
with
r(L)\geq 1
and
S_{L,r(L)}=S_{0}
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{j}(j^{*}A)(a)\cong_{L}\mathscr{H}^{j}(j^{*}A)(a(S_{0}=r-j))

\displaystyle\quad(\max\left\{t,r-a_{S_{0}}\right\}+1\leq j\leq s)

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{j}(j^{*}A)=0

\displaystyle\quad(j\geq s+1)

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{j}(j^{*}\psi)(a)\colon\mathscr{H}^{j}(j^{*}B)(a)\cong_{L}\mathscr{H}^{j}(j^{*}C)(a)

\displaystyle\quad(j\leq\min\left\{\max\left\{t,r-a_{S_{0}}\right\},s\right\})

\displaystyle i^{*}\psi\colon i^{*}B\cong_{LG}i^{*}C

(e.g.,
\psi\colon B=\tau_{\leq(t,r-S_{0},s)}^{Z}C\to C
). Then we
have

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{D_{[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})}(A,B)\cong\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{D_{[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})}(A,C).

Proof.

By applying
Ri_{!}i^{*}\to\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\to Rj_{*}j^{*}\to^{[1]}
to
B
and

C
, the assertion follows from five lemma and Corollary
4.58

for
(j^{*}A,j^{*}B,j^{*}C)
and
(j^{*}A,j^{*}B[-1]_{\deg},j^{*}C[-1]_{\deg})
.
∎

5.
Sheaf-theoretic construction and Poincaré-Verdier duality

Let
X=(\Lambda,w)
be a tropical variety of dimension
d
in a
tropical toric variety
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma})
. We assume that
T_{\Sigma}

is smooth and
X
is regular at infinity. In this section, we shall
introduce

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{p,*}\in D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0]}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

(Definition
5.18
), and give an LG-quasi-isomorphism

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{p,*}\cong_{LG}D(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*})[2d]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}

(Theorem
5.26
). The
tropical intersection
complex

IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{p,*}
is defined as
\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{p,*}
,
and the above LG-quasi-isomorphism gives a quasi-isomorphism

IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{p,*}\cong D(IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*})[2d]_{\deg}.

Later (Proposition
6.3
), we will
show that there is a natural LG-quasi-isomorphism

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{p,*}\cong_{LG}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,X}^{p,*}.

Remind that we put

\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X):=\sum_{S\in\Lambda}(\dim S+2\dim\sigma_{S})e_{S}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda},

where
e_{S}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}
is the element whose
S
-component
is
1
and other components are
0
.

5.1.
On smooth part

In this subsection, we study a sheaf
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{p,w}[2d-p]_{\deg}

on
X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}:=\bigcup_{R\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits R
. The sheaf
\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{p,w}=\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{p,w}

on
X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}
is a version of Gubler-Jell-Rabinoff’s sheaves for Berkovich
analytic spaces (
[
GJR21b
]
)
and for weighted metric graphs with boundaries (
[
GJR21a
]
).
(See also the beginning of Subsection
3.1
.) In
Subsection
5.2
, we will extend

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{p,w}[2d-p]_{\deg}
to
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{p,*}

on
X
.

Definition 5.1
.

For
R\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}
, we put

F^{p,w}(R):=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}(F_{p,w}(R),\mathbb{Q}).

More explicitly, for
P\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d}
,

F^{p,w}(P):=\bigwedge^{p}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}P,\mathbb{Q})

and for
Q\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d-1}
, the set
F^{p,w}(Q)
is the subset
of
\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d}\\
Q\subset P\end{subarray}}\bigwedge^{p}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}P,\mathbb{Q})
consisting of elements
(f_{P})_{P}
satisfying

\displaystyle f_{P_{i}}|_{\bigwedge^{p}\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}Q}=f_{P_{j}}|_{\bigwedge^{p}\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}Q}

\displaystyle\quad(P_{i},P_{j}\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d},\ Q\subset P_{i}\cap P_{j})\ \text{and}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d}\\
Q\subset P\end{subarray}}w_{P}f_{P}(v_{P,Q}\wedge u)=0

\displaystyle\quad(u\in\bigwedge^{p-1}\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}Q),

where
\tilde{v_{P,Q}}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}P
is as in Definition
3.1
.

For
Q\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d-1}
and
P\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d}
with
Q\subset P
,
the projection gives a map
F^{p,w}(Q)\to F^{p,w}(P)
.

Definition 5.2
.

We put
\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}
the constructible sheaf on
X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}

given by
\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}|_{\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits R}=\underline{F^{p,w}(R)}_{\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits R}

(
R\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}
) and the above maps
F^{p,w}(Q)\to F^{p,w}(P)
,
where
\underline{F^{p,w}(R)}_{\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits R}
is a constant sheaf.

Since every chain on
X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}
is allowable, the geometric tropical
intersection complex
IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{p,*}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}
on
X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}

are just the complex of polyhedral chains with
F_{d-p,w}
-coefficients.
In paricular,
IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d,*}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}
is just the complex
of
\mathbb{Q}
-coefficients polyhedral chains.

Lemma 5.3
.

(
[
GS23
, Proposition A.9 and Theorem 4.20]
)
We have a natural isomorphism

IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{p,*}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}\cong\mathop{\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}[d+p]_{\deg},IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d,*}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}})[2d]_{\deg}

and a quasi-isomorphism

\mathop{\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}[d+p]_{\deg},IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d,*}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}})[2d]_{\deg}\cong D(\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}[d+p]_{\deg})[2d]_{\deg}.

(Note that our sign convention of coboundary maps of complexes of
homomorphisms
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(-,-)
are the standard one, see e.g.,
[
Ach21
, Appendix A.6]
,

[
Fu15
, Section 6.4]
, [SGA4-3].)

Proof.

The assertion can be proved in the same way as tropical homology
[
GS23
, Proposition A.9 and Theorem 4.20]
.
Note that the quasi-isomorphism is given by a morphism
R\Gamma_{c}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d,*}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}\to\mathbb{Q}

of counting points.
∎

Lemma 5.4
.

We have

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{m}(IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{p,*}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}})=0\quad(m\geq-2d+p+1).

Proof.

The assertion is local, hence we may assume that
X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}=\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times C

for some
1
-dimensional tropical fan
C\subset\mathbb{R}^{s}
. By Lemma

5.3
and Künneth formula
for
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times C}^{d-p,w}[d+p]_{\deg}
, we have Künneth
formula for
IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times C}^{p,*}
. Then the assertion
follows from the case of
X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}=\mathbb{R}
or
C
, which easily follows
from direct computation.
∎

Definition 5.5
.

(
[
Bou74
, III Subsection 11.7]
)
Let
A
be a finite dimensional
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space,
A^{\vee}
its
dual
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space. For
p\leq q
, the contraction

-\llcorner-\colon\bigwedge^{q}A\otimes\bigwedge^{p}A^{\vee}\to\bigwedge^{q-p}A

is a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear map characterized by

\displaystyle(e_{1}\wedge\dots\wedge e_{q})\llcorner(e_{1}^{\vee}\wedge\dots\wedge e_{p}^{\vee})

\displaystyle=e_{p+1}\wedge\dots\wedge e_{q}

\displaystyle(e_{2}\wedge\dots\wedge e_{q+1})\llcorner(e_{1}^{\vee}\wedge\dots\wedge e_{p}^{\vee})

\displaystyle=0

for any basis
e_{i}\in A
and its dual basis
e_{i}^{\vee}\in A^{\vee}
.
By contraction,
\bigwedge^{*}A
is a right
\bigwedge^{*}A^{\vee}
-module,
and
\bigwedge^{p}A^{\vee}
is the dual
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space of
\bigwedge^{p}A
,
where
\bigwedge^{*}A^{\vee}
has a ring structure by the wedge product.

The fundamental class
[X]\in C_{d,d,w}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{BM}}\nolimits}(X)
(
[
MZ14
]
,

[
JRS18
, Definition 4.8]
,

[
GS23
, Subsection 5.1]
)
is given as

[X]=\sum_{P\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d}}w_{P}1_{\bigwedge^{d}\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Z}}P}[P],

where we take generators
1_{\bigwedge^{d}\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Z}}P}\in\bigwedge^{d}\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Z}}P\cong\mathbb{Z}

and
[P]\in H_{d}^{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}(P,\partial P;\mathbb{Z})
so that for any
d
-form

a\neq 0\in\bigwedge^{d}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Z}}P,\mathbb{Z})
, the sign of
1_{\bigwedge^{d}\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Z}}P}\llcorner a

(Definition
5.5
) is same as the sign of
\int_{[P]}ga

for a non-negative integrable function
g\not\equiv 0
. For example,
when
[P]
is the standard
d
-simplex
[\Delta^{d}]\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
we have

1_{\bigwedge^{d}\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Z}}P}=\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{1}}\wedge\dots\wedge\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{d}},

where
x_{1},\dots,x_{d}
is the standard coordinate of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
The fundamental class is allowable (Remark
3.15
),
i.e.,
[X]\in IH_{d,d}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{BM}}\nolimits}(X)
.

Similarly to tropical homology (e.g.,
[
JRS18
, Definition 4.11]
),
the fundamental class
[X]
induces a cap product

[X]\cap-\colon\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}\to\mathscr{H}^{-2d+p}(IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{p,*}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}})

characterized by

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits},x}^{p,w}\cong F^{p,w}(P)

\displaystyle\to\mathscr{H}^{-2d+p}(IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{p,*}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}})_{x}

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle\mapsto 1_{\wedge^{d}\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Z}}P}[P]\llcorner f

at
x\in\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits P
(
P\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d}
).

Lemma 5.6
.

The cap product
[X]\cap-

is an isomorphism
\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}\cong\mathscr{H}^{-2d+p}(IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{p,*}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}})
.
In particular, we have a quasi-isomorphism
\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}[2d-p]_{\deg}\cong IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{p,*}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}
.

Proof.

The first assertion easily follows from local computation. The second
assertion follows from the first one and Lemma
5.4
.
∎

By Lemma
5.3
and Lemma
5.6
,
we have the following.

Corollary 5.7
.

The cap product
-\cap[X]

induces a quasi-isomorphism

\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}[2d-p]_{\deg}\cong D(\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}[d+p]_{\deg})[2d]_{\deg}.

We shall prove an LG-version (Proposition
5.15
)
of Corollary
5.7
. We define

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0]}(X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits},\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

by

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}(a):=(-\cap[X])^{-1}(\mathscr{H}^{-2d+p}(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{p,*}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}})(a))\subset\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}

(
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}
) and inclusions. For a subset
I\subset\Lambda
,
we put

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{I}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}

\displaystyle:=\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda\setminus I}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}

(Definition
4.45
). For a sequnce
L=(S_{L,1}\subsetneq\dots\subsetneq S_{L,r(L)})

(
S_{L,i}\in\Lambda
,
r(L)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
), we put
\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L,r(L)}}\subset\Lambda

the subset of polyhedra strictly containing
S_{L,r(L)}
and
\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L,r(L)},L}:=\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L,r(L)}}\cup\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}
.
We also put
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{L}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}:=\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda\setminus\{S_{L,i}\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}
.
Remind that we fixed open polyhedral (Definition
2.3
)
neighborhoods
W_{S}
of
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S
in
X
(
S\in\Lambda
) such
that
W_{S}\cap W_{S^{\prime}}=\emptyset
for
S,S^{\prime}\in\Lambda
with
S\not\subset S^{\prime}

and
S^{\prime}\not\subset S
. We put
W_{L}:=\bigcap_{i=1}^{r(L)}W_{S_{L,i}}
.
For
b\in\mathbb{Z}^{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}
, we put

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits_{b}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{L}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}(b)/\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}\\
b^{\prime}\lneq b\end{subarray}}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{L}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}(b^{\prime}),

and also define

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L,r(L)}}}\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits_{b}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L},\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L,r(L)}}})

by

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L,r(L)}}}\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits_{b}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}(a^{\prime})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L,r(L)},L}}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}((b,a^{\prime}))/\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}\\
b^{\prime}\lneq b\end{subarray}}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L,r(L)},L}}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}((b^{\prime},a^{\prime}))

(
a^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L,r(L)}}}
) and natural morphisms.
Obviously, for
a^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L,r(L)}}},

we have

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L,r(L)}}}\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits_{b}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}(a^{\prime})\cong\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits_{b}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}.

Lemma 5.8
.

There is a (non-canonical) decomposition

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L,r(L)}}}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}\cong\bigoplus_{b\in\mathbb{Z}^{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L,r(L)}}}\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits_{b}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}

in
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L},\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L,r(L)}}})
.
In particular, we have

\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}\cong\bigoplus_{b\in\mathbb{Z}^{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}}\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits_{b}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}.

Proof.

We fix an inner product of
N\otimes\mathbb{Q}
. For each
P\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d}

containing
S_{L,r(L)}
, a sequence

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}\sigma_{S_{L,r(L)}}\subseteq\dots.\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}\sigma_{S_{L,1}}

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\sigma_{S_{L,1}}}^{-1}(\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}S_{L,1})\subseteq\dots\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\sigma_{S_{L,r(L)}}}^{-1}(\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}S_{L,r(L)})

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}P

gives an orthogonal decomposition of
\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}P
. These decompositions
for all such
P
induces the required decomposition of
\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}

into
\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits_{b}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}
(
b\in\mathbb{Z}^{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}
)
(cf. Proposition
3.28
). We shall
show that this decomposition of
\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}

induces the required decomposition of
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L,r(L)}}}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}(a^{\prime})

for each
a^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L,r(L)}}}
. For this, it
suffices to show that for
a^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L,r(L)}}}

such that the set of
S\in\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L,r(L)}}
with
a^{\prime}_{S}\leq-1

forms a sequence
L(a^{\prime})=(S_{L(a^{\prime}),1}\subsetneq\dots\subsetneq S_{L(a^{\prime}),r(L(a^{\prime}))})

(
r(L(a^{\prime}))\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
), there exists a decomposition of
\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L^{\prime}(a^{\prime})}}

such that
\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits_{b}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L^{\prime}(a^{\prime})}}
(
b\in\mathbb{Z}^{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}
)
and
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L,r(L)}}}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L^{\prime}(a^{\prime})}}(a^{\prime})

are direct sums of some direct summands, where we put

L^{\prime}(a^{\prime})=(S_{L^{\prime}(a^{\prime}),1}\subsetneq\dots\subsetneq S_{L^{\prime}(a^{\prime}),r(L)+r(L(a^{\prime}))})

a sequence with
S_{L^{\prime}(a^{\prime}),i}:=S_{L,i}
(
1\leq i\leq r(L)
) and

S_{L^{\prime}(a^{\prime}),i}:=S_{L(a^{\prime}),i-r(L)}
(
r(L)+1\leq i\leq r(L)+r(L(a^{\prime}))
).
For a polyhedron
P\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d}
containing
S_{L^{\prime}(a^{\prime}),r(L)+r(L(a^{\prime}))}
,
we have a sequence

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}\sigma_{S_{L^{\prime}(a^{\prime}),r(L)+r(L(a^{\prime}))}}\subseteq\dots.\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}\sigma_{S_{L^{\prime}(a^{\prime}),1}}

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\sigma_{S_{L^{\prime}(a^{\prime}),1}}}^{-1}(\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}S_{L^{\prime}(a^{\prime}),1})\subseteq\dots\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\sigma_{S_{L^{\prime}(a^{\prime}),r(L)+r(L(a^{\prime}))}}}^{-1}(\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}S_{L^{\prime}(a^{\prime}),r(L)+r(L(a^{\prime}))})

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}P.

This gives the required decomposition of a decomposition
\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L^{\prime}(a^{\prime})}}
.
∎

For a poyhedron
S\in\Lambda
and
c\in\mathbb{Z}
, we put

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{c}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}

\displaystyle:=\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda\setminus\left\{S\right\}}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}(-c).

Corollary 5.9
.

We have a quasi-isomorphism

D(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{L}\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{d-p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}})\cong\mathop{gr\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{L}\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{d-p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}},\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}}\omega_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}),

where
\omega_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}\in D^{b}(X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q})

is the injective dualizing complex.

Proof.

This directly follows from the second assertion of Lemma
5.8

and Lemma
8.18
.
∎

Remark 5.10
.

Let

\omega_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}}\in D^{b}(X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits},\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}})

be the dualizing complex. By a quasi-isomorphism
IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d,*}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}\cong\omega_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}

(see e.g.,
[
GS23
, Theorem 4.20]
)
and Lemma
8.17
,
we have a quasi-isomorphism

\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{\Lambda}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d,*}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}\cong\omega_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda}.

The following is a case of an LG-version of
[
GS23
, Proposition A.9]
.
By Lemma
4.44
, we have

D(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{p,w}[2d-p]_{\deg})[2d]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}\in D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0]}^{b}(X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits},\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda}).

Lemma 5.11
.

The quasi-isomorphism in Remark
5.10

gives an LG-quasi-isomorphism

\displaystyle D(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{d-p,w}[d+p]_{\deg})[2d]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}

\displaystyle\cong_{LG}

\displaystyle\mathop{gr\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{d-p,w}[d+p]_{\deg},\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{\Lambda}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d,*}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}})[2d]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}.

Proof.

Let
L=(S_{L,1}\subsetneq\dots\subsetneq S_{L,r(L)})
(
S_{L,i}\in\Lambda
,

r(L)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
) be a sequence. By Lemma
4.46
,
it suffices to show that

\displaystyle D(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{L}\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{d-p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathop{gr\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{L}\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{d-p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}},\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d,*}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}).

We fix the injective dualizing complex
\omega_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}\in D^{b}(X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q})
.
By Corollary
5.9
, we have

\displaystyle D(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{L}\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{d-p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathop{gr\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{L}\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{d-p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}},\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}}\omega_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}).

For
c\in\mathbb{Z}^{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}
and
E=\omega_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}

or
IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d,*}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}
, we have isomorphims

\displaystyle\mathop{gr\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{L}\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{d-p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}},\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}}E)(c)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}\nolimits(\mathop{\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{d-p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}},E)\to\mathop{\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(\sum_{i=1}^{r(L)}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S_{L,i}}^{c_{S_{L,i}}+1}\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{d-p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}},E))

(5.1)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}/\sum_{i=1}^{r(L)}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S_{L,i}}^{c_{S_{L,i}}+1}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}},E),

where the kernel is taken in
C(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}))
.
By
[
GS23
, Proposition A.9]
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}/\sum_{i=1}^{r(L)}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S_{L,i}}^{c_{S_{L,i}}+1}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}},IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d,*}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}/\sum_{i=1}^{r(L)}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S_{L,i}}^{c_{S_{L,i}}+1}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}},\omega_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}).

Thus we proved the assertion.
∎

Remark 5.12
.

Let
S\in\Lambda

and
P\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d}
with
S\subset P
. We put
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{k}F_{d-p,w}(P)\subset F_{d-p,w}(P)

the subspace spanned by elements
\alpha
with
v(\alpha\cap\sigma_{S})+u(\alpha\cap\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\sigma_{S}}^{-1}(S))\geq k

(see Definition
3.4
for
v
and
u
). Then for
k\in\mathbb{Z}
,
the wedge product induces a non-degenerate pairing

\wedge\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{-k+\dim S+2\dim\sigma_{S}}F_{d-p,w}(P)\times(F_{p,w}(P)/\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S}^{k+1}F_{p,w}(P))\to F_{d,w}(P)(\cong\mathbb{Q}).

Lemma 5.13
.

Let
L=(S_{L,1}\subsetneq\dots\subsetneq S_{L,r(L)})

(
S_{L,i}\in\Lambda
,
r(L)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
) be a sequence and
c=(c_{S_{L,i}})_{S_{L,i}\in\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{m}(\mathop{\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}/\sum_{i=1}^{r(L)}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S_{L,i}}^{c_{S_{L,i}}+1}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}},IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d,*}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}))

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{L}\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{p,w}(c-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}})|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}&(m=-d)\\
0&(m\neq-d).\end{cases}

Proof.

Similarly to
[
GS23
, Theorem 4.20]
,

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}/\sum_{i=1}^{r(L)}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S_{L,i}}^{c_{S_{L,i}}+1}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}},IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d,*}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}})

is isomorphic to the complex of sheaves of locally finite chains
with coefficients in the dual of

\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}/\sum_{i=1}^{r(L)}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S_{L,i}}^{c_{S_{L,i}}+1}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}.

Namely, by Remark
5.12
,
it is isomorphic to

\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda\setminus\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{p,*}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}[-d+p]_{\deg}(c-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}),

which is, similarly to Lemma
5.8
,
a direct sum of direct summands of a decomposition of
IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{p,*}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}
.
Hence by Lemma
5.4
and Lemma

5.6
, the assertion holds.
∎

Remark 5.14
.

By Lemma
4.46

and Corollary
5.7
, we have a
quasi-isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{d-p,w}[d+p]_{\deg}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{d-p,w}[d+p]_{\deg}

\displaystyle\cong D(\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{p,w}[2d-p]_{\deg})[2d]_{\deg}

\displaystyle\cong\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda}D(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{p,w}[2d-p]_{\deg})[2d]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}.

Proposition 5.15
.

There is a unique LG-quasi-isomorphism

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{p,w}[2d-p]_{\deg}\cong_{LG}D(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{d-p,w}[d+p]_{\deg})[2d]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}

in
D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits},\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

which induces the quasi-isomorphism in Remark
5.14

by the “forgetful” functor
\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda}
.

Proof.

The uniqueness holds since by definition of
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{p,w}[2d-p]_{\deg}
,
LG-automorphisms of
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{p,w}[2d-p]_{\deg}

are uniquely determined by induced automorphisms of
\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{p,w}[2d-p]_{\deg}.

By Lemma
5.6
, the cap product

[X]\cap-
induces a quasi-isomorphism

\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{p,w}[2d-p]_{\deg}\to IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{p,*}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}\cong\mathop{\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}[d+p]_{\deg},IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d,*}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}})[2d]_{\deg}.

By Lemma
5.13
and
isomorphism (
5.1
) in proof of Lemma
5.11

for
E=IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d,*}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}
, it induces
an LG-quasi-isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{p,w}[2d-p]_{\deg}

\displaystyle\cong_{LG}\mathop{gr\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{d-p,w}[d+p]_{\deg},\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{\Lambda}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{d,*}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}})[2d]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}.

Hence the assertion follows from Lemma
5.11
.
∎

5.2.
Sheaf-theoretic construction and Poincaré-Verdier duality

We put
X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}:=U_{1}:=\bigcup_{R\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits R
. We
fix an order
\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}=\left\{S_{2,}\dots,S_{\#\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}+1}\right\}

such that
S_{i}\supset S_{j}
only if
i\leq j
. In particular,
for
2\leq k\leq\#\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}+1
, the union
U_{k}:=U_{1}\cup\bigcup_{j=2}^{k}\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{j}

is an open subset of
X
, and
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{k}\subset U_{k}
is a
closed subset. We put
i_{k}\colon U_{k-1}\hookrightarrow U_{k}

and
j_{k}:\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{k}\hookrightarrow U_{k}
embeddings. We put

m_{p,S}:=-2p-\dim S-\dim\sigma_{S}-1.

In this subsection, for simplicity, for a locally closed polyhedral
subset
Y\subset X
, we put

D_{*}^{b}(Y):=D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],*}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

(
*=\emptyset,LG
).

Definition 5.16
.

Let
2\leq k\leq\#\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}+1
,
E\in D_{LG}^{b}(U_{k})
,
and
p\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. We put
\epsilon(\sigma_{S_{k}}):=\min\left\{\dim\sigma_{S_{k}},1\right\}
.
We say that
E
satisfies Axiom
A_{p}
at
S_{k}
if for an adapted
pair
(L,a)
(Definition
4.19
) with
r(L)\geq 1

and
S_{L,r(L)}=S_{k}
, the following conditions hold:

(1)

(local vanishing) we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{m}(j_{k}^{*}E)(a)\cong_{L}\mathscr{H}^{m}(j_{k}^{*}E)(a(S_{k}=m_{p,S_{k}}-m))

\displaystyle(\max\left\{-p-d-\epsilon(\sigma_{S_{k}}),m_{p,S_{k}}-a_{S_{k}}\right\}+1\leq m\leq-p-\dim S_{k}-2+\epsilon(\sigma_{S_{k}}))

and

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{m}(j_{k}^{*}E)=0

\displaystyle\quad(m\geq-p-\dim S_{k}-1+\epsilon(\sigma_{S_{k}})),

(2)

(attaching property) the natural map

\mathscr{H}^{m}(j_{k}^{*}E)(a)\to\mathscr{H}^{m}(j_{k}^{*}Ri_{k*}i_{k}^{*}E)(a)

is an
L
-local isomorphism for

\displaystyle m\leq\max\left\{-p-d-\epsilon(\sigma_{S_{k}}),\min\left\{m_{p,S_{k}}-a_{S_{k}},-p-\dim S_{k}-2+\epsilon(\sigma_{S_{k}})\right\}\right\}.

To simplify notation, we put

\tau_{p,S_{k}}:=\begin{cases}\tau_{\leq(-p-d,m_{p,S_{k}}-S_{k},-p-\dim S_{k}-2)}^{\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{k}}&(\dim\sigma_{S_{k}}=0,\dim S_{k}+3\leq d)\\
\tau_{\leq-p-d}^{\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{k}}&(\dim\sigma_{S_{k}}=0,\dim S_{k}+2=d)\\
\tau_{\leq(-p-d-1,m_{p,S_{k}}-S_{k},-p-\dim S_{k}-1)}^{\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{k}}&(\dim\sigma_{S_{k}}\geq 1)\end{cases}.

Proposition 5.17
.

For
2\leq k\leq\#\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}+1

and
p\geq 0
, the functor

\tau_{p,S_{k}}Ri_{k*}\colon D^{b}(U_{k-1})\to D^{b}(U_{k})

induces an equivalence of categories between
D_{LG}^{b}(U_{k-1})

and the full subcategory of
D_{LG}^{b}(U_{k})
consisting of complexes
satisfying Axiom
A_{p}
at
S_{k}
.

Proof.

When
\dim\sigma_{S_{k}}=0
and
\dim S_{k}+2=d
, this is
[
GM83
, Subsection 3.5, Theorem]
.
The other cases can be also proved in the same way by Corollary
4.59
.
∎

Definition 5.18
.

We put

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}

\displaystyle:=\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,X}^{d-p,*}

\displaystyle:=\tau_{p,S_{\#\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}+1}}Ri_{\#\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}+1*}\tau_{p,S_{\#\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}}}Ri_{\#\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}*}\dots\tau_{p,S_{2}}Ri_{2*}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}[d+p]_{\deg}

\displaystyle\in D^{b}(X).

We also put

\displaystyle IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}:=IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,X}^{d-p,*}:=\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}

\displaystyle\in D_{c}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}).

Definition 5.19
.

Let
2\leq k\leq\#\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}+1
,
E\in D_{LG}^{b}(U_{k})
,
and
p\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. We put
\epsilon(\sigma_{S_{k}}):=\min\left\{\dim\sigma_{S_{k}},1\right\}
.
We say that
E
satisfies Axiom
A_{p}
(2)
′
at
S_{k}
if for
an adapted pair
(L,a)
with
r(L)\geq 1
and
S_{L,r(L)}=S_{k}

and

\displaystyle m\leq\max\left\{-p-d+1-\epsilon(\sigma_{S_{k}}),\min\left\{m_{p,S_{k}}-a_{S_{k}}+1,-p-\dim S_{k}-1+\epsilon(\sigma_{S_{k}})\right\}\right\},

we have
\mathscr{H}^{m}(j_{k}^{!}E)(a)=0
in
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{L}(\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{k},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q})
.

Remark 5.20
.

For
x\in\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{k}
, we put
u_{x}\colon\left\{x\right\}\to\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{k}

and
j_{x}\colon\left\{x\right\}\to U_{k}
the inclusions. Since

j_{x}^{!}\cong u_{x}^{!}j_{k}^{!}\cong u_{x}^{*}j_{k}^{!}[-\dim S_{k}]
,
similarly to
[
GM83
, Subsection 3.4]
,
we can reformulate Axiom
A_{p}
(2)
′
as follows. A complex
E\in D_{LG}^{b}(U_{k})

satisfies Axiom
A_{p}
(2)
′
at
S_{k}
if and only if there
are a complex
F\in D^{b}(U_{k})
LG-quasi-isomorphic to
E
and
open polyhedral neighborhoods
W_{S}^{\prime}\subset X
of
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S
(
S\in\Lambda

with
S\subset S_{k}
) such that for an adapted pair
(L,a)
with

r(L)\geq 1
and
S_{L,r(L)}=S_{k}
, a point
x\in\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{k}\cap W_{L}^{\prime}
,
and

\displaystyle m\leq\max\left\{-p-d+1-\epsilon(\sigma_{S_{k}}),\min\left\{m_{p,S_{k}}-a_{S_{k}}+1,-p-\dim S_{k}-1+\epsilon(\sigma_{S_{k}})\right\}\right\}+\dim S_{k},

we have
H^{m}(j_{x}^{!}F)(a)=0
.

For
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}
, we put

n_{p,S_{k},a}:=\max\left\{-p-d+1-\epsilon(\sigma_{S_{k}}),\min\left\{m_{p,S_{k}}-a_{S_{k}}+1,-p-\dim S_{k}-1+\epsilon(\sigma_{S_{k}})\right\}\right\}.

Note that when
n_{p,S_{k},a}\leq-p-\dim S_{k}-2+\epsilon(\sigma_{S_{k}})
,
we have
m_{p,S_{k}}-n_{p,S_{k},a}\leq a_{S_{k}}-1
.

Lemma 5.21
.

•

When
E
satisfies Axiom
A_{p}
(2)
′
, it also satisfies Axiom

A_{p}
(2).

•

We assume that
E
satisfies Axiom
A_{p}
(1) and (2). We also
assume that for an adapted pair
(L,a)
such that
r(L)\geq 1
,
S_{L,r(L)}=S_{k}
,
and
n_{p,S_{k},a}\leq-p-\dim S_{k}-2+\epsilon(\sigma_{S_{k}})
,
the natural map

(5.2)

\mathscr{H}^{n_{p,S_{k},a}}(j_{k}^{*}Ri_{k*}i_{k}^{*}E)(a(S_{k}=m_{p,S_{k}}-n_{p,S_{k},a}))\to\mathscr{H}^{n_{p,S_{k},a}}(j_{k}^{*}Ri_{k*}i_{k}^{*}E)(a)

is injective in
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits_{L}(\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{k},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q})
. Then the complex

E
satisfies Axiom
A_{p}
(2)
′
.

Proof.

The case of
\dim S_{k}+2=d
and
\dim\sigma_{S}=0
is
[
GM83
, Subsection 3.4]
.
The other cases, given below, are similar. Let
(L,a)
be an adapted
pair with
r(L)\geq 1
and
S_{L,r(L)}=S_{k}
. By a distinguished
triangle
j_{k}^{!}E^{*}\to j_{k}^{*}E^{*}\to j_{k}^{*}Ri_{k*}i_{k}^{*}E^{*}\to^{[1]}\cdot
,
the first assertion holds, and Axiom
A_{p}
(2) shows
\mathscr{H}^{m}(j_{k}^{!}E^{*})(a)=0

for
m\leq n_{p,S_{k},a}-1
. We assume that
E
satisfies Axiom

A_{p}
(1) and (2). We also assume that
n_{p,S_{k},a}\leq-p-\dim S_{k}-2+\epsilon(\sigma_{S_{k}})
.
The other case is easy. We have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{H}^{n_{p,S_{k},a}}(j_{k}^{!}E^{*})(a)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{H}^{n_{p,S_{k},a}}(j_{k}^{*}E^{*})(a)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{H}^{n_{p,S_{k},a}}(j_{k}^{*}Ri_{k*}i_{k}^{*}E^{*})(a)}
\textstyle{\mathscr{H}^{n_{p,S_{k},a}}(j_{k}^{*}E^{*})(a^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathscr{H}^{n_{p,S_{k},a}}(j_{k}^{*}Ri_{k*}i_{k}^{*}E^{*})(a^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

with exact first row, where for simplicity, we put
a^{\prime}:=a(S_{k}=m_{p,S_{k}}-n_{p,S_{k},a})
,
and the first vertical arrow is an
L
-isomorphism by Axiom
A_{p}

(1). Hence when the last vertical arrow is injective, we have
\mathscr{H}^{n_{p,S_{k},a}}(j_{k}^{!}E^{*})(a)=0
.
∎

Definition 5.22
.

A complex
A\in D_{LG}^{b}(\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{k})
is said to be
point-wise
free
if there are a complex
A^{\prime}\in D^{b}(\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{k})
LG-quasi-isomorphic
to
A
and open polyhedral neighborhoods
W_{S}^{\prime}\subset X
of
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S

(
S\in\Lambda
with
S\subset S_{k}
) such that for a sequenece

L=(S_{L,1}\subsetneq\dots\subsetneq S_{L,r(L)})
(
S_{L,i}\in\Lambda
,

r(L)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
) with
S_{L,r(L)}=S_{k}
, an integer
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,
and
x\in\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{k}\cap W_{L}^{\prime}
, a
\mathbb{Q}[T_{S_{L,i}}]_{i=1}^{r(L)}
-module

H^{j}(\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda\setminus\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}u_{x}^{*}A^{\prime})

is free, where we put
u_{x}\colon\left\{x\right\}\to\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{k}
.

Remind that we put

\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X):=\sum_{S\in\Lambda}(\dim S+2\dim\sigma_{S})e_{S}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}.

Corollary 5.23
.

Let
A\in D_{LG}^{b}(U_{k-1})
such that
j_{k}^{\epsilon}\tau_{d-p,S_{k}}Ri_{k*}A

(
\epsilon=*,!
) is point-wise free and the morphism (
5.2
)
is injective for
\tau_{d-p,S_{k}}Ri_{k*}A
. We put

B:=D(A)[2d]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}\in D_{LG}^{b}(U_{k-1})

(Lemma
4.47
). Then there exists a unique LG-quasi-isomorphism

\tau_{p,S_{k}}Ri_{k*}B\cong_{LG}D(\tau_{d-p,S_{k}}Ri_{k*}A)[2d]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}

in
D_{LG}^{b}(U_{k})
extending the equality on
U_{k-1}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.21
,
\tau_{d-p,S_{k}}Ri_{k*}A

satisfies Axiom
A_{d-p}
(2)
′
. Let
A^{\prime}\in D^{b}(U_{k})
be LG-quasi-isomorphic
to
\tau_{d-p,S_{k}}Ri_{k*}A
such that
H^{j}(\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda\setminus\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}u_{x}^{*}j_{k}^{\epsilon}A^{\prime})

(
\epsilon=*,!
) is free for some open polyhedral neighborhoods
W_{S}^{\prime}\subset X

of
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S
(
S\in\Lambda
with
S\subset S_{k}
), a sequenece

L=(S_{L,1}\subsetneq\dots\subsetneq S_{L,r(L)})
(
S_{L,i}\in\Lambda
,

r(L)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
) with
S_{L,r(L)}=S_{k}
, an integer
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,
and
x\in\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{k}\cap W_{L}^{\prime}
. We may assume that
A^{\prime}
satisfies
the condition in Remark
5.20
for
W_{S}^{\prime}

(S\in\Lambda
with
S\subset S_{k}
). For
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}
adapted
to
L
and
\left\{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}\right\}=\left\{*,!\right\}
,
we have

\displaystyle H^{m}(j_{x}^{\epsilon_{1}}D(A^{\prime})[2d]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits})(a)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle H^{m+2d}(D(\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda\setminus\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}(j_{x}^{\epsilon_{2}}A^{\prime}))[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits})(a|_{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(H^{-m-2d}(\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda\setminus\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}(j_{x}^{\epsilon_{2}}A^{\prime}))[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)|_{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits},\mathbb{Q}[T_{S_{L,i}}]_{i=1}^{r(L)})(a|_{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}),

where the first isomorphism follows from Lemma
4.46
,
and the second isomorphism follows from point-wise freeness, where
we put
j_{x}\colon\left\{x\right\}\to U_{k}
. (Note that
u_{x}^{!}\cong u_{x}^{*}[-\dim S_{k}]

for
