.) Then the assertion
follows from direct computation and Proposition
5.17

(cf. Example
4.7
).
∎

For a sequenece
L=(S_{L,1}\subsetneq\dots\subsetneq S_{L,r(L)})

(
S_{L,i}\in\Lambda
,
r(L)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
) and
b\in\mathbb{Z}^{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}
,
we define

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L,r(L)}}}\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits_{b}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{p,*}|_{W_{L}\setminus\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{L,r(L)}}\in D^{b}(W_{L}\setminus\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{L,r(L)},\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L,r(L)}}})

in the same way as
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{p,*}
from

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L,r(L)}}}\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits_{b}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}[2d-p]_{\deg}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L}}

(see above Lemma
5.8
) using truncation
functors and push-forward under open immersions. (Note that

W_{L}\setminus\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{L,r(L)}\subset\bigcup_{S\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cup\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L,r(L)}}}\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S.)

By Lemma
5.8
, we have an isomorphism

IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{p,*}|_{W_{L}\setminus\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{L,r(L)}}\cong\bigoplus_{b\in\mathbb{Z}^{\left\{S_{L,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L)}}}\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L,r(L)}}}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L,r(L)}}}\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits_{b}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{p,*}|_{W_{L}\setminus\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{L,r(L)}}.

Hence we get the following.

Corollary 5.24
.

For a sequenece
L=(S_{L,1}\subsetneq\dots\subsetneq S_{L,r(L)})

(
S_{L,i}\in\Lambda
,
r(L)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
), the tropical intersection
complex
IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{p,*}|_{W_{L}}
is isomorphic to the direct
sum of (usual) truncations of push-forwards of

\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L,r(L)}}}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L,r(L)}}}\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits_{b}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{p,*}|_{W_{L}\setminus\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{L,r(L)}}

under
W_{L}\setminus\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{L,r(L)}\to W_{L}
.

Corollary 5.25
.

The complex
j_{k}^{\epsilon}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{p,*}|_{U_{k}}

(
\epsilon=*,!
) is point-wise free, and the morphism (
5.2
)
is injective for
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}|_{U_{k}}
.

Proof.

By Corollary
5.24
, for an adapted
pair
(L,a)
with
r(L)\geq 1
and
S_{L,r(L)}=S_{k}
, the complex

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{p,*}|_{W_{L}}(a)
is a direct
sum of direct summands in Corollary
5.24
.
Hence the assertion hold.
∎

Theorem 5.26
.

There is a unique LG-quasi-isomorphism

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{p,*}\cong_{LG}D(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*})[2d]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}

in
D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})
whose
restriction to
X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}
is the LG-quasi-isomorphism

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{p,w}[2d-p]_{\deg}\cong_{LG}D(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}_{X_{sm}}^{d-p,w}[d+p]_{\deg})[2d]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}

in Lemma
5.15
.

Proof.

This follows from Corollary
5.23

and Corollary
5.25
.
∎

By Lemma
4.46
, this LG-quasi-isomorphism
gives a quasi-isomorphism

IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{p,*}\cong D(IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*})[2d]_{\deg}.

We put

IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,*}^{p,q}(X):=H^{p+q-2d}R\Gamma_{*}(IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{p,*})

(
*=\emptyset,c
), which is finite dimensional by Corollary
8.31
.

Corollary 5.27
.

For any
p
and
q
,
we have a non-degenerate bilinear map

IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{p,q}(X)\times IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,c}^{d-p,d-q}(X)\to\mathbb{Q}.

6.
Comparisons

Let
X=(\Lambda,w)
of dimension
d
in a tropical toric variety

\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma})
. We assume that
T_{\Sigma}
is smooth and
X

is regular at infinity. In this section, we shall show that
IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{p,q}(X)

is isomorphic to

•

IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits}^{p,q}(X)
(Proposition
6.3
),

•

tropical cohomology
H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,q}(X)
(Proposition
6.12
)
when
X
is e.g., smooth, and

•

IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{p,q}(X^{\prime})
(Proposition
6.15
)
for a tropical variety
X^{\prime}
given by a subdivision of
\Lambda
.

We use notations in Section
5
.

6.1.
Künneth formula and comparison of two constructions

In this subsection, we shall give an LG-quasi-isomorphism

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{p,*}\cong_{LG}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,X}^{p,*}

(Proposition
6.3
). By Lemma
5.6
,
definition of
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}
, and Proposition

5.17
, it suffices to show that

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{p,*}
satisfies Axiom
A_{d-p}
.
In Subsection
3.2
, we have proved it
except Axiom
A_{d-p}
(2) for
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{p,*}

at
S_{k}\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}
with
\dim\sigma_{S_{k}}\geq 1
. In
this case, as we will see, instead of attaching property, computation
of stalks (Lemma
3.22
)
and Künneth formula (Proposition
6.2
)
for
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,X}^{p,*}
are enough to prove
the above LG-quasi-isomorphism.

Remind that
X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}:=U_{1}:=\bigcup_{R\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits R
,

\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}=\left\{S_{2,}\dots,S_{\#\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}+1}\right\}
,
and
U_{k}:=U_{1}\cup\bigcup_{j=2}^{k}\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{j}
. We put
i_{k}\colon U_{k-1}\hookrightarrow U_{k}

and
j_{k}:\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{k}\hookrightarrow U_{k}
embeddings.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
A\in D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(U_{k},\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})
.
We put

B:=D(A)[2d]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}\in D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(U_{k},\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda}).

We assume that
j_{k}^{*}A
and
j_{k}^{*}B
are point-wise free,
and both
A
and
B
satisfies Axiom
A_{d-p}
(1) and
A_{p}

(1), respectively. Then we have a natural LG-quasi-isomorphisms

A\cong\tau_{d-p,S_{k}}Ri_{k*}i_{k}^{*}A,\quad B\cong\tau_{p,S_{k}}Ri_{k*}i_{k}^{*}B.

Proof.

In the same way as Corollary
5.23
,
this follows from direct computation.
∎

Let
V=(\Lambda_{V},w_{V})
(
V=Y,Z
) be a tropical variety regular
at infinity of dimension
d_{V}
in a smooth tropical toric variety

\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma_{V}})
. Let
Y\times Z=(\Lambda_{Y\times Z},w_{Y\times Z})

their product (Definition
2.10
).
For
V=Y,Z
and
S_{V}\in\Lambda_{V}
, we fix an open polyhedral
neighborhood
W_{S_{V}}
of
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{V}
as in Subsection
3.1
.
Then

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{V}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,V}^{d_{V}-p,*}\in D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(V),0]}^{b}(V,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S_{V}}]_{S_{V}\in\Lambda_{V}})

is defined (Definition
5.18
), and we get
their external tensor product (Subsection
4.3
)

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{Y}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,Y}^{d_{Y}-p,*}\overset{L}{\XBox}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{Z}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,Z}^{d_{Z}-q,*}\in D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(Y\times Z),0]}^{b}(Y\times Z,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S_{Y\times Z}}]_{S_{Y\times Z}\in\Lambda_{Y\times Z}}).

Let
W_{S_{Y}\times S_{Z}}:=W_{S_{Y}}\times W_{S_{Z}}
be a product,
which is an open polyhedral neighborhood of

\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits(S_{Y}\times S_{Z})=\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{Y}\times\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{Z}.

We fix an order
\Lambda_{Y\times Z,\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}=\left\{S_{Y\times Z,2},\dots\right\}

such that
S_{Y\times Z,i}\supset S_{Y\times Z,j}
only if
i\leq j
.
We put
U_{Y\times Z,1}:=\bigcup_{R\in\Lambda_{Y\times Z,\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits R
,
and for
2\leq k\leq\#\Lambda_{Y\times Z,\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}+1
, we put

U_{Y\times Z,k}:=U_{Y\times Z,1}\cup\bigcup_{j=2}^{k}\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{Y\times Z,j}.

Proposition 6.2
.

We have a natural LG-quasi-isomorphism

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{p_{Y}+p_{Z}=p_{Y\times Z}}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{Y}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,Y}^{d_{Y}-p_{Y},*}\overset{L}{\XBox}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{Z}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,Z}^{d_{Z}-p_{Z},*}\cong_{LG}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{Y\times Z}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,Y\times Z}^{d_{Y}+d_{Z}-p_{Y\times Z},*}

in

D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(Y\times Z),0],LG}^{b}(Y\times Z,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S_{Y\times Z}}]_{S_{Y\times Z}\in\Lambda_{Y\times Z}}).

In particular, we have a natural quasi-isomorphism

\bigoplus_{p_{Y}+p_{Z}=p_{Y\times Z}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,Y}^{d_{Y}-p_{Y},*}\overset{L}{\XBox}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,Z}^{d_{Z}-p_{Z},*}\cong IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,Y\times Z}^{d_{Y}+d_{Z}-p_{Y\times Z},*}.

Proof.

We shall prove the existence of the LG-quasi-isomorphism on
U_{Y\times Z,k}

by induction on
k\geq 1
. Let
L_{Y\times Z}=(S_{L_{Y\times Z},1}\subsetneq\dots\subsetneq S_{L_{Y\times Z},r(L_{Y\times Z})})

(
S_{L_{Y\times Z},i}\in\Lambda_{Y\times Z}
) and
a_{Y\times Z}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda_{Y\times Z}}

be an adapted pair. We put
S_{L_{Y\times Z},i}=S_{L_{Y},i}\times S_{L_{Z},i}

(
S_{L_{V},i}\in\Lambda_{V}
), and for
V=Y,Z
, we put
L_{V}=(S_{L_{V},1}^{\prime}\subsetneq\dots\subsetneq S_{L_{V},r(L_{V})}^{\prime})

such that
\left\{S_{L_{V},i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L_{Y\times Z})}=\left\{S_{L_{V},j}^{\prime}\right\}_{j=1}^{r(L_{V})}
.
We also put
W_{L_{Y\times Z}}:=\bigcap_{i=1}^{r(L_{Y\times Z})}W_{S_{L_{Y\times Z},i}}
.
When
k=1
, we have

\bigoplus_{p_{Y}+p_{Z}=p_{Y\times Z}}(\mathscr{F}_{Y_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d_{Y}-p_{Y},w_{Y}}\XBox\mathscr{F}_{Z_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d_{Z}-p_{Z},w_{Z}})|_{(Y\times Z)_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}\cong\mathscr{F}_{(Y\times Z)_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d_{Y}+d_{Z}-p_{Y\times Z},w_{Y\times Z}}.

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle((\mathscr{F}_{Y_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d_{Y}-p_{Y},w_{Y}}\XBox\mathscr{F}_{Z_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d_{Z}-p_{Z},w_{Z}})|_{(Y\times Z)_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}\cap\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{Y\times Z}}\mathscr{F}_{(Y\times Z)_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d_{Y}+d_{Z}-p_{Y\times Z},w_{Y\times Z}}(a_{Y\times Z}))|_{(Y\times Z)_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L_{Y\times Z}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle((\mathscr{F}_{Y_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d_{Y}-p_{Y},w_{Y}}\XBox\mathscr{F}_{Z_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d_{Z}-p_{Z},w_{Z}})|_{(Y\times Z)_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}\cap\bigcap_{i=1}^{r(L_{Y\times Z})}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S_{L_{Y\times Z},i}}^{-a_{Y\times Z,S_{L_{Y\times Z},i}}}\mathscr{F}_{(Y\times Z)_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d_{Y}+d_{Z}-p_{Y\times Z},w_{Y\times Z}})|_{(Y\times Z)_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L_{Y\times Z}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigcap_{i=1}^{r(L_{Y\times Z})}(\sum_{a_{Y,i}+a_{Z,i}=a_{Y\times Z,S_{L_{Y\times Z},i}}}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S_{L_{Y},i}}^{-a_{Y,i}}\mathscr{F}_{Y_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d_{Y}-p_{Y},w_{Y}}\XBox\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S_{L_{Z},i}}^{-a_{Z,i}}\mathscr{F}_{Z_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d_{Z}-p_{Z},w_{Z}})|_{(Y\times Z)_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L_{Y\times Z}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{a_{Y}+a_{Z}=a_{Y\times Z}}(\bigcap_{i=1}^{r(L_{Y\times Z})}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S_{L_{Y},i}}^{-a_{Y,i}}\mathscr{F}_{Y_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d_{Y}-p_{Y},w_{Y}}\XBox\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S_{L_{Z},i}}^{-a_{Z,i}}\mathscr{F}_{Z_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d_{Z}-p_{Z},w_{Z}})|_{(Y\times Z)_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L_{Y\times Z}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{Y}}\mathscr{F}_{Y_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d_{Y}-p_{Y},w_{Y}}\XBox\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{Z}}\mathscr{F}_{Z_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d_{Z}-p_{Z},w_{Z}}(a_{Y\times Z})|_{(Y\times Z)_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L_{Y\times Z}}},

where the third equality can be seen as follows. Each
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{S_{L_{V},i}}^{-a_{V,i}}\mathscr{F}_{V_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d_{V}-p_{V},w_{V}}|_{V_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap W_{L_{V}}}

(
V=Y,Z
) is a direct sum of direct summands of the second decomposition
of Lemma
5.8
. Hence the third line
is generated by external products of some direct summands of the decompositions
for
Y
and
Z
. Then it is easy to see that the third line is contained
in the fourth one, i.e., the third equality holds.

We assume that
k\geq 2
and that we already have the LG-quasi-isomorphism
on
U_{Y\times Z,k-1}
. By Lemma
4.18

and Theorem
5.26
, we have

\displaystyle D(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{Y}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,Y}^{d_{Y}-p_{Y},*}\overset{L}{\XBox}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{Z}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,Z}^{d_{Z}-p_{Z},*})[2d_{Y\times Z}]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(Y\times Z)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}

\displaystyle\cong_{LG}

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{Y}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,Y}^{p_{Y},*}\overset{L}{\XBox}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{Z}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,Z}^{p_{Z},*}.

Hence by Corollary
5.25
and
Lemma
6.1
, it suffices to show
that

(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{Y}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,Y}^{d_{Y}-p_{Y},*}\overset{L}{\XBox}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{Z}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,Z}^{d_{Z}-p_{Z},*})|_{U_{Y\times Z,k}}

satisfies Axiom
A_{p_{Y\times Z}}
(1) at
S_{Y\times Z,k}
. We
put
S_{Y\times Z,k}=S_{Y,k}\times S_{Z,k}
(
S_{V,k}\in\Lambda_{V}

(
V=Y,Z
)). We assume that
r(L_{Y\times Z})\geq 1
and
S_{L_{Y\times Z},r(L_{Y\times Z})}=S_{Y\times Z,k}
.
We have

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{m}(j_{Y\times Z,k}^{*}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{Y}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,Y}^{d_{Y}-p_{Y},*}\overset{L}{\XBox}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{Z}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,Z}^{d_{Z}-p_{Z},*})(a_{Y\times Z})

\displaystyle\cong_{L}

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{m_{Y}+m_{Z}=m}\sum^{\prime}_{a_{Y}+a_{Z}=a_{Y\times Z}}\mathscr{H}^{m_{Y}}(j_{Y,k}^{*}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{Y}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,Y}^{d_{Y}-p_{Y},*})(\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\Lambda_{Y}}a_{Y})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\qquad\qquad\quad\XBox\mathscr{H}^{m_{Z}}(j_{Z,k}^{*}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{Z}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,Z}^{d_{Z}-p_{Z},*})(\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\Lambda_{Z}}a_{Z}),

where
\sum^{\prime}_{a_{Y}+a_{Z}=a_{Y\times Z}}
is the sum of
a_{Y},a_{Z}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda_{Y\times Z}}

adapted to
L_{Y},L_{Z}
with
a_{Y}+a_{Z}=a_{Y\times Z}
, and
j_{V,k}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{V,k}\to V

(
V=Y,Z,Y\times Z
) is an inclusion. We shall compute each

(6.1)

\mathscr{H}^{m_{Y}}(j_{Y,k}^{*}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{Y}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,Y}^{d_{Y}-p_{Y},*})(\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\Lambda_{Y}}a_{Y})\XBox\mathscr{H}^{m_{Z}}(j_{Z,k}^{*}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{Z}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,Z}^{d_{Z}-p_{Z},*})(\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\Lambda_{Z}}a_{Z}).

When

m\geq-p_{Y\times Z}-\dim S_{Y\times Z,k}-1+\epsilon(\sigma_{S_{Y\times Z,k}}),

we have

m_{V}\geq-p_{V}-\dim S_{V,k}-1+\epsilon(\sigma_{S_{V,k}})

for
V=Y
or
Z
, hence (
6.1
) is
0
.
We assume

\displaystyle\max\left\{-p_{Y\times Z}-d_{Y\times Z}-\epsilon(\sigma_{S_{Y\times Z,k}}),m_{p_{Y\times Z},S_{Y\times Z,k}}-a_{Y\times Z,S_{Y\times Z,k}}\right\}+1

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle m\leq-p_{Y\times Z}-\dim S_{Y\times Z,k}-2+\epsilon(\sigma_{S_{Y\times Z,k}}),

and shall show that (
6.1
) is contained in

(6.2)

\mathscr{H}^{m}(j_{Y\times Z,k}^{*}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{Y}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,Y}^{d_{Y}-p_{Y},*}\overset{L}{\XBox}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{Z}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,Z}^{d_{Z}-p_{Z},*})(a_{Y\times Z}(S_{Y\times Z,k}=m_{p_{Y\times Z},S_{Y\times Z,k}}-m))

up to
L_{Y\times Z}
-local isomorphisms. We may assume that
m_{V}\geq-p_{V}-d_{V}

for both
V=Y,Z
since otherwise (
6.1
) is

0
. Moreover, we have

-p_{V}-d_{V}-\epsilon(\sigma_{S_{V,k}})+1\leq m_{V}

for
V=Y
or
Z
. We assume that this holds for
Z
. When
Y

also satisfies this inequlity, since

m_{p_{Y\times Z},S_{Y\times Z,k}}-m=m_{p_{Y},S_{Y,k}}-m_{Y}+m_{p_{Z},S_{Z,k}}-m_{Z}+1,

by Axiom
A_{p_{V}}
at
S_{V,k}
for
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{V}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,V}^{d_{V}-p_{V},*}

(
V=Y,Z
), (
6.1
) is contained in (
6.2
).
Hence we may assume that
Y
does not satisfy the inequlity, i.e.,

\epsilon(\sigma_{S_{Y,k}})=0
and
m_{Y}=-p_{Y}-d_{Y}
. In this
case,

m_{p_{Y},S_{Y,k}}-m_{Y}+1=-p_{Y}-\dim S_{Y,k}-\dim\sigma_{S_{Y,k}}+d_{Y}.

Hence by the first inequality in Remark
3.6
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{m_{V_{1}}}(j_{V_{1},k}^{*}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{V_{1}}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,V_{1}}^{d_{V_{1}}-p_{V_{1}},*})(\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\Lambda_{V_{1}}}a_{V_{1}})

\displaystyle\subset

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{m_{V_{1}}}(j_{V_{1},k}^{*}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{V_{1}}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,V_{1}}^{d_{V_{1}}-p_{V_{1}},*})(\min\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{\Lambda_{V_{1}}}a_{V_{1}}(S_{V_{1},k}=m_{p_{V_{1}},S_{V_{1},k}}-m_{V_{1}}+1)).

Hence by Axiom
A_{p_{Z}}
at
S_{Z,k}
for
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{Z}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,Z}^{d_{V}-p_{V},*}
,
(
6.1
) is contained in (
6.2
).
Thus

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{Y}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,Y}^{d_{Y}-p_{Y},*}\overset{L}{\XBox}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{Z}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,Z}^{d_{Z}-p_{Z},*}|_{U_{Y\times Z,k}}

satisfies Axiom
A_{r}
(1) at
S_{Y\times Z,k}
, and we have finished
proof.
∎

Proposition 6.3
.

We have a natural LG-quasi-isomorphism

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{p,*}\cong_{LG}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,X}^{p,*}

in
D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})
.

Proof.

We prove the existence of the LG-quasi-isomorphism on
U_{k}
by
induction on

r:=r(k,X):=\max\left\{\dim\sigma\mid U_{k}\cap N_{\sigma,\mathbb{R}}\neq\emptyset\right\}.

We may assume that
\dim\sigma_{S_{i}}\leq r
for
i\leq k
and

\dim\sigma_{S_{k}}=r
. When
r=0
. i.e.,
U_{k}\subset N_{\mathbb{R}}
,
by induction on
k
, the assertion follows from Proposition
3.28
,
Lemma
5.6
(and Definition of

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}
), and Proposition
5.17
.
We assume that
r\geq 1
. By induction on
k
and Proposition
3.28
,
we have a morphism

(6.3)

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,X}^{p,*}|_{U_{k}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,X}^{p,*}|_{U_{k}}

in
D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(U_{k},\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})

which is an LG-quasi-isomorphism on
U_{k-1}
. A small neighborhood
of
X\cap N_{\sigma_{S_{k}}}\subset X
can be identified with that
of

\left\{\infty\right\}\times\left\{(\infty)^{r-1}\right\}\times(X\cap N_{\sigma_{S_{k}},\mathbb{R}})\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\mathbb{A}^{1})\times\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\mathbb{A}^{r-1})\times(X\cap N_{\sigma_{S_{k}},\mathbb{R}}),

where
X\cap N_{\sigma_{S_{k}},\mathbb{R}}
has a natural structure of a
tropical variety (Remark
3.21
).
Then by the case of
X=\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\mathbb{A}^{1})
(Example
2.8
),
which follows from easy computation and Proposition
5.17
,
hypothesis of induction to
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\mathbb{A}^{r-1})\times(X\cap N_{\sigma_{S_{k}},\mathbb{R}})
,
and Künneth type formula (Lemma
3.22

and Proposition
6.2
), the morphism (
6.3
)
is an LG-quasi-isomorphism on
U_{k}
.
∎

In particular, we have a natural isomorphism
IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{geom}}\nolimits,\epsilon}^{p,q}(X)\cong IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,\epsilon}^{p,q}(X)

(
\epsilon=\emptyset,c
). We simply denote them by
IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\epsilon}^{p,q}(X)

(or
IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\epsilon}^{p,q}(X;\mathbb{Q})
).

6.2.
Comparison to tropical cohomology

In this subsection, we give a natural morphism
H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,q}(X;\mathbb{Q})\to IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,q}(X;\mathbb{Q})

from tropical cohomology
H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,q}(X;\mathbb{Q})
, introduced by Itenberg-Katzarkov-Mikhalkin-Zharkov

[
IKMZ19
]
, and
show that it is an isomorphism when Poincaré-Verdier duality
holds for tropical cohomology. Let
M
be a free
\mathbb{Z}
-module of
finite rank such that
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}\nolimits K[M]\subset T_{\Sigma}
is the dense
torus, where
K
is the base field.

Definition 6.4
.

For
R\in\Lambda
, we put

F^{p}(R):=\bigwedge^{p}(M\cap\sigma_{R}^{\perp})\otimes\mathbb{Q}/\bigcap_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d}\\
R\subset P\end{subarray}}\mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}\nolimits(\bigwedge^{p}(M\cap\sigma_{R}^{\perp})\otimes\mathbb{Q}\to\bigwedge^{p}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}P,\mathbb{Q})).

For
R_{1},R_{2}\in\Lambda
with
R_{1}\subset R_{2}
, the inclusion

M\cap\sigma_{R_{1}}^{\perp}\hookrightarrow M\cap\sigma_{R_{2}}^{\perp}

induces a map
F^{p}(R_{1})\to F^{p}(R_{2})
. We put
\mathscr{F}^{p}=\mathscr{F}_{X}^{p}

a constructible sheaf on
X
given by
\mathscr{F}^{p}|_{\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits R}=\underline{F^{p}(R)}_{\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits R}

(
R\in\Lambda
) and natural maps
F^{p}(R_{1})\to F^{p}(R_{2})

(
R_{1},R_{2}\in\Lambda
with
R_{1}\subset R_{2}
).

Remark 6.5
.

Similarly, we can define a sheaf
\mathscr{F}^{p,w}
on
X
by
F^{p,w}(S):=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(F_{p,w}(S),\mathbb{Q})

(
S\in\Lambda
) (see Definition
3.1
). When
X
is a tropicalization
of a smooth complex algebraic variety with respect to the trivial
valuation, the stalks of
\mathscr{F}^{p}
and
\mathscr{F}^{p,w}
at some points
approximate the highest weight graded quotients of some strata. See
Introduction for details. We leave the reader to compute the isomorphism
there.

Definition 6.6
.

Tropical homology
H_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{BM}}\nolimits}(X;\mathbb{Q})
(resp. cohomology
H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\epsilon}^{p,q}(X;\mathbb{Q})

(
\epsilon=\emptyset,c
)) is defined as homology (resp. cohomology)
of singular or cellular chains (resp. cochains) with coefficients
in the dual of
F^{p}
(resp.
F^{p}
-coefficients) (
[
IKMZ19
]
).
Tropical homology (resp. cohomology) is canonically isomorphic to
sheaf cohomology of Verdier dual of
\mathscr{F}^{p}
(
[
GS23
, Theorem, 4.20]
)
(resp. sheaf cohomology of
\mathscr{F}^{p}
(
[
MZ14
, Section 12.4]
)),
i.e.,

\displaystyle H_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{BM}}\nolimits}(X;\mathbb{Q})

\displaystyle\cong H^{-p-q}(R\Gamma D(\mathscr{F}_{X}^{p}[2d-p]_{\deg})[2d]_{\deg})

\displaystyle\cong H^{-p-q}(\Gamma\mathop{\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(\mathscr{F}_{X}^{p}[2d-p]_{\deg},\Delta_{X}^{\Lambda,*})[2d]_{\deg})

\displaystyle H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\epsilon}^{p,q}(X;\mathbb{Q})

\displaystyle\cong H^{q-2d+p}(R\Gamma_{\epsilon}\mathscr{F}_{X}^{p}[2d-p]_{\deg}),

where
\Delta_{X}^{\Lambda,*}
is the complex of sheaves of singular
simplexes with
\mathbb{Q}
-coefficients respecting
\Lambda
(
[
GS23
, Appendix A]
),
and the second isomorphism is given in proof of
[
GS23
, Theorem, 4.20]
.

There is a natural injective morphism
\mathscr{F}^{p}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}
,
whose restriction to
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits P
(
P\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits,d}
) is a natural
isomorphism of constant sheaves given by
\bigwedge^{p}M\otimes\mathbb{Q}\to\bigwedge^{p}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(\mathop{\mathrm{Tan}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Q}}P,\mathbb{Q})
.
For
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}
, we define a sheaf
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{p}(a)

on
X
by defining
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{p}(a)(U)
as the inverse
image of
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}(a)(U\cap X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits})

for open subsets
U\subset X
. Then inclusion morphisms give

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{p}:=\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda}}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{p}(a)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda}).

Axiom
A_{d-p}
(1) at
S_{k}
for
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{p}[2d-p]_{\deg}

holds trivially when
\dim\sigma_{S_{k}}=0
and easily follows from
definition when
\dim\sigma_{S_{k}}\geq 1
. Hence by Corollary
4.59
,
we have a natural morphism

\displaystyle\varphi^{p}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{p}[2d-p]_{\deg}\to\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{p,*}

in
D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})
.

We recall cap product given in
[
GS23
, Subsection 4.6]
.
The fundamental class
[X]\in IH_{d,d}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{BM}}\nolimits}(X)
in Subsection

5.1
gives an element

H_{d,d}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{BM}}\nolimits}(X;\mathbb{Q})\cong H^{0}(\Gamma\mathop{\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(\mathscr{F}_{X}^{d}[d]_{\deg},\Delta_{X}^{\Lambda,*}))\cong\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q})}(\mathscr{F}_{X}^{d}[d]_{\deg},\Delta_{X}^{\Lambda,*}).

It is also denoted by
[X]
. We put
[X]\cap-\colon H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,c}^{d,d}(X)\to\mathbb{Q}

the induced morphism. Using singular cochains with
F^{d}
-coefficients,
the cap product
[X]\cap-\colon H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,c}^{d,d}(X)\to\mathbb{Q}
is given
by the natural pairing of singular cochains with
F^{d}
-coefficients
and singular chains with coefficients in the dual of
F^{d}
-coefficients.
The wedge product of
\bigwedge^{p}(M\cap\sigma_{R}^{\perp})\otimes\mathbb{Q}

(
R\in\Lambda
) induces a morphism

-\wedge-\colon(\mathscr{F}^{p}[2d-p]_{\deg}\otimes\mathscr{F}^{d-p}[d+p]_{\deg})[-2d]_{\deg}\to\mathscr{F}^{d}[d]_{\deg},

which induces a morphism

-\cup-\colon H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,q}(X)\otimes H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,c}^{d-p,d-q}(X)\to H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{d,d}(X).

Composition of
-\wedge-
with
[X]\cap-\colon\mathscr{F}^{d}[d]_{\deg}\to\Delta_{X}^{\Lambda,*}

induces a morphism

\displaystyle[X]\cap-\colon\mathscr{F}^{p}[2d-p]_{\deg}

\displaystyle\to\mathop{\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(\mathscr{F}^{d-p}[d+p]_{\deg},\Delta_{X}^{\Lambda,*})[2d]_{\deg}

\displaystyle\cong D(\mathscr{F}^{d-p}[d+p]_{\deg})[2d]_{\deg},

where the quasi-isomorphism is given by
[
GS23
, Proposition A.9]
.
We also denote the induced morphism on cohomology groups by
[X]\cap-
.
Then by construction, we have the following commutative diagram

Similarly to Lemma
5.13
,
for
L=(S_{L,1}\subsetneq\dots\subsetneq S_{L,r(L)})
(

,

the cap product

gives a morphism

and

)). Hence by isomorphism ((

))
in proof of Lemma (

) for

instead of

and

,
we have a morphism

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{p}[2d-p]_{\deg}

\displaystyle\to\mathop{gr\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{d-p}[d+p]_{\deg},\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{\Lambda}}\Delta_{X}^{\Lambda,*})[2d]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}

\displaystyle\to D(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{d-p}[d+p]_{\deg})[2d]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits},

where the second morphism is given by Lemma

.

Lemma 6.7
.

We have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{p}[2d-p]_{\deg}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi^{p}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces D(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{d-p}[d+p]_{\deg})[2d]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}}
\textstyle{Ri_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\hookrightarrow X,*}D(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{d-p}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}[d+p]_{\deg})[2d]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}}

in

whose
morphisms in the second column gives a commutative diagram

\textstyle{D(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{d-p}[d+p]_{\deg})[2d]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Ri_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\hookrightarrow X,*}D(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{d-p}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}[d+p]_{\deg})[2d]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits},}

where

is the open embedding.

Proof.

The commutativity on
U_{1}
follows from construction, and that
on

(

) holds by induction on

.
∎

Lemma 6.8
.

The natural map

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})}(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{p},D(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{d-p})[-d]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits})

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})}(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{p},Ri_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\hookrightarrow X,*}D(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{d-p}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}})[-d]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits})

is injective.

Proof.

It suffices to show that for any
k\geq 2
, the natural map

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(U_{k},\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})}(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{p}|_{U_{k}},D(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{d-p}|_{U_{k}})[-d]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits})

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(U_{k-1},\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})}(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{p}|_{U_{k-1}},D(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{d-p}|_{U_{k-1}})[-d]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits})

is injective. By applying
j_{k*}j_{k}^{!}\to\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}\nolimits\to Ri_{k*}i_{k}^{*}\to^{[1]}\cdot

to
D(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{d-p}|_{U_{k}})[-d]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}
,
it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(U_{k},\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})}(j_{k}^{*}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{p}|_{U_{k}},j_{k}^{!}D(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{d-p}|_{U_{k}})[-d]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0],LG}^{b}(U_{k},\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda})}(j_{k}^{*}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{p}|_{U_{k}},D(j_{k}^{*}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{d-p}|_{U_{k}})[-d]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits})

is
0
. This holds since

\mathscr{H}^{m}(D(j_{k}^{*}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{d-p}|_{U_{k}})[-d]_{\deg})=0

for
m\neq d-\dim S_{k}
and
d-\dim S_{k}\geq 1
.
∎

As a direct corollary of the above two lemmas, we have the following.

Corollary 6.9
.

0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.22836pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{D(\varphi^{d-p})[2d]_{\deg}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 45.59808pt\raise-65.98004pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\times}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 61.3334pt\raise-65.98004pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,c}^{d-p,d-q}(X;\mathbb{Q})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 187.55896pt\raise-65.98004pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 157.55896pt\raise-65.98004pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 187.55896pt\raise-65.98004pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{Q}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

∎

In the rest of this subsection, we assume that Poincaré-Verdier
duality holds for tropical cohomology of
X
, i.e., the cap product

[X]\cap-
induces a quasi-isomorphism
\mathscr{F}^{p}\cong D(\mathscr{F}^{d-p})[-d]_{\deg}

(
0\leq p\leq d
) on
X
(e.g,
X
is smooth (i.e., locally matroidal)
(Jell-Shaw-Smacka (
[
JSS19
, Theorem 2]
),
Gross-Shokrieh (
[
GS23
, Theorem D]
))
(cf.
[
AP24a
, Theorem 1.2 and 1.3]
)).

Lemma 6.11
.

The isomorphism

\mathscr{F}^{p}\cong D(\mathscr{F}^{d-p})[-d]_{\deg}
induces an LG-quasi-isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{p}[2d-p]_{\deg}\cong D(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{d-p}[d+p]_{\deg})[2d]_{\deg}[\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X)]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}.

Proof.

Proof can be given in the same way as Proposition
5.15
.
(In particular, analogs of Lemma
5.8

and Lemma
5.13
hold
for
\mathscr{F}^{*}
instead of
\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{*,w}
.)
∎

Proposition 6.12
.

When
\mathscr{F}^{p}\cong D(\mathscr{F}^{d-p})[-d]_{\deg}
,
the morphism

\varphi^{p}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{p}[2d-p]_{\deg}\to\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{p,*}

is an LG-quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

We prove the assertion on
U_{k}
by induction on
k\geq 1
. When

k=1
, the assertion easily follows from direct computation (see
proof of Lemma
5.4
). We assume

k\geq 2
. Similarly to Lemma
5.8
,
the sheaf
\mathscr{F}^{p}|_{W_{L}}
has a decomposition. In particular, an
analog of Corollary
5.25
holds
for
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{p}[2d-p]_{\deg}
. Since
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{p}[2d-p]_{\deg}|_{U_{k}}

and
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{d-p}[d+p]_{\deg}|_{U_{k}}
satisfy Axiom

A_{p}
(1), by Lemma
6.1

and Lemma
6.11
, the assertion
holds on
U_{k}
.
∎

In paticular, we have a quasi-isomorphism
\varphi^{p}\colon\mathscr{F}^{p}[2d-p]_{\deg}\cong IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{p,*}

on
X
, and we have

\displaystyle H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\epsilon}^{p,q}(X;\mathbb{Q})\cong IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\epsilon}^{p,q}(X;\mathbb{Q}),\quad H_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{BM}}\nolimits}(X;\mathbb{Q})\cong IH_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{BM}}\nolimits}(X;\mathbb{Q}).

6.3.
Independence of polyhedral structures

Let
\Lambda^{\prime}
be a subdivision of
\Lambda
. We put
w^{\prime}=(w^{\prime}_{P^{\prime}})_{P^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{d}}

and
w^{\prime}_{P^{\prime}}:=w_{P}
, where
P\in\Lambda_{d}
is the unique polyhedron
containing
P^{\prime}
. Then
X^{\prime}:=(\Lambda^{\prime},w^{\prime})
is a tropical variety
of dimension
d
in
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma})
. We have
X=X^{\prime}\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma})

as subsets (by abuse of notation). We assume that
X^{\prime}
is also regular
at infinity. In this subsection, we shall show that there is a natural
quasi-isomorphism

IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,X}^{d-p,*}\cong IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,X^{\prime}}^{d-p,*}.

We put
X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}^{\prime}:=U^{\prime}_{1}:=\bigcup_{R^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}^{\prime}}\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits R^{\prime}
.
We define

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda^{\prime}}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}^{\prime}}^{d-p,w}

\displaystyle\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}^{\prime},\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S^{\prime}}]_{S^{\prime}\in\Lambda^{\prime}})

similarly to
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}
. We put
\iota_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\colon X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}
.
We have a natural morphism

\iota_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}^{*}\colon\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}\to\iota_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits*}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}^{\prime}}^{d-p,w}(\cong\iota_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits*}\iota_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}^{*}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}).

For
\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\prime}\subset 2^{X}
, we define

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\prime}}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits},\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S^{\circ}}]_{S^{\circ}\in\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\prime}})

by

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\prime}}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}(a)\subset\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}(a|_{\Lambda})\cap(\iota_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}^{*})^{-1}(\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda^{\prime}}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}(a|_{\Lambda^{\prime}}))

(
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\prime}}
) and inclusions, where
-|_{\Xi}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\prime}}\to\mathbb{Z}^{\Xi}

(
\Xi=\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}
) is the projection. We put

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\prime}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}:=

\displaystyle\tau_{p,S_{\#\Lambda+1}}Ri_{\#\Lambda+1*}\circ\dots\circ\tau_{p,S_{2}}Ri_{2*}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\prime}}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}[d+p]_{\deg}

\displaystyle\in

\displaystyle D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X,X^{\prime}),0]}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S^{\circ}}]_{S^{\circ}\in\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\prime}}),

where
\tau_{p,S_{i}}
is as in Subsection
5.2
,
and we put

\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X,X^{\prime}):=\sum_{S^{\circ}\in\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\prime}}(\dim S^{\circ}+2\dim\sigma_{S^{\circ}})e_{S^{\circ}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\prime}}.

By construction, we have a quasi-isomorphism

\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda^{\prime}\setminus\Lambda}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\prime}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}\cong\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,X}^{d-p,*}\in D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X),0]}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S}]_{S\in\Lambda}).

Lemma 6.13
.

There are open polyhedral
neighborhoods
W_{S^{\prime}}^{\prime}
of
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S^{\prime}
(
S^{\prime}\in\Lambda^{\prime}
) such
that for an adapted pair
L^{\prime}=(S^{\prime}_{L^{\prime},1}\subsetneq\dots\subsetneq S^{\prime}_{L^{\prime},r(L^{\prime})})

(
S^{\prime}_{L^{\prime},i}\in\Lambda^{\prime}
,
r(L^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
) and
a^{\prime}=(a^{\prime}_{S^{\circ}})_{S^{\circ}\in\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\prime}}

(i.e.,
a^{\prime}_{S^{\circ}}\geq 0
for
S^{\circ}\in(\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\prime})\setminus\left\{S_{L^{\prime},i}^{\prime}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L^{\prime})}
)
satisfying
\dim S^{\prime}_{L^{\prime},r(L^{\prime})}\neq\dim S_{L^{\prime}}
, we have injections

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{m}((j_{S_{L^{\prime}}}^{\epsilon}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\prime}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*})|_{\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{L^{\prime}}\cap\bigcap_{i=1}^{r(L^{\prime})}W_{S^{\prime}_{L^{\prime},i}}^{\prime}}(a^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{m}((j_{S_{L^{\prime}}}^{\epsilon}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\prime}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*})|_{\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{L^{\prime}}\cap\bigcap_{i=1}^{r(L^{\prime})}W_{S^{\prime}_{L^{\prime},i}}^{\prime}}(a^{\prime}(S_{L^{\prime},r(L^{\prime})}^{\prime}=0,\ S_{L^{\prime}}=a^{\prime}_{S_{L^{\prime},r(L^{\prime})}^{\prime}})))

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{m}((j_{S_{L^{\prime}}}^{\epsilon}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\prime}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*})|_{\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{L^{\prime}}\cap\bigcap_{i=1}^{r(L^{\prime})}W_{S^{\prime}_{L^{\prime},i}}^{\prime}}(a^{\prime}(S_{L^{\prime},r(L^{\prime})}^{\prime}=a^{\prime}_{S_{L^{\prime},r(L^{\prime})}^{\prime}}+\mathop{\mathrm{codim}}\nolimits_{S_{L^{\prime}}}S_{L^{\prime},r(L^{\prime})}^{\prime})))

(
\epsilon=*,!
), where
S_{L^{\prime}}\in\Lambda
is the polyhedron such
that
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{L^{\prime}}\supset\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S^{\prime}_{L^{\prime},r(L^{\prime})}
, the morphism
j_{S_{L^{\prime}}}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S_{L^{\prime}}\to X

is the inclusion,

a^{\prime}(S_{L^{\prime},r(L^{\prime})}^{\prime}=0,\ S_{L^{\prime}}=a^{\prime}_{S_{L^{\prime},r(L^{\prime})}^{\prime}})\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\prime}}

is the element whose
S_{L^{\prime},r(L^{\prime})}^{\prime}
-component is
0
,
S_{L^{\prime}}
-component
is
a^{\prime}_{S^{\prime}_{L^{\prime},r(L^{\prime})}}
, and the other components are same as
a^{\prime}
,
and

a^{\prime}(S_{L^{\prime},r(L^{\prime})}^{\prime}=a^{\prime}_{S_{L^{\prime},r(L^{\prime})}^{\prime}}+\mathop{\mathrm{codim}}\nolimits_{S_{L^{\prime}}}S_{L^{\prime},r(L^{\prime})}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\prime}}

is the element whose
S_{L^{\prime},r(L^{\prime})}^{\prime}
-component is
a^{\prime}_{S_{L^{\prime},r(L^{\prime})}^{\prime}}+\mathop{\mathrm{codim}}\nolimits_{S_{L^{\prime}}}S_{L^{\prime},r(L^{\prime})}^{\prime}
,
and the other components are same as
a^{\prime}
.

Proof.

In the same way as Lemma
5.8
, there
is a decomposition

\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L^{\prime}}}}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap\bigcap_{i=1}^{r(L^{\prime})}W_{S^{\prime}_{L^{\prime},i}}^{\prime}}\cong\bigoplus_{b\in\mathbb{Z}^{\left\{S^{\prime}_{L^{\prime},i}\right\}_{i=1}^{r(L^{\prime})}\cup\left\{S_{L^{\prime}}\right\}}}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda_{\supsetneq S_{L^{\prime}}}}\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits_{b}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}\cap\bigcap_{i=1}^{r(L^{\prime})}W_{S^{\prime}_{L^{\prime},i}}^{\prime}},

where direct summands of the right-hand side are defined similarly
to above Lemma
5.8
. Then we have a
decomposition of
IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,X}^{d-p,*}|_{\bigcap_{i=1}^{r(L^{\prime})}W_{S^{\prime}_{L^{\prime},i}}^{\prime}}

similar to Corollary
5.24
. Then
cohomology groups in the assertion are direct sums of cohomology groups
of direct summands, and the assertion immediately follows from direct
computation. (Note that we can take
W_{S^{\prime}_{L^{\prime},r(L^{\prime})}}^{\prime}\subset W_{S_{L^{\prime}}}
,
since
a^{\prime}
is adapted to
L^{\prime}
, we have
a^{\prime}_{S_{L^{\prime}}}\geq 0
, and
we have
\sigma_{S_{L^{\prime}}}=\sigma_{S^{\prime}_{L^{\prime},r(L^{\prime})}}
.)
∎

Remark 6.14
.

By construction,
\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\prime}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}

satisfies analogs of Axiom
A_{p}
(1) and (2) at each
S_{k}\in\Lambda_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}

for an adapted pair
L^{\prime}=(S^{\prime}_{L^{\prime},1}\subsetneq\dots\subsetneq S^{\prime}_{L^{\prime},r(L^{\prime})-1}\subsetneq S_{k})

(
S^{\prime}_{L^{\prime},i}\in\Lambda^{\prime}
) and
a^{\circ}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\prime}}
.
By decompositions in proof of Lemma
6.13

and Lemma
5.21
, it also satisfies
an analog of Axiom
A_{p}
(2)
′
.

Proposition 6.15
.

We have a natural
LG-quasi-isomorphism

\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda\setminus\Lambda^{\prime}}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\prime}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}\cong\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda^{\prime}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,X^{\prime}}^{d-p,*}

in
D_{c,[-\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits(X^{\prime}),0],LG}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}[T_{S^{\prime}}]_{S^{\prime}\in\Lambda^{\prime}})
.

Proof.

We have a natural LG-isomorphism

\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda\setminus\Lambda^{\prime}}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\prime}}\mathscr{F}_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w}|_{X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}^{\prime}}\cong\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda^{\prime}}\mathscr{F}_{X^{\prime}_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}}^{d-p,w^{\prime}}

on
X_{\mathop{\mathrm{sm}}\nolimits}^{\prime}
. By Proposition
5.17
,
it suffices to show that
\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda\setminus\Lambda^{\prime}}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\prime}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}

satisfies Axiom
A_{p}
(1) and (2)
′
at each
S^{\prime}\in\Lambda^{\prime}_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}
.
This follows from Remark
5.20
, Lemma
6.13
,
and Remark
6.14
directly. (Note
that for
S\in\Lambda
and
S^{\prime}\in\Lambda^{\prime}
with
\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S^{\prime}\subset\mathop{\mathrm{rel.int}}\nolimits S
,
we have
m_{p,S}+\mathop{\mathrm{codim}}\nolimits_{S}S^{\prime}=m_{p,S^{\prime}}
.)
∎

In particular, we have

\displaystyle IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,X}^{d-p,*}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,X}^{d-p,*}

\displaystyle\cong\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\prime}}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda\cup\Lambda^{\prime}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits}^{d-p,*}

\displaystyle\cong\mathop{\mathrm{For}}\nolimits_{\Lambda^{\prime}}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}\nolimits_{*}^{\Lambda^{\prime}}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,X^{\prime}}^{d-p,*}

\displaystyle=IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,X^{\prime}}^{d-p,*}.

7.
Algebraicity

In this section, we shall compare tropical intersection homology and
algebraic cycles with rational coefficients modulo numerical equivalence.
More generally, we study tropical intersection homology of natural
compactifications of tropical fans. To give examples, we shall also
see the existence of good blow-ups.

Let
(\Lambda,w)
be a tropical variety of dimension
d
in
N_{\mathbb{R}}\cong\mathbb{R}^{n}

with a unimodular fan
\Lambda
. We put
T_{\Lambda}
the smooth
toric variety corresponding to
\Lambda
, and
X:=\overline{(\Lambda,w)}:=(\overline{\Lambda},w)

the closure in
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda})
, i.e., a tropical variety regular
at infinity of dimension d with
\overline{\Lambda}:=\left\{\overline{P}\cap\overline{N_{\lambda,\mathbb{R}}}\right\}_{P\in\Lambda,\lambda\in\Lambda},

and
w_{\overline{P}}:=w_{P}
(
P\in\Lambda_{d}
). We put

\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits_{(\Lambda,w)}^{p}(T_{\Lambda}):=\left\{\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits^{p}(T_{\Lambda})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\mid(\Lambda,w)(\alpha\cdot\beta)=0\ (\beta\in\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits^{d-p}(T_{\Lambda})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})\right\},

where
\alpha\cdot\beta
is intersection product of
CH^{*}(T_{\Lambda})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
,
and
(\Lambda,w)
is considered as a Minkowski weight
(\Lambda,w)\in\mathop{\mathrm{MW}}\nolimits_{d}(\Lambda)\cong\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits^{d}(T_{\Lambda}),\mathbb{Z})
.
See
[
CLS11
]
for toric geometry.

Amini-Piquerez (
[
AP24b
, Theorem 1.1]
)
proved that there is an isomorphism
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits^{p}(T_{\Lambda})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\cong H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,p}(X;\mathbb{Q})

which compatible with the pairing given by
(\alpha,\beta)\mapsto(\Lambda,w)(\alpha\cdot\beta)

and that given by the fundamental class
[X]\cap-
(see Subsection

6.2
), and
H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,q}(X;\mathbb{Q})=0

for
p\leq q-1
. The following analog is the main result in this
section.

Theorem 7.1
.

For
p\geq 0
, the natural
map
H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,p}(X;\mathbb{Q})\to IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,p}(X;\mathbb{Q})
given by
\mathscr{F}_{X}^{p}[2d-p]_{\deg}\to IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,X}^{p,*}

(see Subsection
6.2
)
induces an isomorphism

\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits^{p}(T_{\Lambda})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}/\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits_{(\Lambda,w)}^{p}(T_{\Lambda})\cong IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,p}(X;\mathbb{Q}),

and we have
IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,q}(X;\mathbb{Q})=0
for
p\neq q
. Moreover,
the pairing of
IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,p}(X;\mathbb{Q})
coincides with that of
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits^{p}(T_{\Lambda})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}/\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits_{(\Lambda,w)}^{p}(T_{\Lambda})

given by
(\alpha,\beta)\mapsto(\Lambda,w)(\alpha\cdot\beta)
.

Proof.

The assertion directly follows from Corollary
6.10
,
Lemma
7.5
, Lemma
7.7
,
and Lemma
7.9
.
∎

Remark 7.2
.

To prove Theorem
7.1
, by Poincaré
duality (Corollary
5.27
) and
the isomorphism
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits^{p}(T_{\Lambda})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\cong H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,p}(X;\mathbb{Q})

(
[
AP24b
, Theorem 1.1]
)
of Amini-Piquerez, it suffices to show that the natural map
H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,p}(X;\mathbb{Q})\twoheadrightarrow IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,p}(X;\mathbb{Q})

is surjective, and
IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,q}(X;\mathbb{Q})=0
for
p\leq q-1
. However,
computations of
H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,p}(X;\mathbb{Q})
in Amini-Piquerez’s paper
(
[
AP24b
, Subsection 2.4, 2.5, 2.7]
)
is elementary but complicated. For readers convenience, we prove Theorem

7.1
without directly using
Amini-Piquerez’s isomorphism
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits^{p}(T_{\Lambda})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\cong H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,p}(X;\mathbb{Q})

but using an essentially same (and technically different) method.

Corollary 7.3
.

Let
T_{\Sigma}
be a smooth toric
variety over a field, and
Y\subset T_{\Sigma}
a smooth irreducible
proper variety which is a tropical compactification (
[
Tev07
, Definition 1.1]
)
(i.e.,
Y\cap O(\sigma)
(
\sigma\in\Sigma
) is non-empty and of
codimension
=\dim\sigma
in
Y
, where
O(\sigma)\subset T_{\Sigma}

is the torus orbit corresponding to
\sigma\in\Sigma
). We assume
that the natural morphism

\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits^{*}(T_{\Sigma})\otimes\mathbb{Q}\to\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits}^{*}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}

is surjective, where
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits}^{*}(Y)
is the Chow group modulo
numerical equivalence.

Then we have

IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,p}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(Y);\mathbb{Q})\cong\begin{cases}\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits}^{*}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}&(p=q)\\
0&(p\neq q)\end{cases},

where
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(Y)\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Sigma})
is the tropicalization
with respect to the trivial valuation of the base field (see
[
GRW17
, Subsection 3.3 and 3.5]
).

Proof.

By
[
KP11
, Lemma 2.3]
,
we have
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\varphi(Y))=\overline{\deg(\varphi^{*}(-))}
, where

\deg(\varphi^{*}(-))\in\mathop{\mathrm{MW}}\nolimits_{\dim Y}(\Sigma)
is the Minkowski weight,
considered as a tropical fan, given by the composition of the pull-back

\varphi^{*}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits^{*}(T_{\Sigma})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\to\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits^{*}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}

and
\deg\colon\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits^{\dim Y}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\to\mathbb{Q}
. (Note that the
definition of tropicalizations used in [loc.cit.] coincides with
the one in
[
GRW17
, Subsection 3.3 and 3.5]

by
[
Gub13
, Theorem 5.6]
). Then
the assertion easily follows from projection formula and Theorem
7.1
.
∎

Example 7.4
.

For a smooth irreducible projective variety

Y_{0}
over an infinite field, there is a blow-up
Y\to Y_{0}

with a closed immersion
\varphi\colon Y\hookrightarrow T_{\Sigma}

to a smooth toric variety
T_{\Sigma}
satisfying the assumptions
of Corollary
7.3
.

This can be given in the following way. By Proposition
7.11

(which will be proved later in this section), there exists a composition

Y\to Y_{0}
of blow-ups at smooth centers such that
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits}^{*}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}

is generated by the Chern classes of (finitely many) very ample line
bundles
L_{i}
on
Y
as a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra. Let
Y\hookrightarrow\prod_{i}\mathbb{P}(L_{i})

be the product of the closed immersions to the projective spaces given
by
L_{i}
. We fix a generic structure of a toric variety of each

\mathbb{P}(L_{i})
, and hence fix a structure of a smooth projective toric
variety of
\prod_{i}\mathbb{P}(L_{i})
, denoted by
T_{\Sigma_{1}}
. Then
by Bertini’s theorem the pull-back of toric divisors of
T_{\Sigma_{1}}

is simple normal crossing divisors of
Y
. Hence
Y\subset T_{\Sigma}

is a required one, where
T_{\Sigma}\subset T_{\Sigma_{1}}
is the
minimal open toric subvariety containing
Y
.

We shall prove lemmas (Lemma
7.5
,
Lemma
7.7
, and Lemma
7.9
)
for Theorem
7.1
. Proof of Theorem

7.1
is similar to
[
AP24b
, Theorem 1.1]
.
We shall use a spectral sequence for
IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{*,*}
which is
an analog of the one for tropical cohomology used there, and is similar
to the one used to show algebraicity of singular cohomology of smooth
projective toric varieties (
[
CLS11
, Section 12.3]

and
[
Tot14
, Theorem 4]
).
It is also similar to coniveau speactral sequences (see e.g.,
[
CTHK97
]
),
which is used to show
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits^{p}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\cong H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,p}(X;\mathbb{Q})

and
H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,q}(X,\mathbb{Q})=0
(
p\leq q-1
) for smooth algebraic
varieties
X
over trivially valued fields (
[
Mik20
]
).
To show compatibility of pairings, we shall use compatibility of tropical
cohomology and singular cohomology of toric varieties. This can be
considered as a simpler case of
[
IKMZ19
, Theorem 1.1]
.
For this purpose, we shall also use the spectral sequences for tropical
cohomology
H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{*,*}
and singular cohomology
H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{*}

.

Let
-\log\lvert\cdot\rvert\colon T_{\Lambda}(\mathbb{C})\to\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda})

be the disjoint union of maps

-\log\lvert\cdot\rvert\colon O(\lambda)(\mathbb{C})=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(M\cap\lambda^{\perp},\mathbb{C}^{\times})\to\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(M\cap\lambda^{\perp},\mathbb{R})=N_{\lambda,\mathbb{R}}

(
\lambda\in\Lambda
) given by composition with
-\log\lvert\cdot\rvert\colon\mathbb{C}^{\times}\to\mathbb{R}
.
Then we get a morphism

\bigoplus_{s\geq 0}\mathscr{F}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda})}^{s}[-s]_{\deg}\to(-\log\lvert\cdot\rvert)_{*}C_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits,T_{\Lambda}(\mathbb{C})}^{*}(-;\mathbb{C})

of complexes of sheaves on
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda})
given by

\mathscr{F}^{s}\ni\sum_{I}a_{I}m_{I,1}\wedge\dots\wedge m_{I,s}\mapsto(\gamma\mapsto(\frac{-1}{2\pi i})^{s}\sum_{I}a_{I}\int_{\gamma}d\log m_{I,1}\wedge\dots\wedge d\log m_{I,s})

(
a_{I}\in\mathbb{Q}
,
m_{I,j}\in M
), where
m_{I,j}\in M
is also considered
as meromorphic function on
T_{\Lambda}(\mathbb{C})
, and
C_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits,T_{\Lambda}(\mathbb{C})}^{*}(-;R)

is the complex of flabby sheaves of smooth singular cochains with

R
-coefficients. This is well-defined by definition of
\mathscr{F}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda})}^{s}

(see Subsection
6.2

for
\mathscr{F}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda})}^{s}
). This gives a map

\bigoplus_{p+q=r}H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,q}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda});\mathbb{Q})\to H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{r}(T_{\Lambda}(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{C}).

The first assertion in the following lemma is a simpler case of
[
IKMZ19
, Theorem 1.1]
.
For readers convenience, we give a proof here.

Lemma 7.5
.

•

For
r\geq 0
, the above map induces an isomorphism

\bigoplus_{p+q=r}H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,q}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda});\mathbb{Q})\cong H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{r}(T_{\Lambda}(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q}).

Moreover, for
p,q\geq 0
, this gives isomoprhisms

H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,q}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda});\mathbb{Q})\cong\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}\nolimits_{2p}^{W}H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{p+q}(T_{\Lambda}(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q}),

where
\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}\nolimits_{*}^{W}H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{p+q}(T_{\Lambda}(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q})
is the graded
quotient of the weight filtration of the mixed Hodge structure.

•

For
p\geq 0
, the natural map

H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,p}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda});\mathbb{Q})\to IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,p}(X;\mathbb{Q})

induced by

\mathscr{F}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda})}^{p}[2\dim X-p]\to i_{X,*}\mathscr{F}_{X}^{p}[2\dim X-p]\to i_{X,*}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,X}^{p,*}

is surjective, and
IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,p}(X;\mathbb{Q})=0
for
p\leq q-1
,
where
i_{X}\colon X\hookrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda})
is an embedding.

Proof.

We put

T^{p}:=\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\Lambda\\
\dim\lambda=p\end{subarray}}\overline{N_{\lambda,\mathbb{R}}}\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda}),

i_{p}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda})\setminus T^{p}\hookrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda})

an open immersion, and
j_{p}\colon T^{p}\hookrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda})

its complement. By applying
H^{*}(R\Gamma-)
and
j_{p+1*}j_{p+1}^{!}\to\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}\nolimits\to Ri_{p+1*}i_{p+1}^{*}\to^{[1]}\cdot

to
j_{p*}j_{p}^{!}G
for a complex of sheaves
G
on
\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda})
,
we have an exact sequence

\displaystyle\dots

\displaystyle\to H^{p+q}(R\Gamma j_{p+1}^{!}G)\to H^{p+q}(R\Gamma j_{p}^{!}G)\to

\displaystyle H^{p+q}(R\Gamma i_{p+1}^{*}j_{p*}j_{p}^{!}G)\to H^{p+q+1}(R\Gamma j_{p+1}^{!}G)\to\dots.

This gives an exact couple

D^{p,q}:=H^{p+q}(R\Gamma j_{p}^{!}G)\quad E^{p,q}=H^{p+q}(R\Gamma i_{p+1}^{*}j_{p*}j_{p}^{!}G)

(see
[
Wei94
, Section 5.9]

for exact couples). We get a spectral sequence

E_{1,G}^{p,q}\cong\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\Lambda\\
\dim\lambda=p\end{subarray}}H^{p+q}(R\Gamma j_{\lambda}^{!}G)\Rightarrow H^{p+q}(R\Gamma G).

where we put
j_{\lambda}\colon N_{\lambda,\mathbb{R}}\hookrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda})
.
By Künneth formula (
[
GS23
, Lemma 4.13]

for tropical cohomology and Proposition
6.2

for tropical intersection cohomology) and easy computation for (tropical)
affine line, we have

H^{p+q}(R\Gamma j_{\lambda}^{!}G)\cong\begin{cases}\bigoplus_{s\geq 0}H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{s-p,q-s}(N_{\lambda,\mathbb{R}})&(G=\bigoplus_{s\geq 0}\mathscr{F}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda})}^{s}[-s]_{\deg})\\
H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{q-p}(O(\lambda)(\mathbb{C});R)(-p)&(G=(-\log\lvert\cdot\rvert)_{*}C_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits,T_{\Lambda}(\mathbb{C})}^{*}(-;R))\\
IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{s-p,q-s}(X\cap N_{\lambda,\mathbb{R}})&(G=i_{X*}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,X}^{s,*}[-2d]_{\deg}(s\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})\end{cases},

where
R=\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{C}
, the intersection
X\cap N_{\lambda,\mathbb{R}}
is endowed
with the natural structure of a tropical variety (see above Proposition

3.22
), and
(-p)
is
the Tate-twist of Hodge structure.

•

By retractions to
0\in N_{\lambda,\mathbb{R}}
, we have
H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{s-p,q-s}(N_{\lambda,\mathbb{R}})=0

for
q-s\geq 1
. Hence the decomposition of
E_{1,\bigoplus_{s\geq 0}\mathscr{F}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda})}^{s}[-s]_{\deg}}^{p,q}

into
E_{1,\mathscr{F}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda})}^{s}[-s]_{\deg}}^{p,q}
shows that
it degenerates at
E_{2}
-pages.

•

Moreover, the morphisms

\bigoplus_{s\geq 0}\mathscr{F}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda})}^{s}[-s]_{\deg}\to(-\log\lvert\cdot\rvert)_{*}C_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits,T_{\Lambda}(\mathbb{C})}^{*}(-;\mathbb{C})\leftarrow(-\log\lvert\cdot\rvert)_{*}C_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits,T_{\Lambda}(\mathbb{C})}^{*}(-;\mathbb{Q})

induces an isomorphism

E_{1,\bigoplus_{s\geq 0}\mathscr{F}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda})}^{s}[-s]_{\deg}}^{p,q}\cong E_{1,(-\log\lvert\cdot\rvert)_{*}C_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits,T_{\Lambda}(\mathbb{C})}^{*}(-;\mathbb{Q}))}^{p,q}.

The first assertion follows from this. Since
H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{q-p}(O(\lambda)(\mathbb{C});R)(-p)

is of pure weight
2q
,
E_{1,(-\log\lvert\cdot\rvert)_{*}C_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits,T_{\Lambda}(\mathbb{C})}^{*}(-;R))}^{p,q}

degenerates at
E_{2}
-pages, and we have

E_{2,(-\log\lvert\cdot\rvert)_{*}C_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits,T_{\Lambda}(\mathbb{C})}^{*}(-;\mathbb{Q}))}^{p,q}\cong\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}\nolimits_{2q}^{W}H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{p+q}(T_{\Lambda}(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q}).

Thus the second assertion holds.

•

By Lemma
7.6
and Poincaré-Verdier duality (Corollary

5.27
), we have

IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{s-p,q-s}(X\cap N_{\lambda,\mathbb{R}})=0

for
\max\left\{s-p,1\right\}\leq q-s
. Hence we have
E_{1,i_{X*}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,X}^{s,*}[-2d]_{\deg}}^{p,q}=0

for
2s\leq p+q
except for
p=q=s
. In particular,
IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{s,p+q-s}(X)=0

for
s\leq p+q-s-1
, and

\displaystyle E_{1,\mathscr{F}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda})}^{s}[-s]_{\deg}}^{s,s}

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Lambda,\dim\lambda=s}H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{0,0}(N_{\lambda,\mathbb{R}})

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Lambda,\dim\lambda=s}\mathbb{Q}

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Lambda,\dim\lambda=s}IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{0,0}(X\cap N_{\lambda,\mathbb{R}})

\displaystyle\cong E_{1,i_{X*}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,X}^{s,*}[-2d]_{\deg}}^{s,s}

\displaystyle\twoheadrightarrow E_{\infty,i_{X*}IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{sheaf}}\nolimits,X}^{s,*}[-2d]_{\deg}}^{s,s}\cong IH_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{s,s}(X).

Since

E_{1,\mathscr{F}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda})}^{s}[-s]_{\deg}}^{s,s}\twoheadrightarrow E_{2,\mathscr{F}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda})}^{s}[-s]_{\deg}}^{s,s}\cong H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{s,s}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda})),

the last assertion holds.

∎

Lemma 7.6
.

Let
W\subset N_{\mathbb{R}}
be a tropical fan. Then
we have

IH_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{cpt}}\nolimits}(W)=0

for
\max\left\{p,1\right\}\leq q
.

Proof.

For
\gamma\in IC_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{cpt}}\nolimits}(W)
with
\partial\gamma=0
,
let
c(\gamma)
be the mapping cone of the retraction
N_{\mathbb{R}}\to\left\{0\right\}

to
0\in N_{\mathbb{R}}=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(M,\mathbb{R})
. Since
\max\left\{p,1\right\}\leq q
,
by definition, the chain
c(\gamma)
is allowable at the
0
-dimensional
stratum
\left\{0\right\}
. It is easy to see that
c(\gamma)

is also allowable at any other strata since so is
\gamma
. Thus

\gamma=\partial c(\gamma)
is
0
in
IH_{p,q}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{cpt}}\nolimits}(W)
.
∎

Remind that
W_{2p}H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{2p}\cong\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}\nolimits_{2p}^{W}H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{2p}

is the lowest part.

Lemma 7.7
.

(
[
Tot14
, Theorem 3]
)
The cycle class map induces an isomorphism

CH^{p}(T_{\Lambda})\otimes\mathbb{Q}\cong W_{2p}H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{2p}(T_{\Lambda}(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q}).

Proof.

This is a special case of
[
Tot14
, Theorem 3]
.
In our case, we can prove the assertion using spectral sequences in
proof of Lemma
7.5
, the projective
case, and some functoriality. We omit proof.
∎

Consequently, we have a natural isomorphism

CH^{p}(T_{\Lambda})\otimes\mathbb{Q}\cong H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,p}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda});\mathbb{Q}),

which preserves ring structures by construction. We shall compare
their cap products. See Subsection
5.1
and
Subsection
6.2
for
the fundamental class

[X]\in H_{d,d}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}((\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda});\mathbb{Q})\cong\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{d,d}((\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda});\mathbb{Q}),\mathbb{Q})

and the cap product
[X]\cap-\colon H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{d,d}((\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda});\mathbb{Q})\to\mathbb{Q}
.

Lemma 7.8
.

We have a commtative
diagram

\textstyle{CH^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1})\otimes\mathbb{Q}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt\deg}
\textstyle{\mathbb{Q}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{1,1}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\mathbb{P}^{1});\mathbb{Q})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt[\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\mathbb{P}^{1})]\cap-}
\textstyle{\mathbb{Q},}

where
\deg\colon CH^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1})\otimes\mathbb{Q}\to\mathbb{Q}
is the map counting
points.

Proof.

Since the map
\deg\colon CH^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1})\otimes\mathbb{Q}\to\mathbb{Q}
is compatible
with
[\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C})]\cap-\colon H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{2}(\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q})\to\mathbb{Q}
,
we can use the latter. Then the assertion easily follows from direct
computation using long exact sequences

\textstyle{H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{1}(\mathbb{C}^{\times};\mathbb{Q})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits,\left\{0\right\}}^{2}(\mathbb{C};\mathbb{Q})\oplus H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits,\left\{\infty\right\}}^{2}(\mathbb{C}^{\times}\cup\left\{\infty\right\};\mathbb{Q})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{2}(\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C});\mathbb{Q})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{1,0}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{Q})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\left\{\infty\right\}}^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}\cup\left\{\infty\right\};\mathbb{Q})\oplus H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits,\left\{-\infty\right\}}^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}\cup\left\{-\infty\right\};\mathbb{Q})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{1,1}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(\mathbb{P}^{1});\mathbb{Q}),}

where in the first vertical morphism,
T\in H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{1,0}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{Q})

maps to the element of
H_{\mathop{\mathrm{sing}}\nolimits}^{1}(\mathbb{C}^{\times};\mathbb{Q})
given by integration
of
\frac{-1}{2\pi i}d\log T
, where
T
is the affine parameter
of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
which give a fixed toric structure of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
.
∎

Lemma 7.9
.

We have a
commtative diagram

\textstyle{\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits^{d}(T_{\Lambda})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\ \hskip 8.19447pt(\Lambda,w)}
\textstyle{\mathbb{Q}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{d,d}(\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda});\mathbb{Q})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt[X]\cap-}
\textstyle{\mathbb{Q}.}

Proof.

By projection formula, we may assume that
T_{\Lambda}
is quasi-projective.
By projective formula again, we may assume that
T_{\Lambda}
is
projective. Then by projective formula and the fact that
MW_{d}(T_{\Sigma})\cong CH^{n-d}(T_{\Sigma})

is generated by toric subvarieties, we may assume that
T_{\Lambda}

is projective and
X=\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits(T_{\Lambda})
. By projective formula again,
we may assume that
T_{\Lambda}=(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{d}
and
X=\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits((\mathbb{P}^{1})^{d})
.
Since fundamental classes are compatible with cross products (Remark

7.10
), the assertion follows
from Lemma
7.8
.
∎

Remark 7.10
.

We recall cross products
of tropical homology groups and compatiblity with fundamental classes

[
GS23
, Proposition 5.9]
.
Let
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
be tropical varieties of dimension
d_{1}

and
d_{2}
,
\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{i}\colon X_{1}\times X_{2}\to X_{i}
(
i=1,2
)
the projection. The pullback maps
\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{i}^{*}\mathscr{F}_{X_{i}}^{d_{i}}\to\mathscr{F}_{X_{1}\times X_{2}}^{d_{i}}

(
i=1,2
) and the wedge product of
\mathscr{F}_{X_{1}\times X_{2}}^{*}[-*]_{\deg}

induce a morphism

(7.1)

-\wedge-\colon\mathscr{F}_{X_{1}}^{d_{1}}[-d_{1}]_{\deg}\XBox\mathscr{F}_{X_{2}}^{d_{2}}[-d_{2}]_{\deg}\to\mathscr{F}_{X_{1}\times X_{2}}^{d_{1}+d_{2}}[-d_{1}-d_{2}]_{\deg}.

This morphism induces a morphism

(7.2)

H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{d_{1},d_{1}}(X_{1})\otimes H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{d_{2},d_{2}}(X_{2})\to H_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{d_{1}+d_{2},d_{1}+d_{2}}(X_{1}\times X_{2})

of tropical cohomology. By Verdier dual functors, we get a morphism

D(\mathscr{F}_{X_{1}\times X_{2}}^{d_{1}+d_{2}}[-d_{1}-d_{2}]_{\deg})\to D(\mathscr{F}_{X_{1}}^{d_{1}}[-d_{1}]_{\deg})\overset{L}{\XBox}D(\mathscr{F}_{X_{2}}^{d_{2}}[-d_{2}]_{\deg}),

which also induces a morphism

H_{d_{1}+d_{2},d_{1}+d_{2}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(X_{1}\times X_{2})\to H_{d_{1},d_{1}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(X_{1})\otimes H_{d_{2},d_{2}}^{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}(X_{2}).

This morphism maps
[X_{1}\times X_{2}]
to
[X_{1}]\otimes[X_{2}]

(
[
GS23
, Proposition 5.9]
).
This follows from the fact that the natural quasi-isomorphism
D_{X_{1}}\overset{L}{\XBox}D_{X_{2}}\cong D_{X_{1}\times X_{2}}

gives the usual cross products of singular homology (
[
GS23
, Lemma 4.11]
).
See
[
GS23
]

for details. (Note that our convention of the sign of fundamental
classes
[X]
(Subsection
5.1
) is different
from the one in [loc.cit.] (see also Definition
5.5
),
but the same proof works.)

We shall see that Kleiman’s splitting theorem of vector bundles (
[
Kle69
, Theorem 4.7]
)
and Bertini’s theorem give good blow-ups (Proposition
7.11
)
of a smooth projective variety
Y
over an infinite field. These
blow-ups are used in Example
7.4
.

Proposition 7.11
.

There exists a composition
Y^{\prime}\to Y

of blow-ups at smooth centers such that
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits}^{*}(Y^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}

is generated by
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits^{1}(Y^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
as a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra, where

\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits}^{*}(Y^{\prime})
is the Chow group modulo numerical equivalences.

Proposition
7.11
is a special case
of Proposition
7.13
.

Definition 7.12
.

Let
V\subset Y
be a smooth closed subscheme of dimension
r
.
A
good blow-up

Y^{\prime}\to Y
with respect to
V
is a composition

\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{Y_{m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bigcup}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{m}}
\textstyle{Y_{m-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bigcup}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{m-1}}
\textstyle{\dots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{1}}
\textstyle{Y_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bigcup}
\scriptstyle{:=}
\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{V^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{:=}
\textstyle{V_{m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V_{m-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bigcup}
\textstyle{\dots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
