\textstyle{V_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bigcup}
\scriptstyle{:=}
\textstyle{V}
\textstyle{Z_{m-1}}
\textstyle{\dots}
\textstyle{Z_{0}}

of blow-ups

\varphi_{i}\colon Y_{i}:=\mathop{\mathrm{Bl}}\nolimits_{Z_{i-1}}Y_{i-1}\to Y_{i-1}

at smooth centers
Z_{i-1}\subsetneq V_{i-1}
of dimension
\leq(r-1)

such that
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits}^{*}(Z_{i})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
is generated by pull-back
of
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits^{1}(Y_{i})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
, and
Z_{i-1}
does not contain
any irreducible component of
V_{i-1}
, where

V_{i}:=\mathop{\mathrm{Bl}}\nolimits_{Z_{i-1}}V_{i-1}\to V_{i-1}

is the strict transform of
V_{i-1}
.

Proposition 7.13
.

Let
V\subset Y

be a smooth closed subscheme of dimension
r
. Then there exists
a good blow-up
Y^{\prime}\to Y
with respect to
V
such that
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits}^{*}(V^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}

is generated by pull-back of
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits^{1}(Y^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
.

Proof.

We shall prove the assertion by induction on
r
. The case of
r=0

is trivial. We assume that
r\geq 1
and the assertion holds for smaller

r
. By blow-up formula of Chow groups (
[
Ful98
, Proposition 6.7, Example 8.3.4, Example 8.39]
),
it suffices to show that the existence of a good blow-up
Y^{\prime}\to Y

with respect to
V
such that the image of
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits}^{*}(V)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}

to
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits}^{*}(V^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
is generated by
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits^{1}(Y^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
.
Since
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits^{*}(V^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
is generated by chern classes of
vector bundles, the assertion follows from the following two Lemmas.
∎

Lemma 7.14
.

Let
V
be as
in Proposition
7.13
.
We assume that Proposition
7.13

holds for smooth closed subscheme of dimension
\leq(r-1)
. For a
vector bundle
G
on
V
, there exists a good blow-up
\varphi_{G}\colon Y_{G}^{\prime}\to Y

with respect to
V
such that the pull-back
(\varphi_{G}|_{V_{G}^{\prime}})^{*}G

to the strict transform
V^{\prime}_{G}
contains a line bundle.

Proof.

By
[
Kle69
, Theorem 4.7]
, there
exists a smooth closed subscheme
Z\subset V
of dimension
\leq(r-1)

such that
\varphi_{Z}^{*}G
contains a line bundle and
Z
does
not contain any irreducible component of
V
, where
\varphi_{Z}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Bl}}\nolimits_{Z}V\to V

is the blow-up at
Z
. By the assumption, there exists a good blow-up

\varphi_{G,0}\colon Y_{G,0}^{\prime}\to Y
with respect to
Z
(and hence
with respect to
V
) such that
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits}^{*}(Z^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}

is generated by
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits^{1}(Y_{G,0}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
, where
Z^{\prime}
is
the strict transform. We put
\varphi_{G}\colon Y_{G}^{\prime}\to Y
the
composition of
\varphi_{G,0}
and the blow-up at
Z^{\prime}
. Then
\varphi_{G}

is a good blow-up with respect to
V
. There is a natural morphism
from
V^{\prime}_{G}
to
\mathop{\mathrm{Bl}}\nolimits_{Z}V
, hence
(\varphi_{G}|_{V_{G}^{\prime}})^{*}G

contains a line bundle.
∎

Lemma 7.15
.

Let
V
be as
in Proposition
7.13
.
We assume that Proposition
7.13

holds for smooth closed subscheme of dimension
\leq(r-1)
. For a
line bundle
L
on
V
, there exists a good blow-up
\varphi_{L}\colon Y^{\prime}_{L}\to Y

with respect to
V
such that the pull-back
(\varphi_{L}|_{V_{L}^{\prime}})^{*}L

to the strict transform
V^{\prime}_{L}
is isomorphic to the restriction
of a line bundle on
Y^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We may assume that
L
is a very ample line bundle. By Bertini’s
theorem, we have
L\cong\text{$\mathcal{O}$(D)}
for some
smooth divisor
D\subset V
. By the assumption, there exists a good
blow-up
\varphi_{L,0}\colon Y_{L,0}^{\prime}\to Y
with respect to
D

(and hence with respect to
V
) such that
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Num}}\nolimits}^{*}(D^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}

is generated by
\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}\nolimits^{1}(Y_{L,0}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
, where
D^{\prime}
is
the strict transform. We put
\varphi_{L}\colon Y_{L}^{\prime}\to Y
the
composition of
\varphi_{L,0}
and the blow-up
\psi_{L}\colon Y_{L}^{\prime}\to Y_{L,0}^{\prime}

at
D^{\prime}
. Then
\varphi_{L}
is a good blow-up with respect to
V
,
and
(\varphi_{L}|_{V_{L}^{\prime}})^{*}L
is given by a sum of intersections
of
V_{L}^{\prime}
and exceptional divisors of
\varphi_{L}\colon Y^{\prime}_{L}\to Y
.
Thus
(\varphi_{L}|_{V_{L}^{\prime}})^{*}L
is isomorphic to the restriction
of a line bundle on
Y_{L}^{\prime}
.
∎

8.
Appendix. Six functors for graded sheaves

Let
k
be a commutative noetherian ring of finite global dimension
with unit,
I
a finite set, and
f\colon X\to Y
a continuous map
of paracompact locally compact Hausdorff spaces. (Paracompactness
is used only for soft sheaves, e.g.,
IC_{\mathop{\mathrm{Trop}}\nolimits}^{p,q}
.) Let

D(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}):=D(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}))

be the derived category of complexes of sheaves of
\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
-modules
(see Section
4
), and
D^{*}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

(
*=b,+,-
) its full triangulated subcategory of complexes quasi-isomorphic
to bounded (resp. bounded below, bounded above) complexes. In this
section, for readers convenience, we shall see that there exist six
functors on them, and they satisfies the usual compatibility and preserves
constructibility. Constructions and proofs directly follow from the
case of sheaves of
k
-modules or can be given in a parallel way,
see e.g.,
[
KS90
]
and
[
Bea84
]
.
We also give some computation of Verdier duality in Subsection
8.2
.

8.1.
Five functors

Remind that

\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\cong\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}^{I},\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k))\cong\mathop{\mathrm{Fct}}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}^{I},\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)).

We have three functors

\displaystyle f_{*}

\displaystyle\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\to\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

\displaystyle f^{*}

\displaystyle\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\to\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

\displaystyle f_{!}

\displaystyle\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\to\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}),

direct sums of the usual functors for sheaves of
k
-modules at each
grade
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
. We put

\displaystyle Rf_{*}

\displaystyle\colon D^{+}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\to D^{+}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

\displaystyle f^{*}

\displaystyle\colon D^{*}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\to D^{*}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

\displaystyle Rf_{!}

\displaystyle\colon D^{+}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\to D^{+}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

their derived functors. We also put
R\Gamma:=Ra_{X*}
and
R\Gamma_{c}:=Ra_{X!}
,
where
a_{X}\colon X\to\left\{\mathop{\mathrm{pt}}\nolimits\right\}
.

Lemma 8.1
.

Let
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
. The functor

\displaystyle-(a)\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

\displaystyle\to\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle\mapsto F(a)

sends injective objects to injective objects.

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle\geq a\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)

\displaystyle\to\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

be the functor given by
\geq a(A):=\bigoplus_{b\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq a}^{I}}A

(
A\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)
) and the identity map of
A
, here we use
notation similar to Subsection
4.1
.
This functor is exact, and is left adjoint of
-(a)
. Hence the assertion
holds.
∎

Remark 8.2
.

By Lemma
8.1
,
three functors
Rf_{*},f^{*},Rf_{!}
are given in a graded-wise manner,
e.g.,
(Rf_{*}E)(a)\cong Rf_{*}(E(a))
, and
Rf_{*}
and
Rf_{!}

have finite cohomological dimensions when so do

Rf_{*},Rf_{!}\colon D^{+}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)\to D^{+}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k).

Definition 8.3
.

F\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
is flat if for
x\in X
, the
stalk
F_{x}\in\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
is flat.

We have a forgetful functor
\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}\to\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
.
By abuse of notation, we donote the images of objects and morphisms
under this functor by the same symbols.

Lemma 8.4
.

Let
P\in\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}

be projective as a non-graded
k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
-module, i.e., as
an object in
\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
. Then it is also projective
in
\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
.

Proof.

Let
B\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\to}}C\to 0
be an exact sequence in
\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
,
and
\psi\colon P\to C
a morphism in
\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
.
Then there exists a morphism
\varphi\colon P\to B
in
\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}

such that
\psi=f\circ\varphi
. For each
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
, we have

\psi(a)=f(a)\circ\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{a}\circ\varphi|_{P(a)}\colon P(a)\to C(a),

where
pr_{a}\colon B\to B(a)
is the projection. We put

\phi(a):=\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\nolimits_{a}\circ\varphi|_{P(a)}\colon P(a)\to B(a).

Then it is easy to see that
\phi=(\phi(a))_{a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}}\colon P\to B

is a morphism in
\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
satisfying
\psi=f\circ\phi
.
∎

Corollary 8.5
.

Every object
B

of
\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
has a projective resolution whose length
is at most the global dimension of
B
as a non-graded
k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
-module.

Proof.

Let

0\to P_{r}\to\dots\to P_{0}\to B\to 0

be a resolution in
\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
(which is also exact
in
\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
) such that
P_{i}
(
0\leq i\leq r-1
)
is a direct sum of modules of the form
\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq b}^{I}}k

(
b\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
) (in particular, projective), and
P_{r}
is projective
in
\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
. Then by Lemma
8.4
,

P_{r}
is also projective in
\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
.
∎

Remark 8.6
.

By Corollary
8.5

and the same discussion as
[
Wei94
, Section 4.1]
,
every object
B
of
\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
also has an injective
resolution whose length is at most the global dimension of
B
as
a non-graded
k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
-module.

Remark 8.7
.

By Corollary
8.5
,
as in the case of non-graded modules, every element of
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

has a flat resolution of finite length.

Definition 8.8
.

For
F,G\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
, we put
F\otimes G\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

the sheafification of

U\mapsto F(U)\otimes_{k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}}G(U).

By Remark
8.7
, we have a derived
functor

\displaystyle-\overset{\mathrm{L}}{\otimes}-

\displaystyle\colon D^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\times D^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\to D^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}).

Definition 8.9
.

For
F,G\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
, we put
\mathop{gr\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(F,G)

the sheaf

U\mapsto\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(U,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})}(F|_{U},G|_{U}[a]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}).

We put

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{R}gr\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits

\displaystyle\colon D^{-}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})^{\mathop{\mathrm{op}}\nolimits}\times D^{+}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\to D^{+}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

its derived functor. We also put
\mathop{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits:=R\Gamma\circ\mathop{\mathrm{R}gr\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits
.

Definition 8.10
.

For a locally closed subset
h\colon W\hookrightarrow X
,
we put

h^{!}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\to\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(W,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

the direct sum of
h^{!}
for sheaves of
k
-modules at each grade,
and put

Rh^{!}\colon D^{+}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\to D^{+}(W,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

its derived functor, which is given in a grade-wise manner by Lemma

8.1
.

For
E\in D^{+}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
, a closed subset
j\colon Z\hookrightarrow X
,
and its complement
i\colon U\hookrightarrow X
, we have two distinguished
triangles

\displaystyle Ri_{!}i^{*}E\to E\to j_{*}j^{*}E\to^{[1]}\cdot

\displaystyle j_{*}j^{!}E\to E\to Ri_{*}i^{*}E\to^{[1]}\cdot.

Definition 8.11
.

Let
F\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
be a sheaf.

•

F
is
flabby
if
F(X)\to F(U)
is surjective for any open
subset
U\subset X
.

•

F
is
soft
if
F(X)\to F|_{Z}(Z)
is surjective for any
closed subset
Z\subset X
.

•

F
is
c-soft
if
F(X)\to F|_{K}(K)
is surjective for any
compact subset
K\subset X
.

•

F
is
f
-soft
if
F|_{f^{-1}(y)}
is c-soft for any
y\in Y
.

Injective sheaves are flabby, flabby sheaves are soft, soft sheaves
are c-soft, and c-soft sheaves are
f
-soft.

Remark 8.12
.

Remind that the derived functor of a left (resp. right) exact functor

F
is computable by
F
-injective (resp.
F
-projective) full
subcategories (
[
KS90
, Definition 1.8.2]
).
Here is a list of
F
-injective (resp.
F
-projective) full subcategories
similar to
[
Ach21
, Table 1.4.1]

(see
[
Bre97
, Chapter II, Section 9]
for soft
sheaves). (This follows from Lemma
8.1

and the fact that surjectivity in
\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
are computed
in the grade-wise manner.)

•

f^{*}
is exact.

•

Rf_{*}
is left exact. Injective sheaves, flabby sheaves, soft sheaves
consist
Rf_{*}
-injective subcategories, respectively.

•

Rf_{!}
is left exact. Injective sheaves, flabby sheaves, soft sheaves,
c-soft sheaves, and
f
-soft sheaves consist
Rf_{!}
-injective
subcategories, respectively.

•

\overset{\mathrm{L}}{\otimes}
is right exact in both variables. The pair
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
and the full subcategory of
flat sheaves is
\overset{\mathrm{L}}{\otimes}
-projective (
[
KS90
, Definition 1.10.6]
).

•

\mathop{\mathrm{R}gr\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits
is left exact in both variables. The pair of
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

and the full subcategory of injective sheaves is
\mathop{\mathrm{R}gr\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits
-injective.

The list of relations of these classes and functors
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)

in
[
Ach21
, Table 1.4.1]

also holds for
\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
. The relations give
various compatibility of these functors. See
[
Ach21
, Section 1.4]

and also
[
KS90
, Section 2.6]
.

Theorem 8.13
.

(projection formula
[
Ach21
, Theorem 1.4.9]
)For

F\in D^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
and
G\in D^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
,
we have
f_{!}F\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{L}}}{{\otimes}}G\cong f_{!}(F\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{L}}}{{\otimes}}f^{*}G)
.

Proof.

Similarly to the case of non-graded sheaves, this follows from graded
versions of
[
KS90
, Lemma 2.5.12]

and
[
KS90
, Proposition 2.5.13]
,
which can also be proved similarly.
∎

8.2.
Verdier duality

In this subsection, we assume that

Rf_{!}\colon D^{+}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)\to D^{+}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k).

has finite cohomological dimension (cf. Remark
8.2
).
In the same way as
[
KS90
, Section 3.1]
,
the right adjoint

f^{!}\colon D^{+}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\to D^{+}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

of
Rf_{!}
exists. This is constructed as follows, for details
see [loc.cit.]. Let
K^{*}
be a flat and f-soft resolution of

\underline{\mathbb{Z}}_{X}
of finite length. For an injective sheaf
F\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
,
we put
f_{K^{i}}^{!}(F)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
an injective
sheaf

U\mapsto\mathop{\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})}(f_{!}(\underline{k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}}_{X}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}\nolimits(K^{i}\to j_{U*}j_{U}^{*}K^{i})),F),

where
j_{U}\colon X\setminus U\hookrightarrow X
is the complement,
and for sheaves
G\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
and
K\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits\mathbb{Z})
,
we put
G\otimes K\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
the sheaf given
by

(G\otimes K)(a):=G(a)\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}}_{X}}K

and morphisms
T_{i}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}\nolimits_{K}
. For a bounded below injective
complex
F^{*}
on
Y
, we put
f^{!}F^{*}
the single complex
associated with the double complex
(f_{K^{-q}}^{!}F^{p})^{p,q}
.
Using injective resolutions, this gives the functor

f^{!}\colon D^{+}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\to D^{+}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}).

Let
a_{X}\colon X\to\left\{\mathop{\mathrm{pt}}\nolimits\right\}
. We put
\omega_{X}[T_{i}]_{i\in I}:=a_{X}^{!}k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}

the dualizing complex and

\displaystyle D=D_{X}\colon D^{-}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})^{\mathop{\mathrm{op}}\nolimits}

\displaystyle\to D^{+}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathop{\mathrm{R}gr\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(F,\omega_{X}[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

the Verdier dual.

Lemma 8.14
.

(
[
Ach21
, Lemma 1.5.15]
)
For
F,G\in D^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
, we have

f^{!}\mathop{\mathrm{R}gr\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(F,G)\cong\mathop{\mathrm{R}gr\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(f^{*}F,f^{!}G).

In particular, we have
f^{!}D\cong Df^{*}
.

Proof.

For
H\in D^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
, by projection formula

f_{!}H\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{L}}}{{\otimes}}F\cong f_{!}(H\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{L}}}{{\otimes}}f^{*}F)

and adjunction, we have

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(H,f^{!}\mathop{\mathrm{R}gr\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(F,G))\cong\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(H,\mathop{\mathrm{R}gr\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(f^{*}F,f^{!}G)).

Hence the assertion follows from Yoneda’s lemma.
∎

We shall describe
\omega_{X}[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
and
D
more explicitly.

Lemma 8.15
.

Let
A
be an injective
k
-module, and
J\subset I
a subset.
Then
\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq(-1,\dots,-1)}^{J}\times\mathbb{Z}^{I\setminus J}}A

is an injective graded
k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
-modules.

Proof.

A graded
k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
-module
B
is injective if and only
if for a finitely generated ideal
J\subset k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
in

\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
, and an element
b\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
, any morphism

J[b]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}\to B
in
\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
extends to
k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}[b]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}\to B
.
(This can be seen in the same way as non-graded rings, see e.g.,
[
Wei94
, Baer’s Criterion 2.3.1]
.)
Let

\psi\colon J[b]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}\to\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq(-1,\dots,-1)}^{J}\times\mathbb{Z}^{I\setminus J}}A

be a morphism. Let
j_{1},\dots,j_{r}\in J
be a generator of pure
grades
c_{1},\dots,c_{r}\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
. Then
\psi
is determined by

\psi(j_{l})\in A
for
l
with
c_{l}-b\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq(-1,\dots,-1)}^{J}\times\mathbb{Z}^{I\setminus J}
.
Let
c\in\mathbb{Z}^{I\setminus J}
be an element such that

c_{l}-b\leq((-1,\dots,-1),c)

for such
l
. Then

J[b]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}(((-1,\dots,-1),c))=k\langle c_{l}\rangle_{c_{l}-b\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq(-1,\dots,-1)}^{J}\times\mathbb{Z}^{I\setminus J}}.

Since
A
is an injective
k
-module,
k
-linear morphism

\psi|_{((-1,\dots,-1),c)}\colon J[b]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}(((-1,\dots,-1),c))\to A

extends to
\varphi\colon k\to A
. It induces a morphism

k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}[b]_{\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}\nolimits}\to\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq(-1,\dots,-1)}^{J}\times\mathbb{Z}^{I\setminus J}}A

in
\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
. By construction, this is an extension
of
\psi
. Thus
\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq(-1,\dots,-1)}^{J}\times\mathbb{Z}^{I\setminus J}}A

is injective.
∎

Corollary 8.16
.

Let
A^{*}
be an injective
resolution of
k
as a
k
-module. Then the total complex

\mathop{\mathrm{Tot}}\nolimits(\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}}A^{*}\to\bigoplus_{i\in I}\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq-1}^{\left\{i\right\}}\times\mathbb{Z}^{I\setminus\left\{i\right\}}}A^{*}\to\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left\{i,j\right\}\subset I\\
\#\left\{i,j\right\}=2\end{subarray}}\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq(-1,-1)}^{\left\{i,j\right\}}\times\mathbb{Z}^{I\setminus\left\{i,j\right\}}}A^{*}\to\dots\to\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq(-1,\dots,-1)}^{I}}A^{*})

is an injective resolution of
k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
as a graded
k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
-module,
where morphisms are given by
A^{p}\to A^{p+1}
and projections

\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq(-1,\dots,-1)}^{J}\times\mathbb{Z}^{I\setminus J}}A^{p}\to\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq(-1,\dots,-1)}^{J\cup\left\{i\right\}}\times\mathbb{Z}^{I\setminus(J\cup\left\{i\right\})}}A^{p}

(
i\in I\setminus J
) (up to
\{\pm 1\}
).

Let

\omega_{X}=(\omega_{X}^{j})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\in D^{b}(\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k))

be the dualizing complex with injective sheaves
\omega_{X}^{j}

(
j\in\mathbb{Z}
).

Lemma 8.17
.

We have
a quasi-isomorphism
\omega_{X}[T_{i}]_{i\in I}\cong\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I}}\omega_{X}
.

Proof.

Let
A^{*}
be an injective resolution of
k
. We may assume that

\omega_{X}=a_{X}^{!}A^{*}
. We have an injective resolution of
k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}

as in Corollary
8.16
. For a
k
-module

B
and
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, obviously, we have an exact sequence

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\to\mathop{\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I}}B,\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I}}A^{j})\to\mathop{\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I}}B,\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}}A^{j})

\displaystyle\to\mathop{\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I}}B,\bigoplus_{i\in I}\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq-1}^{\left\{i\right\}}\times\mathbb{Z}^{I\setminus\left\{i\right\}}}A^{j})\to\dots

\displaystyle\to\mathop{\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I}}B,\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq(-1,\dots,-1)}^{I}}A^{j})\to 0.

Hence the natural morphism

\varphi\colon a_{XK^{-q}}^{!}(\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I}}A^{p})\to a_{XK^{-q}}^{!}(\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}}A^{p}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in I}\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq-1}^{\left\{i\right\}}\times\mathbb{Z}^{I\setminus\left\{i\right\}}}A^{p-1}\oplus\dots\oplus\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq(-1,\dots,-1)}^{I}}A^{p-\#I})

given by the inclusion
\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I}}A^{p}\hookrightarrow\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}}A^{p}

induces a quasi-isomorphism of single complexes associated to the
double complex
(a_{XK^{-q}}^{!}(\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I}}A^{p}))^{p,q}

and

(a_{XK^{-q}}^{!}(\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}}A^{p}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in I}\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq-1}^{\left\{i\right\}}\times\mathbb{Z}^{I\setminus\left\{i\right\}}}A^{p-1}\oplus\dots\oplus\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq(-1,\dots,-1)}^{I}}A^{p-\#I}))^{p,q},

where
K^{*}
is a flat and f-soft resolution of
\underline{\mathbb{Z}}_{X}

of finite length, and
a_{XK^{-q}}^{!}(\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I}}A^{p})

is defined similarly to
a_{XK^{i}}^{!}(F)
for an injective sheaf

F\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(\mathop{\mathrm{pt}}\nolimits,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
. Thus we have a quasi-isomorphism

\omega_{X}[T_{i}]_{i\in I}\cong\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I}}\omega_{X}
.
∎

Lemma 8.18
.

Let
B^{*}\in D^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

be such that
B^{j}
(
j\in\mathbb{Z}
) is a direct sum of sheaves of the
form
\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq a_{0}}^{I}\end{subarray}}C
(
a_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
,
C\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)
). Then the quasi-isomorphism

\omega_{X}[T_{i}]_{i\in I}\cong\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I}\end{subarray}}\omega_{X}
in Lemma
8.17

induces a quasi-isomorphism

D(B^{*})\cong\mathop{gr\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(B^{*},\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I}\end{subarray}}\omega_{X}).

Proof.

Let
A^{*}
be an injective resolution of
k
and
K^{*}
a flat
and f-soft resolution of
\underline{\mathbb{Z}}_{X}
of finite length. For

j,p,q\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have exact sequences

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\to\mathop{\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(B^{j},a_{XK^{-q}}^{!}(\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I}\end{subarray}}A^{p}))\to\mathop{\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(B^{j},a_{XK^{-q}}^{!}(\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}\end{subarray}}A^{p}))

\displaystyle\to\mathop{\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(B^{j},a_{XK^{-q}}^{!}(\bigoplus_{i\in I}\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq-1}^{\left\{i\right\}}\times\mathbb{Z}^{I\setminus\left\{i\right\}}}A^{p}))\to\dots

\displaystyle\to\mathop{\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(B^{j},a_{XK^{-q}}^{!}(\bigoplus_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq(-1,\dots,-1)}^{I}}A^{p}))\to 0.

Thus the assertion follows in a similar way to Lemma
8.17
.
∎

Note that every element of
D^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
is
quasi-isomorphic to some
B^{*}
as in Lemma
8.18
.

8.3.
Constructible complexes

Six functors preserve constructible complexes (Remark
8.23
,
Theorem
8.34
, Proposition

8.35
, Theorem
8.36
,
Corollary
8.41
). This can be seen
in the same way as the case of sheaves of
k
-modules in
[
Bea84
, Chapter V]
.
Since results we need spread apart in
[
Bea84
, Chapter V]
,
for readers’ convenience, we survey proof.

Remind that
f\colon X\to Y
is a continuous map of paracompact locally
compact Hausdorff spaces. In this subsection, we assume that

Rf_{*},Rf_{!}\colon D^{+}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)\to D^{+}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}\nolimits k)

have finite cohomological dimensions. Then by Remark
8.2
,

Rf_{*},Rf_{!}\colon D^{+}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\to D^{+}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

also have finite cohomological dimensions. (Note that by Remark
8.7
,

\overset{\mathrm{L}}{\otimes}
has finite homological dimension.)

Definition 8.19
.

(
[
Bea84
, Chapter V. 2.1. Remark]
)
An unrestricted topological stratification
(X_{j})_{j}
of
X

is a filtration

\emptyset=X_{-1}\subset X_{0}\subset X_{1}\subset\dots\subset X_{\dim X-1}\subset X_{\dim X}=X

by closed subspaces such that each stratum
X_{j}\setminus X_{j-1}

is a topological manifold of dimension
j
, and for each point
x\in X_{j}\setminus X_{j-1}
,
there is a neighborhood
U
of
x
and an homeomorphism of
U

to
B_{j}\times\overset{\circ}{c}(L)
preserving stratifications,
where
B_{j}
is the
j
-dimensional open disk ,
L
is a
(\dim X-j-1)
-dimensional
compact unrestricted topological stratified space, and
\overset{\circ}{c}(L):=[0,1)\times L/(\left\{0\right\}\times L\backsim\mathop{\mathrm{pt}}\nolimits)

is the open cone over
L
with the natural stratification.

In this subsection, we fix unrestricted topological stratifications

(X_{j})_{j}
and
(Y_{j})_{j}
of
X
and
Y
.

Definition 8.20
.

(
[
Bea84
, Chapter V. 10.12]
)
A continuous map
f\colon X\to Y
is
stratified
if

•

for a connected component
S
of a stratum
Y_{j}\setminus Y_{j-1}

of
Y
, the inverse image
f^{-1}(S)
is a union of connected components
of strata of
X
, and

•

for
y\in Y
, there exist a neighborhood
U
of
y
in the stratum
of
Y
containing
y
, an unrestricted topological stratified space

F
, and a stratification preserving homeomorphism
F\times U\cong f^{-1}(U)

which transform the projection to
U
into
f
.

In this subsection, we assume that
f\colon X\to Y
is stratified
with respect to
(X_{j})_{j}
and
(Y_{j})_{j}
.

Definition 8.21
.

Let
F\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}\nolimits(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
.

•

F
is
locally constant
if there is an open covering of
X

such that the restriction of
F
to each open subset is a constant
sheaf.

•

F
is
of finite type
if each stalk
F_{x}
(
x\in X
) is
a finitely generated graded
k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
-module.

•

F
is
weakly constructible
with respect to a stratification

(X_{j})_{j}
if
F|_{X_{j}\setminus X_{j-1}}
is locally constant.

•

F
is
constructible
with respect to a stratification
(X_{j})_{j}

if
F|_{X_{j}\setminus X_{j-1}}
is locally constant of finite type.

Definition 8.22
.

E\in D^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

is
(weakly) constructible
with respect to a stratification

(X_{j})_{j}
if
\mathscr{H}^{k}(E)
(
k\in\mathbb{Z}
) is (weakly) constructible
with respect to
(X_{j})_{j}
. We put

D_{(w-)c,(X_{j})_{j}}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\subset D^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

the full triangulated subcategories of (weakly) constructible complexes
with respect to
(X_{j})_{j}
.

Remind that
f^{*},Rf_{*},Rf_{!},R\Gamma,R\Gamma_{c}
(and
f^{!}

when
f
is a locally closed embedding) are given as direct sums
of these functors at each grade (Remark
8.2
).
In particular, we can apply some results on such functors for sheaves
of
k
-modules to sheaves of graded
k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
-modules,
e.g., Remark
8.23
, Lemma
8.24
,
Proposition
8.25
, and Lemma

8.26
.

Remark 8.23
.

Obviously, for
G\in D_{c,(Y_{j})_{j}}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
,
we have
f^{*}G\in D_{c,(X_{j})_{j}}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

(
[
Bea84
, Chapter V. Theorem 10.16 (i)]
).

Lemma 8.24
.

(
[
Bea84
, Chapter V. Theorem 10.16 (ii)]
)
For
F\in D_{w-c,(X_{j})_{j}}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
, we
have
Rf_{*}F,Rf_{!}F\in D_{w-c,(Y_{j})_{j}}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
.

Proposition 8.25
.

(
[
Bea84
, Chapter V. Proposition 3.7 (a)]
)
Assume
X
is a manifold and
(X_{j})_{j}
is the trivial stratification.
Then for
x\in X
and
F\in D_{w-c,(X_{j})_{j}}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
,
we have

H^{r}(R\Gamma_{c}F|_{U})\cong H^{r-\dim X}(F_{x})

for an open neighborhood
U
of
x
homeomorphic to an open ball.

Lemma 8.26
.

(
[
Bea84
, Chapter V. Lemma 3.8 (b)]
)
Let
Z
be an open ball of dimension
s
. We assume that
X
is
compact. We equip
W:=X\times Z
with a filtration
(W_{j}:=X_{j}\times Z)_{j}
.
Let
\pi\colon W\to Z
be the projection, and
F\in D_{w-c,\left\{W_{j}\right\}_{j}}^{b}(W,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
.
Then for
z\in Z
, we have

\displaystyle H^{r}(R\Gamma_{c}F)

\displaystyle\cong H^{r-s}(R\Gamma_{c}F|_{\pi^{-1}(z)})

\displaystyle H^{r}(R\Gamma F)

\displaystyle\cong H^{r}(R\Gamma F|_{\pi^{-1}(z)}).

Proposition 8.27
.

Let
F\in D_{c,(X_{j})_{j}}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
.

(1)

(
[
Bea84
, Chapter V. Proposition 3.10 a).b).e)]
)
Let
x\in X_{j}\setminus X_{j-1}
, and
U
a neighborhood of
x

homeomorphic to
B_{j}\times\overset{\circ}{c}(L)
as in Definition

8.19
. Then
H^{r}(R\Gamma_{c}F|_{U})

does not depend on
U
and finitely generated over
k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
.
Moreover, we have
H^{r}(R\Gamma_{c}F|_{U})\cong H^{r}(j_{x}^{!}F)
,
where
j_{x}\colon\left\{x\right\}\to X
is the inclusion

(2)

(
[
Bea84
, Chapter V. Theorem 3.5]
)
Let
P\subset Q\subset X
be open subsets such that
\overline{P}\subset Q

and
\overline{P}
is compact. Then

\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits(H^{r}(R\Gamma_{c}F|_{P})\to H^{r}(R\Gamma_{c}F|_{Q}))

is finitely generated over
k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
.

When
X
is compact, we can take
P=Q=X
, and hence
H^{r}(R\Gamma_{c}F)

is finitely generated.

Proof.

We shall prove both assertions by induction on
\dim X
. When
\dim X=0
,
the assertions are trivial. We assume
\dim X\geq 1
.

(1) There exists an exact sequence

\dots\to H^{r}(R\Gamma_{c}F|_{U\setminus B_{j}})\to H^{r}(R\Gamma_{c}F|_{U})\to H^{r}(R\Gamma_{c}F|_{B_{j}})\to\dots.

By Proposition
8.25
, the
cohomology group
H^{r}(R\Gamma_{c}F|_{B_{j}})
does not depend on

U
and finitely generated. By Lemma
8.26
, we have

H^{r}(R\Gamma_{c}F|_{U\setminus B_{j}})\cong H^{r-j}(R\Gamma F|_{L}).

The right-hand side does not depend on
U
and, by hypothesis of
induction of (2), finitely generated. Hence the first assertion holds.
The last assertion immediately follows from the case of non-graded
sheaves
[
Bea84
, Chapter V. Proposition 3.10 c)]
.

(2) This follows in the same way as
[
Bea84
, Chapter V Theorem 3.5]

by diagram chasing and hypothesis of induction. We omit details.
∎

Remark 8.28
.

Note that we assumed that
k
is noether, and in proof of both assertions
of Proposition
8.27
, we used noetherian
property of
k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
, i.e., for a finitely generated graded

k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
-module
A
, its graded
k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
-submodules
are also finitely generated. (This obviously follows from the case
of non-graded
k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
-modules.)

Corollary 8.29
.

(
[
Bea84
, Chapter V Corollary 3.11 (iii)]
)
Let
h\colon V\hookrightarrow X
be an open subset which is a union
of connected components of strata
X_{j}\setminus X_{j-1}
(
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
).
Let
F\in D_{c,(V\cap X_{j})_{j}}^{b}(V,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
.
Then
Rh_{*}F\in D_{c,(X_{j})_{j}}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
.

Proof.

Rh_{*}F
is weakly constructible by Lemma
8.24
.
To see constructibility at
x\in X_{j}\setminus X_{j-1}
, by induction
on
j
, we may assume that
V\cap U\cong B_{j}\times\overset{\circ}{c}(L)\setminus B_{j}

for a neighborhood
U\cong B_{j}\times\overset{\circ}{c}(L)
of
x

as in Definition
8.19
.
Then by Lemma
8.26
, we have

\displaystyle H^{r}(R\Gamma Rh_{*}F|_{U})

\displaystyle\cong H^{r}(R\Gamma F|_{B_{j}\times\overset{\circ}{c}(L)\setminus B_{j}})

\displaystyle\cong H^{r}(R\Gamma F|_{L}),

which is finitely generated by Proposition
8.27

(2).
∎

Definition 8.30
.

An unrestricted topological stratified
space
X
with
(X_{j})_{j}
is
compactifiable
if there exist
a compact topological space
\overline{X}
containing
X
as an
open dense subset, and an unrestricted topological stratification

(\overline{X}_{j})_{j}
of
\overline{X}
such that
X
is a union
of connected components of strata
\overline{X}_{j}\setminus\overline{X}_{j-1}

(
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
) and the inducing stratification
(X\cap\overline{X}_{j})_{j}

on
X
is finer than
(X_{j})_{j}
.

Corollary 8.31
.

(
[
Bea84
, Chapter V. Lemma 10.13]
)
We assume that
X
is compactifiable. Then for
F\in D_{c,(X_{j})_{j}}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
,
the cohomology groups
H^{r}(R\Gamma_{c}F)
and
H^{r}(R\Gamma F)

are finitely generated.

Proof.

This follows from Proposition
8.27
(2) and
Corollary
8.29
.
∎

Proposition 8.32
.

Let
F\in D_{c,(X_{j})_{j}}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
.
Then
h_{j}^{!}F\in D^{b}(X_{j}\setminus X_{j-1},\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

is constructible with respect to the trivial stratification, where

h_{j}\colon X_{j}\setminus X_{j-1}\hookrightarrow X
is the embedding.

Proof.

By
[
Bea84
, Proposition 3.10 d)]
,
h_{j}^{!}F

is weakly constructible. For
x\in X_{j}\setminus X_{j-1}
, we put

h_{x}\colon\left\{x\right\}\hookrightarrow X_{j}\setminus X_{j-1}
.
By
[
Bea84
, Chapter V. Proposition 3.7 b) and Proposition 3.10 c)]
,
we have
H^{s}(h_{x}^{*}h_{j}^{!}F)\cong H^{s-j}((h_{j}\circ h_{x})^{!}F)
.
By Proposition
8.27
(1), it is finitely
generated.
∎

Lemma 8.33
.

(
[
Bea84
, Lemma 10.15]
)
We assume that
f\colon X\to Y
is a locally closed embedding. Then
for
F\in D_{c,(Y_{j})_{j}}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
, we have

f^{!}F\in D_{c,(X_{j})_{j}}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
.

Proof.

We may assume that
f
is a closed embedding. We shall show that

f^{!}F|_{X\setminus X_{l}}
is constructible with respect to
(X_{j}\setminus X_{l})_{j}

by induction on
l\leq\dim X-1
. When
l=\dim X-1
, the subset
X\setminus X_{\dim X-1}\subset Y

is a union of connected components of
Y_{\dim X}\setminus Y_{\dim X-1}
.
Hence the assertion of induction follows from Proposition
8.32
.
We assume that
l\leq\dim X-2
. We put
i_{l}\colon X\setminus X_{l+1}\hookrightarrow X\setminus X_{l}

an open embedding and
j_{l}\colon X_{l+1}\setminus X_{l}\hookrightarrow X\setminus X_{l}

the complement. By the hypothesis of induction, a distinguised triangle
given by applying
j_{l*}j_{l}^{!}\to\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}\nolimits\to Ri_{l*}i_{l}^{*}\to^{[1]}\cdot

to
(f^{!}F)|_{X\setminus X_{l}}
, and Corollary
8.29
,
it suffices to show that
j_{l}^{!}((f^{!}F)|_{X\setminus X_{l}})

is constructible with respect to the trivial stratification on
X_{l+1}\setminus X_{l}
.
This follows from Proposition
8.32
.
∎

Theorem 8.34
.

(
[
Bea84
, Chapter V. Theorem 10.16 (iv) (v)]
)
We assume that
each fiber
f^{-1}(y)
with a stratification
(f^{-1}(y)\cap X_{j})_{j}

(
y\in Y
) is compactifiable. Then for
F\in D_{c,(X_{j})_{j}}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
,
we have
Rf_{*}F,Rf_{!}F\in D_{c,(Y_{j})_{j}}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
.

Proof.

By Lemma
8.24
,

Rf_{*}F
and
Rf_{!}F
are weakly constructible. Since
H^{r}(Rf_{!}F_{y})\cong H^{r}(R\Gamma_{c}F|_{f^{-1}(y)})

(
[
Bea84
, Chapter V. Lemma 7.12]
),
by Corollary
8.31
,
H^{r}(Rf_{!}F_{y})
is finitely
generated, i.e.,
Rf_{!}F
is constructible. We shall show that
Rf_{*}F|_{Y\setminus Y_{l}}

is constructible with respect to a stratification
(Y_{j}\setminus Y_{l})_{j}

by induction on
l\leq\dim Y-1
. When
l=\dim Y-1
, since
Y\setminus Y_{\dim Y-1}

is a topological manifold, by
[
Bea84
, Chapter V. Lemma 10.14 (ii)(2)]
,

for
y\in Y\setminus Y_{\dim Y-1}
, we have
H^{r}(Rf_{*}F_{y})\cong H^{r}(R\Gamma F|_{f^{-1}(y)}).

By Corollary
8.31
, it is finitely generated. We
assume that
l\leq\dim Y-2
. We put
i_{l}\colon Y\setminus Y_{l+1}\hookrightarrow Y\setminus Y_{l}

an open embedding and
j_{l}\colon Y_{l+1}\setminus Y_{l}\hookrightarrow Y\setminus Y_{l}

the complement. By the hypothesis of induction, a distinguished triangle
given by applying
j_{l*}j_{l}^{!}\to\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}\nolimits\to Ri_{l*}i_{l}^{*}\to^{[1]}\cdot

to
(Rf_{*}F)|_{Y\setminus Y_{l}}
, and Corollary
8.29
,
it suffices to show that
j_{l}^{!}((Rf_{*}F)|_{Y\setminus Y_{l}})

is constructible with respect to the trivial stratification on
Y_{l+1}\setminus Y_{l}
.
By
[
Ach21
, Proposition 1.5.7 (2)]
,
we have
j_{l}^{!}Rf_{*}F|_{Y\setminus Y_{l}}\cong Rf|_{f^{-1}(Y_{l+1}\setminus Y_{l})*}\varphi_{l}^{!}F
,
where we put
\varphi_{l}\colon f^{-1}(Y_{l+1}\setminus Y_{l})\hookrightarrow X
.
By Lemma
8.33
,
\varphi_{l}^{!}F
is constructible.
Since
Y_{l+1}\setminus Y_{l}
is a manifold, by the case of
l=\dim Y-1
,
the complex
Rf|_{f^{-1}(Y_{l+1}\setminus Y_{l})*}\varphi_{l}^{!}F

is constructible. Thus we have proved the assertion.
∎

Proposition 8.35
.

(
[
Bea84
, Chapter V. Proposition 10.18]
)
For
F,G\in D_{c,(X_{j})_{j}}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
,
the tensor product
F\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{L}}}{{\otimes}}G
is also constructible
with respect to
(X_{j})_{j}
.

Proof.

This follows in the same way as
[
Bea84
, Chapter V. Proposition 10.18]
.
We omit details.
∎

Theorem 8.36
.

(
[
Bea84
, Chapter V. Theorem 8.6]
)
For
F,G\in D_{c,(X_{j})_{j}}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
,
the complex
\mathop{\mathrm{R}gr\mathscr{H}om}\nolimits(F,G)
is also constructible with respect to

(X_{j})_{j}
.

Proof.

This follows in the same way as
[
Bea84
, Chapter V. Theorem 8.6]
.
We omit details.
∎

Lemma 8.37
.

(
[
Ach21
, Lemma 2.8.1]
)
We have
\omega_{X}[T_{i}]_{i\in I}\in D_{c,(X_{j})_{j}}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

and

D\colon D_{c,(X_{j})_{j}}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\to D_{c,(X_{j})_{j}}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}).

Proof.

The first assertion follows from Lemma
8.17

and constructibility of
\omega_{X}
(e.g.,
[
Ach21
, Lemma 2.8.1]
).
The second assertion follows from the first one and Theorem
8.36
.
∎

Lemma 8.38
.

(
[
Bea84
, Chapter V Proposition 3.10 a) ]
)
Let
x\in X_{j}\setminus X_{j-1}
, and
U
a neighborhood of
x

homeomorphic to
B_{j}\times\overset{\circ}{c}(L)
as in Definition

8.19
. Then for
F\in D_{c,(X_{j})_{j}}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
,
we have
H^{r}(R\Gamma F|_{U})\cong H^{r}(j_{x}^{*}F)
, where
j_{x}\colon\left\{x\right\}\to X

is the inclusion.

Proof.

By
[
Bea84
, Chapter V Proposition 3.10 a) ]
,
the cohomology group
H^{r}(R\Gamma F|_{U})
does not depend on
U
.
Hence the assertion holds.
∎

Proposition 8.39
.

(
[
KS90
, Proposition 3.4.3 (iii)]
)
For
F\in D_{c,(X_{j})_{j}}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
, we have

H^{r}(j_{x}^{*}DF)\cong H^{r}(Dj_{x}^{!}F)
.

Proof.

By Lemma
8.37
,
DF
is constructible. Hence
by Lemma
8.27
(1) and Lemma
8.38
,
we have

\displaystyle H^{r}(j_{x}^{*}DF)

\displaystyle\cong H^{r}(R\Gamma DF|_{U})

\displaystyle\cong H^{r}(\mathop{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(R\Gamma_{c}F|_{U},k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}))

\displaystyle\cong H^{r}(Dj_{x}^{!}F),

where
U
is a neighborhood of
x
homeomorphic to
B_{j}\times\overset{\circ}{c}(L)
.
∎

Proposition 8.40
.

(
[
KS90
, Proposition 3.4.3 (ii)]
)For

F\in D_{c,(X_{j})_{j}}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})
, we have

F\cong DDF
.

Proof.

By Proposition
8.39
and Lemma

8.14
, we have

\displaystyle(DDF)_{x}

\displaystyle\cong D(j_{x}^{!}DF)

\displaystyle\cong\mathop{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(j_{x}^{!}DF,k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

\displaystyle\cong\mathop{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(Dj_{x}^{*}F,k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

\displaystyle\cong\mathop{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(\mathop{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{grHom}}\nolimits(F,k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}),k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})

\displaystyle\cong F_{x},

where the last quasi-isomorphism follows in the same way as
[
Ach21
, Theorem A.10.2]
.
∎

Corollary 8.41
.

f^{!}\colon D_{c,(Y_{j})_{j}}^{b}(Y,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I})\to D_{c,(X_{j})_{j}}^{b}(X,\mathop{\mathrm{grMod}}\nolimits k[T_{i}]_{i\in I}).

Proof.

f^{!}\cong f^{!}DD\cong Df^{*}D
.
∎

Since
f^{!}\omega_{Y}[T_{i}]_{i\in I}\cong\omega_{X}[T_{i}]_{i\in I}
,
we have
Rf_{*}D\cong DRf_{!}
.

Corollary 8.42
.

We have
DRf_{*}\cong Rf_{!}D
and
f^{*}D\cong Df^{!}
.
